================================================================================
FINDINGS (14 total: 4 CRITICAL, 5 HIGH, 3 MEDIUM, 2 LOW)
================================================================================

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-V2 [CRITICAL] — ZusdVault.redeem() drains protocol; phantom collateral
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZusdVault.sol  lines 328–346
Severity: CRITICAL — direct loss of all protocol collateral

Body:
  redeem(zusdAmount) burns the redeemer's ZUSD and transfers ZBX out of the
  vault to them, but does NOT decrement ANY user's CDP `cdp.collateral` or
  `cdp.debt`. It only updates the global `totalDebt` counter. After enough
  redemptions:
    - Vault's actual ZBX balance has been drained.
    - Each CDP still records its original `cdp.collateral` (phantom).
    - The first CDP owner to call `closeCDP()` or `withdrawCollateral()`
      finds the vault under-collateralised; subsequent attempts revert.
  Compound this with the broken stability fee (S6-V3) and any holder of
  ZUSD can drain the vault at oracle price minus 0.5%. Critical liveness
  + insolvency vector.

Reproduction:
  1. User A: openCDP(1000 ZBX, 500 ZUSD) — vault holds 1000 ZBX.
  2. User A transfers 100 ZUSD to attacker.
  3. Attacker calls redeem(100) — receives ~100/zbxPrice ZBX, vault balance
     drops, User A's CDP unchanged.
  4. User A: closeCDP() reverts on last-mile transfer due to insufficient
     vault balance. User A's collateral is permanently stuck below the
     accounting amount.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-V3 [CRITICAL] — STABILITY_FEE_PER_SEC ~9 orders of magnitude too small
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZusdVault.sol  line 47
Severity: CRITICAL — entire stability-fee mechanism inactive

Body:
  Constant defined as 634_195_839 ray/sec (claimed = 2 % APY). True ray/sec
  rate for 2 % APY compounded continuously:
      r = ln(1.02) / 31_536_000  ≈  6.2814e-10 per sec
      r in ray  =  6.2814e17

  Coded value 6.34e8 ÷ 1e27 = 6.34e-19 per sec. Annualised:
      6.34e-19 × 31_536_000 ≈ 2e-11  (i.e. 2e-9 %)
  Effective stability fee is zero. Borrowers carry interest-free debt
  forever; protocol cannot defend the ZUSD peg via fee accrual.

  Correct ray/sec value (MakerDAO-derived):
      627_937_192_491_029_810

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-EP1 [CRITICAL] — Paymaster postOp revert crashes entire bundler batch
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZbxEntryPoint.sol  lines 144–153
Severity: CRITICAL — protocol-wide DoS by single malicious paymaster

Body:
  ```
  if (paymaster != address(0)) {
      ...
      (bool pmOk, ) = paymaster.call(abi.encodeWithSignature(...));
      require(pmOk, "EntryPoint: paymaster postOp failed");
  }
  ```
  A reverting postOp cascades up through `_handleOp` → `handleOps` and
  reverts the WHOLE batch. A malicious paymaster deployed by an attacker
  can grief any bundler that includes its UserOps alongside legitimate
  ones — every op in the batch fails, bundler loses gas, victim ops are
  not processed. Per ERC-4337 v0.6 spec, postOp failure must be tolerated
  (paymaster eats the loss; op continues). Comment at lines 142–143
  documents the intent backwards.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-BM1 [CRITICAL/Defense-in-Depth] — BridgeMultisig digest omits source-chain
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/BridgeMultisig.sol  lines 213–214
Severity: CRITICAL if mis-deployed; MEDIUM with strict deployment discipline

Body:
  ```
  bytes32 inner = keccak256(
      abi.encode(_DOMAIN_TAG, block.chainid, vault, zebvixSeq, to, amount)
  );
  ```
  block.chainid is the BSC chain id; vault is the BSC vault address. The
  SOURCE chain (Zebvix mainnet vs Zebvix testnet) is NOT bound. If two
  Zebvix networks share a relayer set and the same BSC bridge contract is
  reused (a deployment mistake, but plausible during launch), a relayer
  signature collected for `(zebvixSeq=42, to=X, amount=Y)` on testnet is
  byte-identical to the digest required for mainnet — instant replay.

  Mitigation in the contract: bind an immutable `sourceChainId` (Zebvix
  L1 chain id, 8989) into the digest at construction time. Defense in
  depth — the deployment discipline alone is insufficient for production.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-EP2 [HIGH] — Paymaster `validUntil` deadline silently discarded
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZbxEntryPoint.sol  line 213
Severity: HIGH — validation deadline ignored, post-expiry replay possible

Body:
  ```
  (pmContext, ) = abi.decode(pmRet, (bytes, uint256));
  ```
  ERC-4337 paymasters return `(bytes context, uint256 validUntil)` from
  `validatePaymasterUserOp`. The EntryPoint silently discards the second
  return value — operations remain executable forever even if the paymaster
  intended a deadline. Combined with off-chain bundler replay, this allows
  stale ops to be executed after pricing/state assumptions have shifted.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-EP3 [HIGH] — handleOps lacks reentrancy guard
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZbxEntryPoint.sol  lines 92–102
Severity: HIGH — beneficiary callback can re-enter handleOps

Body:
  At line 99, `beneficiary.call{value: totalGasCollected}("")` invokes
  arbitrary code at the (caller-provided) beneficiary. A malicious
  beneficiary contract can call back into `handleOps` with attacker ops
  before the outer call returns. Each inner op uses its own payer balance
  so direct fund theft is bounded, but cross-batch nonce / accounting
  invariants can be violated, and re-entry compounds gas-billing edge
  cases. Industry-standard mitigation: nonReentrant on handleOps.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-ST1 [HIGH] — ZbxStaking has no MAX_REWARD_RATE cap
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZbxStaking.sol  lines 277–280
Severity: HIGH — single founder-key compromise drains entire reward pool

Body:
  setRewardRate(uint256 newRate) accepts any uint256 from `onlyFounder`.
  A stolen founder key can set rate = type(uint256).max; `updatePool`'s
  `elapsed * rewardRate` then dominates `accRewardPerShare`, claim() can
  drain the entire `rewardToken` balance to the attacker's wallet (modulo
  the `recoverExcessRewards` reserve check which only protects against
  founder-direct withdraw, NOT against legitimate user `claim()`).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-ST2 [HIGH] — ZbxStaking does not use Governable / 2-step ownership
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZbxStaking.sol  lines 287–291, 282–285, 277–280
Severity: HIGH — single-step founder transfer; no timelock route

Body:
  `transferFounder(newFounder)` is single-step (line 287). A typo bricks
  the contract permanently — rate updates and emergency pause become
  unreachable. Sister contracts (BridgeVault, Governable lib) all use the
  2-step `transfer/accept` pattern; staking is the lone outlier. There is
  also no `governor` / `onlyAdmin` indirection — high-risk knobs cannot be
  routed through a future timelock.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-A2 [HIGH] — ZbxLendingPool has no per-reserve supply / borrow caps
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZbxLendingPool.sol  ReserveData struct + addReserve
Severity: HIGH — single-asset blowup risk

Body:
  ReserveData lacks supplyCap / borrowCap fields. An attacker (or an honest
  whale) can flood a low-liquidity reserve, distort the utilization-driven
  rate curve, and either (a) drain healthy reserves via cascading flash-loan
  liquidations or (b) brick the rate model. Aave v3 enforces these per
  asset; ZbxLendingPool does not. Add caps + enforce at supply() entry and
  borrow() entry.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-EP4 [MEDIUM] — gasUsed billed at full verificationGasLimit
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZbxEntryPoint.sol  line 126
Severity: MEDIUM — payer over-billed for unused validation gas

Body:
  `gasUsed = gasBefore - gasleft() + op.preVerificationGas + op.verificationGasLimit`
  adds the FULL verificationGasLimit (the budget cap), not the actually-spent
  validation gas. Over-bills the payer when validation was cheap. Per
  ERC-4337 v0.6 this is spec-compliant (measuring actual validation gas is
  hard within Solidity), but worth surfacing as a known overcharge.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-AMM2 [MEDIUM] — ZbxAMM lacks flash-callback hook (Uni V2 parity)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZbxAMM.sol  swap()
Severity: MEDIUM — feature parity gap, not a vulnerability per se

Body:
  Uni V2 `swap()` invokes `IUniswapV2Callee.uniswapV2Call(sender, a0, a1, data)`
  AFTER transferring outputs and BEFORE the K-invariant check, enabling flash
  swaps. ZbxAMM omits this hook. Existing routers/integrations expecting
  Uni V2 semantics will silently lose flash-swap functionality. Either
  document divergence or add the hook.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-BV1 [MEDIUM] — BridgeVault MINT_WINDOW is tumbling, not rolling
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/BridgeVault.sol  lines 64–66, 261–272
Severity: MEDIUM — comment misrepresents implementation; doubled burst risk

Body:
  Comment states "rolling 24h cap"; the implementation is a tumbling 24h
  bucket — when `block.timestamp >= mintWindowStart + MINT_WINDOW`, the
  full cap resets. Burst attack: mint full cap at T = window_end - 1s, mint
  full cap again at T = window_end + 1s → 2× cap in 2 seconds. True rolling
  enforcement requires a per-mint timestamp queue (more complex). Either
  fix the comment to "tumbling" and document the burst-window behaviour,
  or upgrade to true rolling enforcement.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-V5 [LOW] — Chain-ID comment mismatch (7878 vs 8989)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZusdVault.sol line 21, contracts/ZbxOracle.sol line 18
Severity: LOW — documentation inconsistency, no runtime impact

Body:
  Both contracts annotate `@custom:zbx-chain Chain ID 7878`. The active
  Zebvix L1 chain id is 8989 per project configuration. Block.chainid is
  set by the runtime, so this is comment-only — but documentation drift
  causes auditor confusion and operational mistakes during deployment.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-AMM1 [LOW] — swap() does not check `to != address(0)`
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
File:     contracts/ZbxAMM.sol  swap()
Severity: LOW — defensive hardening

Body:
  swap() rejects `to == token0` and `to == token1` but not `to == address(0)`.
  The downstream `_safeTransfer` will revert on most ERC-20s when destination
  is zero, but explicit early-revert improves diagnostics and gas.

================================================================================
FALSE POSITIVES IDENTIFIED & RESOLVED DURING REVIEW
================================================================================

S6-A1-FP — "Oracle staleness check missing in lending"
  Initial reading flagged ZbxLendingPool's `IZbxOracle.getPrice` interface
  declaration (returns uint256) and assumed no staleness was enforced. On
  inspection of ZbxOracle.sol lines 99 and 107, `getPrice()` itself reverts
  with "Oracle: stale price" when `block.timestamp - p.updatedAt > MAX_STALENESS`
  (1 hour). Caller decoder reads only the first uint256 from the (int256,
  uint256) return — valid Solidity ABI behaviour (extra returndata ignored)
  — and never reaches the use-site if the oracle is stale. Staleness is
  enforced. Not a finding.

S6-V1-FP — "Oracle staleness check missing in vault"
  Same root analysis — ZusdVault's IOracle_Price interface decodes only
  the price word, but the underlying ZbxOracle.getPrice() reverts on stale
  data before returning. Not a finding.

S6-V2 [CRITICAL] — ZusdVault.redeem() vault drain
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STATUS: FIXED (defensive disable; P1 redesign tracked).
ACTION: redeem() now reverts unconditionally with the message
        "Vault: redemption disabled (S6-V2 P1 redesign in progress)".
        Previous broken implementation removed. The Liquity-style
        sorted-by-CR redemption (which both burns redeemer ZUSD AND
        decrements lowest-CR CDPs' debt+collateral atomically) is the
        only correct fix — substantial work tracked as P1 follow-up.
        Disabled-and-honest is strictly safer than enabled-and-broken
        for a production stablecoin.
FILE:   contracts/ZusdVault.sol  redeem()  (~lines 333–358)
RISK:   None during disabled state. Re-enable requires P1 redesign,
        a fresh round of audit, and a deployment of the new vault.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-V3 [CRITICAL] — STABILITY_FEE_PER_SEC constant ~9 orders too small
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STATUS: FIXED.
ACTION: STABILITY_FEE_PER_SEC updated 634_195_839 → 627_937_192_491_029_810
        (canonical MakerDAO ray/sec value for 2 % APY). Annualised effective
        rate now matches the documented 2 %.
FILE:   contracts/ZusdVault.sol  line 56
RISK:   Existing CDPs from the broken-fee era will have incorrectly low
        debt baselines. On chain restart at the new fee constant, any open
        CDP will start accruing ~2 %/yr from `lastFeeUpdate`. No retroactive
        debt adjustment — borrowers benefit from the historical leak.
        Documented; no migration script required for testnet.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-EP1 [CRITICAL] — Paymaster postOp revert crashes batch + gas-grief
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STATUS: FIXED (initial + residual hardening from architect review).
ACTION: Two-part fix.
        (a) `require(pmOk, ...)` replaced with `if (!pmOk) emit
            PostOpReverted(...)`. The op's gas billing has already been
            settled before postOp is invoked, so a reverting paymaster
            only loses its own bookkeeping opportunity — the protocol
            no longer crashes the bundler's batch. New event
            `PostOpReverted(opHash, paymaster, actualGasCost)` lets
            bundlers blacklist abusive paymasters off-chain.
        (b) (Architect-review follow-up, 2026-05-01) The initial fix
            still left a residual gas-grief vector: `paymaster.call(...)`
            without a gas cap, so a malicious paymaster could burn the
            bundler's entire remaining gas budget inside postOp,
            triggering OOG and reverting the rest of the batch.
            Bounded the call to `op.verificationGasLimit` —
            `paymaster.call{gas: op.verificationGasLimit}(...)`. The
            `if (!pmOk)` branch absorbs OOG (low-level `.call` returns
            `(false, "")` on out-of-gas), so the batch survives even an
            adversarial gas-burn paymaster. Bundlers should reject ops
            with unreasonably high verificationGasLimit at simulation
            time as standard hygiene.
FILE:   contracts/ZbxEntryPoint.sol  _handleOp() lines ~167–206
RISK:   Paymasters can no longer use postOp revert as a soft-cancel signal.
        That was never a documented contract feature; ERC-4337 v0.6 does
        not expose it. Off-chain bundlers should monitor PostOpReverted
        events and quarantine paymasters that emit it repeatedly.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-BM1 [CRITICAL/Defense-in-Depth] — BridgeMultisig digest source-chain binding
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STATUS: FIXED.
ACTION: New immutable `uint64 sourceChainId` constructor parameter; bound
        into the relayer signature digest alongside `block.chainid` and
        `vault`. Domain tag bumped: ZEBVIX_BRIDGE_MINT_v1 → _v2. Old v1
        signatures will no longer verify; relayers must re-sign for the
        new domain. Constructor signature now `(address[], uint256, address,
        uint64)` — deployment scripts must pass the Zebvix L1 chain id
        (8989 mainnet, distinct value for testnet).
FILE:   contracts/BridgeMultisig.sol  (constants + constructor + digest)
RISK:   Backward-incompatible. Any pre-existing on-chain v1 deployments
        cannot be upgraded in place — must redeploy. No production bridge
        is live yet (per project status), so this is a pre-launch fix.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-EP2 [HIGH] — Paymaster validUntil deadline discarded
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STATUS: FIXED.
ACTION: Decode the second tuple element from validatePaymasterUserOp's
        return as `validUntil` (unix timestamp; 0 = no deadline) and enforce
        `block.timestamp <= validUntil` when non-zero. Rejected ops revert
        with "EntryPoint: paymaster validity expired".
FILE:   contracts/ZbxEntryPoint.sol  _validateAndPrefund() lines ~242–266
RISK:   Paymasters that previously returned an unintended non-zero second
        word in their return tuple may now reject ops that previously
        passed. Audit existing paymasters' return values at deployment.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-EP3 [HIGH] — handleOps reentrancy
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STATUS: FIXED.
ACTION: nonReentrant guard added to handleOps. Standard single-slot lock
        (1/2 sentinel pattern) using `_entry` state slot. Costs ~5k gas per
        handleOps invocation — acceptable trade for closing the beneficiary
        callback re-entry vector.
FILE:   contracts/ZbxEntryPoint.sol  state + handleOps signature
RISK:   None. Smart wallets calling EntryPoint.depositTo / withdrawTo from
        within their own UserOp validation already used a separate code
        path; nonReentrant only blocks recursion through handleOps itself.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S6-ST1 [HIGH] — ZbxStaking unbounded reward rate
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STATUS: FIXED.
ACTION: New constant MAX_REWARD_RATE = 1_000 * 1e18 wei/sec (~1000 tokens
        per second). setRewardRate now reverts with RewardRateAboveCap
        if newRate exceeds the cap. A stolen founder key can no longer
        crank the rate to drain the pool via legitimate user claim() calls.
FILE:   contracts/ZbxStaking.sol  state + setRewardRate
RISK:   Operationally, no real protocol should ever need to emit more than
        1000 tokens/sec; if a future grant program does, raise the constant
        in a versioned redeploy with on-chain governance approval.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STATUS: FIXED.
ACTION: 2-step transfer added: transferFounder() now sets pendingFounder;
        the nominee must call acceptFounder() to take over. New error
        NotPendingFounder + events FounderTransferStarted /
        FounderTransferred. Setting pendingFounder = address(0) cancels.
FILE:   contracts/ZbxStaking.sol  founder ops region
RISK:   None. Pattern matches BridgeVault, BridgeMultisig and Governable —
        consistent across the entire contract surface now.

ARCHITECT REVIEW PASS (2026-05-01, end of Session 6)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A second-opinion architect review was run against all 10 in-session fixes.
9 of 10 were clean. One residual issue identified and immediately closed:

  - S6-EP1 residual: postOp called without an explicit gas cap. Although
    `actualGasCost` was already settled before the call (as the closure
    block above states), an unbounded `paymaster.call(...)` allowed a
    malicious paymaster to burn the bundler's full remaining gas
    inside postOp, OOG-reverting the surrounding batch and re-opening
    the very DoS vector S6-EP1 was intended to close. Patched in the
    same session: `paymaster.call{gas: op.verificationGasLimit}(...)`.
    The `if (!pmOk)` branch absorbs OOG returns. Documented at the
    expanded S6-EP1 closure entry above.

All other fixes verified clean by the review: V2 (ABI selector preserved
for deterministic revert), V3 (constant magnitude correct), EP2 (decoded
deadline enforced), EP3 (lock correctly resets on revert via state
rollback), BM1 (constructor still validates relayer set + threshold and
binds source chain id), ST1 (cap operationally generous), ST2 (cancel +
zero-address-attempt safe), AMM1 (zero-address rejected), V5 (docs only).

SESSION 7 — RUST CRATES AUDIT (PHASE 4 CRYPTO + PRECOMPILE STUBS)
Date: 2026-05-01 (started end of session 6)
Auditor: independent re-read of core crypto + precompile dispatch surface.
Scope: zbx-crypto (6 files), zbx-block (validation + body), zbx-execution
       (verifier), zbx-sdk (signer), zbx-vm/zbx-evm/zbx-zvm precompile
       dispatchers. Compile sandbox is rocksdb-blocked — zbx-storage / state /
       prover etc. not built; all findings derived from manual code review.
================================================================================

OVERVIEW

Session 6 closed the contract surface; Session 7 turns to the Rust hot-path
where another large class of correctness/security issues lives. 12 findings:

   CRITICAL  (5):  S7-CR1 personal_sign_hash escape    (zbx-crypto + zbx-sdk)
                   S7-CR2 create_address RLP encoding  (zbx-crypto)
                   S7-CR4 vrf_verify accepts any proof (zbx-crypto)
                   S7-CR6 tx_root: flat vs merkle split (block + execution)
                   S7-VM1 zbx-vm ecrecover stub        (zbx-vm)

   HIGH      (4):  S7-CR3 merkle promoted-odd proofs   (zbx-crypto)
                   S7-CR5 vrf_score f64 non-determinism (zbx-crypto)
                   S7-CR7 verify_transactions_root skip on empty (execution)
                   S7-VM2 zbx-zvm precompile stubs return fake "valid"

   MEDIUM    (3):  S7-SDK1 signer recover_address allows v<27 ambiguity
                   S7-SDK2 signer no low-S, no EIP-155 normalization
                   S7-VM3 zbx-vm modexp/bn128/blake2f mock outputs

The majority root-cause: stub-quality crypto and precompile implementations
shipped behind the same public APIs as the production secp256k1/BLS code,
without explicit "not implemented" failure modes. The danger is **silent
acceptance** — wrong VRF proofs verified, ecrecover returning address(0),
KZG always returning success, etc. — rather than honest reverts.

Strategy: every finding either gets a real correct implementation (when it's
trivial — `\x19` byte vs literal, `0xc0+` vs `0xd0|`, removing `&& empty`
short-circuit) or is converted into a fail-explicit error so any contract
that depends on it reverts loudly instead of silently accepting bogus data.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-CR1 [CRITICAL] — EIP-191 personal_sign_hash uses literal "\\x19" string
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-crypto/src/keccak.rs:28-31
        zbx-sdk/src/signer.rs:106-112  (separate copy, same bug)
FACT:   `format!("\\x19Ethereum Signed Message:\\n{}", msg.len())` in Rust
        is the literal 4-char string `\x19` (backslash, x, 1, 9), not the
        single byte 0x19 that EIP-191 mandates. Same with `\\n` (2 chars,
        not 1 LF byte). This is the **same class of bug** Session 5 closed
        in BridgeMultisig.sol (Solidity stored "\\x19...\\n32" verbatim in
        the digest). Every signature produced/verified via these helpers
        is incompatible with `eth_sign` and every standard wallet.
IMPACT: All EIP-191 signature flows in the SDK and any consumers of
        `zbx_crypto::keccak::personal_sign_hash` produce / accept a digest
        no third-party wallet can match. Off-chain signers (MetaMask, hw
        wallets) will produce a different hash → recovery resolves to a
        completely different address → meta-tx flows broken.
RISK:   CRITICAL. Identical-shape bug to S5-BM1, hits user-facing flows.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-CR2 [CRITICAL] — create_address builds invalid RLP for sender||nonce
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-crypto/src/keccak.rs:57-83
FACT:   Two compounding bugs:
        (a) List prefix is `0xd0 | (21 + rlp_len(nonce))` — the bit-OR
            happens to equal `0xc0 + n` only because `n <= 30` for u64
            nonces (rlp_len max is 9), but the formulation is brittle and
            wrong as-stated; any future change touching the high nibble
            of `n` produces a wrong prefix and thus a wrong contract
            address.
        (b) `rlp_len` returns `(64 - leading_zeros)/8 + 1`, which gives
            `1` for nonce in [1..127] (correct: 1 byte), `2` for [128..255]
            (correct: 2 bytes — 0x81 + payload), but **`2` for [256..65535]
            (off by one; correct: 3 bytes — 0x82 + 2 payload)** and
            propagates from there. The **list prefix declares N bytes
            follow but `encode_nonce` writes N+1 bytes** for nonce ≥ 256.
            Result: `keccak256(buf)` consumes a **truncated last byte
            window** versus the true RLP encoding; CREATE addresses for
            any deployer with nonce ≥ 256 do not match Ethereum/standard
            RLP and are unrecoverable by external tooling.
IMPACT: Long-lived deployer accounts (e.g. factory contracts) compute
        wrong addresses for their own deployments; cross-tooling (etherscan
        verify, foundry, hardhat) cannot find the resulting bytecode at
        the predicted address.
RISK:   CRITICAL — silently corrupts CREATE address derivation past nonce
        255; no compile-time signal.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-CR3 [HIGH] — Merkle proof for promoted-odd leaf fails verification
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-crypto/src/merkle.rs build_from_hashes / proof / verify
FACT:   Build promotes an odd trailing node as-is (no duplication).
        Proof generation, when reaching such a leaf, pushes `layer[idx]`
        (the leaf itself) as the "sibling". Verification then folds
        `combine(C, C)` for that step, but the build path produced a
        bare `C` — these never match, so the inclusion proof for the
        last leaf in any odd-length layer **always fails verify even
        for genuinely included leaves**.
IMPACT: Light clients (zbx-light/spv.rs) and any fraud-proof / SPV
        consumer cannot validate inclusion for the most-recent tx in
        odd-tx-count blocks. Worse: the bug is asymmetric — root
        construction succeeds, only verification is broken — so honest
        provers waste effort and downstream consumers reject.
RISK:   HIGH — silent breakage of light-client verification.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-CR4 [CRITICAL] — vrf_verify is a stub that accepts ANY proof
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-crypto/src/vrf.rs:42-57
FACT:   The function computes `check = keccak256(pk||input||proof)` and
        then **immediately discards it**: `let _ = check; Ok(proof.output)`.
        Any caller-supplied (output, proof) tuple verifies; no key
        binding, no input binding, no curve operations. The only
        validation is the byte-length of the proof payload.
IMPACT: VRF-based proposer selection (consensus / leader election /
        any randomness-dependent slashing) is completely broken — an
        attacker forges a VrfProof with any desired `output`, calls
        `vrf_verify`, gets `Ok(output)`, and is treated as the legitimate
        proposer for that slot. The same VrfProof type is exposed at the
        `precompile 0x0E` interface (zbx-zvm) for in-EVM use.
RISK:   CRITICAL — fully bypasses VRF security.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-CR5 [HIGH] — vrf_score uses f64; powf is non-deterministic in consensus
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-crypto/src/vrf.rs:61-67 + select_proposer:71-83
FACT:   `vrf_score` uses `f64` arithmetic and `powf` for the weighting
        stage of proposer selection. f64 multiply/add is mostly
        IEEE-deterministic, but `powf` is implemented via libm or
        platform-specific `pow`, which differs across CPUs / glibc /
        musl / target_feature combinations. Validators on different
        architectures can compute different scores → different
        proposer indices → fork. There is also no guard against
        `stake_weight == 0` (division by zero in the exponent).
IMPACT: Mixed-architecture validator sets may permanently disagree on
        the proposer for a slot.
RISK:   HIGH — silent fork risk.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-CR6 [CRITICAL] — Two transactions_root algorithms; builders/validators split
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-block/src/body.rs:22-27       (flat keccak)
        zbx-execution/src/verifier.rs:29  (zbx_crypto::merkle)
FACT:   `BlockBody::compute_tx_root()` is `keccak256(concat(tx_hashes))`
        — a flat hash, not a Merkle root. `BlockVerifier::verify_transactions_root`
        re-computes via `zbx_crypto::merkle::transactions_root`, which is
        a binary Merkle tree over the same input. The two produce
        different 32-byte values for any block with ≥ 2 txs.
        Block builders use `body.compute_tx_root()` to populate the
        header; full nodes that go through `BlockVerifier` will reject
        every non-empty block as `TxRootMismatch` (or vice versa,
        depending which path produced the canonical header).
IMPACT: Block-level fork between any two nodes that don't share both
        code paths; even within one node, header build and verify
        disagree.
RISK:   CRITICAL — chain cannot make progress past the first multi-tx
        block under the dual code paths.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-CR7 [HIGH] — verify_transactions_root short-circuits on empty tx list
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-execution/src/verifier.rs:30
FACT:   `if computed_root != header.transactions_root && !tx_hashes.is_empty()`
        — the `&& !tx_hashes.is_empty()` makes the check **vacuous** for
        empty blocks: a malicious proposer can set `transactions_root` to
        any 32-byte value and the verifier accepts. Block hashes
        (which include `transactions_root`) become attacker-controlled
        for empty blocks.
IMPACT: Empty-block headers can be crafted with chosen `transactions_root`
        — opens a malleability vector for downstream consumers that key
        off block hash (light clients, indexers, governance contracts
        that snapshot a block hash).
RISK:   HIGH — direct integrity bypass for empty blocks.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-VM1 [CRITICAL] — zbx-vm precompile dispatcher returns stub data for ecrecover
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-vm/src/precompiles.rs:47-59 (and modexp/bn128/blake2f below)
FACT:   `precompile_ecrecover` parses the input, then **discards r/s/v and
        returns `vec![0u8; 32]` regardless** with the comment "In production:
        use secp256k1 crate to recover the public key." This dispatcher is
        the one wired through `zbx-vm/src/interpreter.rs:147` →
        `zbx-execution/src/executor.rs`. Every Solidity contract on ZBX
        that calls precompile 0x01 receives `address(0)` as the recovered
        signer.
IMPACT: Any `require(ecrecover(...) == owner)` succeeds when `owner` is
        zero (e.g. uninitialised storage, default admin in upgrade flows
        before initialise(), etc.); every ERC-2771 / EIP-712 / OpenZeppelin
        SignatureChecker pattern is silently broken — typically failing
        closed (signature never matches a real address) but in any
        owner-zero edge case, **failing open**.
        The peer dispatcher zbx-evm/src/precompiles.rs:32-50 has the
        correct real implementation wired through `zbx_crypto::secp256k1::
        recover_signer` — proof that the right code exists; zbx-vm just
        never adopted it.
RISK:   CRITICAL — universal ecrecover bypass on the VM that actually
        runs.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-VM2 [HIGH] — zbx-zvm native precompiles silently return fake "valid"/data
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-zvm/src/precompiles.rs:74-205
FACT:   Eleven precompile slots are stubbed and return success-looking
        bytes:
        * 0x01 ecrecover     → `vec![0u8; 32]`            (fake address)
        * 0x03 ripemd160     → `vec![0u8; 32]`            (fake digest)
        * 0x05 modexp        → `vec![0u8; 32]`
        * 0x06–0x08 bn128    → `vec![0u8; 64]`            (zk-SNARK paths)
        * 0x09 blake2f       → `vec![0u8; 64]`
        * 0x0A payid_resolve → SHA-256 of pay id (mock; ignores registry)
        * 0x0B kzg_verify    → `out[31] = 1`              (always success)
        * 0x0C price_oracle  → hardcoded $2500            (DeFi liquidations
                                                           pull this!)
        * 0x0D ed25519_verify→ `out[31] = 1`              (always valid)
        * 0x0E vrf_verify    → SHA-256 of input           (no key check)
        * 0x0F zusd_balance  → `vec![0u8; 32]`            (always zero)
IMPACT: zbx-zvm is wired into the proof / zk-execution and test paths
        (zbx-zvm/tests, fuzz/fuzz_targets/fuzz_zvm_*). If any production
        flow falls through to it (e.g. zk-prover trace generation), every
        precompile decision in the trace is wrong — zk proofs would
        attest to garbage. Even purely as a test-only crate this is
        dangerous: any feature gated behind property-test green light is
        validated against a fictional VM.
RISK:   HIGH — depending on whether zk-prover uses zbx-zvm at runtime,
        this can go up to CRITICAL.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-SDK1 [MEDIUM] — recover_address allows any v < 27 to map to recid 0
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-sdk/src/signer.rs:84-95
FACT:   `RecoveryId::from_byte(v.saturating_sub(27))` — any `v` in [0..27]
        saturates to 0, so v=0 and v=27 both map to recid 0; v=1 also
        maps to 0; v=29..255 maps to 2..228 most of which RecoveryId
        rejects, but 28 and 29 silently differ. There is no enforcement
        of low-S (malleability), and no EIP-155 chain-id normalization
        path. Two distinct (v) byte values may recover the same address
        for the same hash, opening a transaction-ID malleability surface
        for any caller using sdk-side recovery for replay protection.
RISK:   MEDIUM — acceptable for some SDK uses (recovery is best-effort)
        but unsafe as a foundation for protocol-level replay defence.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-SDK2 [MEDIUM] — signer.rs lacks low-S enforcement and EIP-155 helpers
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-sdk/src/signer.rs (whole module)
FACT:   `KeyPair::sign_hash` produces signatures via `sign_recoverable`
        (k256 normalises low-S internally), but `recover_address` does
        **not** reject high-S inputs — third-party signers that produce
        high-S can still verify. zbx-crypto/src/secp256k1.rs::recover_signer
        does this correctly; the SDK does not share that path.
RISK:   MEDIUM — duplicated, weaker code; deduplicate.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-VM3 [MEDIUM] — zbx-vm modexp / bn128 / blake2f / kzg are mock outputs
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  zbx-vm/src/precompiles.rs:101-143
FACT:   Same pattern as S7-VM2 but inside the production-path zbx-vm:
        bn128_pairing returns `out[31] = 1` (always success), bn128_add /
        bn128_mul return zero, kzg_point_eval returns zeros without
        verification. Less catastrophic than ecrecover (these are mainly
        used by zk and EIP-4844 paths) but still silently-wrong.
RISK:   MEDIUM — defer to fail-explicit unless real impl ships.

================================================================================
SESSION 7 — FIX PLAN
================================================================================

Trivial-and-correct fixes (apply now):
   S7-CR1, S7-CR1b   escape `\x19` / `\n`            (one-char fixes)
   S7-CR2            replace `0xd0 |` with `0xc0 +`; rewrite `rlp_len` to
                     match the real `encode_nonce` byte count.
   S7-CR5            replace `f64.powf` with deterministic integer
                     ranking (compare `output * stake_weight` as U256).
   S7-CR7            drop the `&& !tx_hashes.is_empty()` short-circuit.
   S7-VM1            replace stub ecrecover with the same wiring
                     zbx-evm uses (zbx_crypto::secp256k1::recover_signer).

Fail-explicit hardening (apply now):
   S7-CR4            vrf_verify returns `Err(ZbxError::Signature(
                     "VRF proof verification not implemented; reject"))`
                     so consensus can never accept a stub proof.
   S7-VM2            every zbx-zvm stub precompile returns
                     `Err(ZvmError::PrecompileNotFound(*address))` so
                     downstream paths revert loudly instead of using
                     fictional outputs.

Larger fixes (apply now, scoped):
   S7-CR3            switch merkle build to **duplicate-up** odd nodes
                     so proof and verify agree (Bitcoin-style); the same
                     duplicate-up trick eliminates the asymmetry without
                     changing the proof format.
   S7-CR6            unify on the merkle implementation:
                     `BlockBody::compute_tx_root` becomes a thin wrapper
                     over `zbx_crypto::merkle::transactions_root` so
                     header build and verify use the same algorithm.

Deferred (require real cryptography):
   S7-VM3 modexp/bn128/blake2f real impls — long; fail-explicit kept.
   S7-SDK1, S7-SDK2  rip out duplicate signer code; route SDK through
                     `zbx-crypto`. (Defer to Session 8: requires SDK
                     consumer audit.)

NEW FINDING discovered during fix-application investigation:

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-PROD1 [CRITICAL] — production block_producer hardcodes transactions_root = 0
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:  node/src/block_producer.rs:224
FACT:   `transactions_root: [0u8; 32]` — every block produced by the
        production node ships with a zero tx-root in the header,
        regardless of body contents. zbx-execution::verifier
        `verify_transactions_root` (S7-CR7 fix removes the empty-list
        short-circuit) will now reject every produced block.
        Net effect with S7-CR7 fix applied: chain cannot make progress
        unless block_producer is updated to compute the real merkle root.
IMPACT: Hard-block on chain progress once S7-CR7 verifier hardening is
        active. **This must land in the same release as S7-CR7 or chain
        liveness halts.**
RISK:   CRITICAL (lifecycle: cannot ship S7-CR7 alone).
RECOMMENDED FIX (deferred — out of crypto-audit scope; tag for node team):
        Replace the literal with
        `zbx_crypto::merkle::transactions_root(&body.tx_hashes())`
        (or whatever shape the producer's body type exposes); ensure
        all four tx_root paths (node producer, consensus producer,
        zbx-block::compute_tx_root, verifier) call the same merkle fn.

S7-CR6 — REVISED severity & scope:
   Original framing was "two algorithms in the same chain produce a
   fork". Re-investigation shows the production node uses zbx_types::Block
   (NOT zbx-block::Block); zbx-block is consumed only by zbx-prover
   (zk), zbx-light (SPV), and metrics/telemetry. The divergence is
   thus between (a) the zk/light derivation of tx_root and (b) the
   production verifier's tx_root. Effect: zk proofs and SPV proofs
   commit to a different root than the production header even when both
   header and body are well-formed → both fail relative to canonical.
   Severity remains HIGH (was CRITICAL), and the canonical fix is
   pulled into the broader S7-PROD1 unification work above.

SESSION 8 — Phase 4 closure: SDK signer + remaining VM precompiles (2026-05-01)
================================================================================

In-scope: S7-SDK1, S7-SDK2, S7-VM3 (all carried over from Session 7's deferred
set). Out-of-scope but flagged: S7-EVM1 / S7-EVM2 / S7-ARCH1 (newly discovered).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-SDK1 [HIGH] — APPLIED — recover_address v-byte logic was broken
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:   crates/zbx-sdk/src/signer.rs (recover_address)
WAS:     `RecoveryId::from_byte(v.saturating_sub(27))`
PROBLEM: saturating_sub silently floored every v in 0..=27 to 0, so
         * v=0 (raw EIP-2098)   → recid=0  ✓ (correct by accident)
         * v=1 (raw EIP-2098)   → recid=0  ✗ (should be 1)
         * v=2..26 (garbage)    → recid=0  ✗ (should be Err)
         * v=27                 → recid=0  ✓
         * v=28                 → recid=1  ✓
         Half of every legitimate raw-v signature recovered to the WRONG
         address. Anything that round-tripped sig→bytes→recover with raw
         {0,1} encoding (EIP-2098 compact sigs, internal SDK pipelines)
         silently authenticated as a different account.
FIX:     Delegate to `zbx_crypto::secp256k1::normalize_v_eip155` +
         `zbx_crypto::recover_signer`, which handle {0,1}, {27,28},
         EIP-155 chain-id-encoded v, and reject everything else.
TESTS:   3 new tests — accepts raw {0,1}; rejects {2,5,10,26,29,34};
         rejects high-S forgery.
COMPILE: `cargo check -p zbx-sdk` clean.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-SDK2 [HIGH] — APPLIED — low-S enforcement + dedup with zbx-crypto
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:   crates/zbx-sdk/src/signer.rs (sign_hash, recover_address)
PROBLEM: SDK signer never enforced low-S. A relayer could replace a valid
         signature `(r, s, v)` with `(r, n-s, v^1)` — both recover to the
         same address. Any nonce-tracking or replay-protection scheme that
         keyed off the signature triple (rather than the (sender, nonce)
         pair) was broken. SDK and zbx-crypto signers were two separate
         codebases drifting out of sync (S7-CR1 had to be applied twice).
FIX:     Sign path uses `zbx_crypto::PrivKey::sign` (k256's
         `sign_prehash_recoverable` is low-S by construction); recover path
         uses `zbx_crypto::recover_signer` which rejects high-S explicitly.
         The SDK file is now a thin compatibility shim — one canonical
         signer in the workspace.
TESTS:   `test_recover_rejects_high_s_malleable` constructs the (r, n-s)
         twin and asserts rejection.
COMPILE: `cargo check -p zbx-sdk` clean.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-VM3 [HIGH→CRITICAL after re-investigation] — APPLIED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:   crates/zbx-vm/src/precompiles.rs (modexp 0x05, bn128_add 0x06,
         bn128_mul 0x07, bn128_pairing 0x08, blake2f 0x09, kzg_eval 0x0a)
WAS:     All six returned plausible-looking constants — most damningly:
         * `precompile_bn128_pairing` returned `1` (true) for ANY input.
           Every Groth16 / EIP-197 zk-SNARK verifier on this VM
           accepted EVERY proof. This is the same fail-open class as
           S7-CR4 (vrf_verify) — silent universal acceptance.
         * `precompile_modexp` returned `vec![0u8; m_len]`, accidentally
           correct only when the true result happens to be 0.
         * `precompile_bn128_{add,mul}` returned point-at-infinity for
           any input, so any contract doing curve arithmetic was lied to.
         * `precompile_blake2f` returned 64 zero bytes irrespective of
           input — wrong hash entirely.
         * `precompile_kzg_point_eval` returned 64 zero bytes — would
           silently authorise EIP-4844 blob inclusion proofs.
FIX:     Added `PrecompileError::NotImplemented(&'static str)` variant
         and converted all six bodies to charge full canonical gas (so
         callers can't game the cost) then return `Err(NotImplemented)`,
         which the VM surfaces as a contract revert. The zero-output
         lie is now a loud failure instead of a quiet wrong-answer.
COMPILE: `cargo check -p zbx-vm` clean.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-EVM1 [HIGH] — NEW DISCOVERY — duplicate precompile impl in zbx-evm
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:   crates/zbx-evm/src/precompiles.rs
DETAIL:  zbx-evm ships its OWN copy of EVM precompiles 0x01..=0x09, with
         an even worse set of stubs than zbx-vm:
         * `bn128_pairing` → out[31]=1 (same fail-open as zbx-vm).
         * `bn128_{add,mul}` → 64 zero bytes.
         * `modexp` → `vec![1u8]` (not even the right length!).
         * `blake2f` → 64 zero bytes.
         * `ripemd160_hash` → `keccak256(input)[..20]` left-padded to 32.
           This is the WRONG hash function entirely; a contract calling
           `ripemd160(x)` got `keccak256(x)[..20]`. Any cross-chain
           bridge / Bitcoin-script verifier relying on RIPEMD-160 was
           silently broken.
APPLIED: Same fail-explicit treatment as S7-VM3 (revert with descriptive
         message, charge full gas). `ripemd160_hash` also reverts with a
         note that real RIPEMD-160 needs the `ripemd` crate (intentionally
         not added in this audit pass to keep the dependency surface frozen).
COMPILE: `cargo check -p zbx-evm` clean.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-EVM2 [HIGH] — NEW DISCOVERY — zbx-execution skips EVM for empty code
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:   crates/zbx-execution/src/executor.rs:257
DETAIL:  `if is_pure_value_transfer || code_to_run.is_empty() { ... skip EVM }`.
         Precompile addresses 0x01..=0x0a have no deployed code, so the
         executor classifies a top-level tx to e.g. ecrecover (0x01) as
         a value transfer and never runs the precompile. Combined with
         the absence of CALL/DELEGATECALL/STATICCALL opcodes in the
         zbx-evm interpreter (separate finding), every EVM precompile is
         currently UNREACHABLE in production via this code path. Until
         either (a) the executor learns to dispatch precompile addresses
         before the empty-code branch, or (b) a CALL opcode lands in the
         interpreter, the zbx-evm precompile fixes above are defence-in-
         depth only.
RECOMMENDED: insert a `if zbx_evm::precompiles::is_precompile(&to) { ... }`
         dispatch inside `executor.rs` BEFORE the empty-code short-circuit.
         This needs a small rewrite of the executor's call frame and is
         deferred to the node team in the same release window as S7-PROD1.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-ARCH1 [MEDIUM] — NEW DISCOVERY — three coexisting EVM crates
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:   crates/zbx-vm/, crates/zbx-zvm/, crates/zbx-evm/
DETAIL:  Three precompile-dispatch surfaces, three opcode tables, three
         drift-prone copies of ecrecover. zbx-evm is currently the
         production execution backend (zbx-execution depends on it),
         zbx-vm appears to be an older standalone interpreter, and
         zbx-zvm is the in-development next-gen ZBX-extended VM. Audit
         work has had to apply the same fix three times (S7-CR1/CR1b is
         the same shape — keccak.rs + signer.rs).
RECOMMENDED: pick ONE EVM crate to be canonical, mark the others
         `#[deprecated]`, and re-export shared building blocks (precompile
         dispatch table, opcode jumptable, gas constants) from a dedicated
         `zbx-evm-common` crate. Out of scope for crypto audit; tracked
         here for the architecture review.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SESSION 8 — POST-REVIEW CORRECTION (architect feedback 2026-05-01)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Architect review of the Session 8 diff caught one material wording discrepancy
between the S7-VM3 fix comments and the runtime gas semantics. Recording the
correction here so future readers don't get misled by the original prose:

ISSUE: The S7-VM3 fix comments stated each precompile "charges its full
       canonical gas cost" on `Err(NotImplemented)`. In fact `interpreter.rs`
       line ~159 hard-codes `gas_used: gas` on every `Err(_)`, i.e. it
       consumes ALL forwarded gas (the standard EVM convention for precompile
       failures, and stricter than what the comment claimed).

IMPACT: None on safety — burning all forwarded gas is strictly more
        conservative than the canonical-cost claim. Callers cannot game the
        unimplemented state by underpaying; they get fully drained.

ACTION: Updated the in-source S7-VM3 banner comment in zbx-vm/src/precompiles.rs
        to describe the actual runtime semantics (full-forwarded-gas burn on
        precompile failure, EVM-conformant) rather than the stricter-sounding
        "canonical cost" wording. Added a parallel note to the zbx-evm mirror
        comment also recording that the zbx-evm precompile dispatch is
        currently dead code (no CALL opcodes in the interpreter — see S7-EVM2).

Architect also confirmed:
  - S7-SDK1 / S7-SDK2: no fail-open remains in the v-byte handling.
  - S7-EVM2 severity HIGH is justified.
  - RIPEMD-160 fail-explicit (vs. adding `ripemd` crate) is the right
    audit-freeze choice; must be implemented before precompile reachability
    is turned on.

No additional code changes required by the review. Both crates re-checked
clean: `cargo check -p zbx-vm -p zbx-evm` → Finished, no errors.

================================================================================
END SESSION 8 — POST-REVIEW CORRECTION
================================================================================

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-ZVM-DOC1 [LOW — documentation drift, no code-level bug] — APPLIED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:   crates/zbx-zvm/src/lib.rs (header doc-block)
         crates/zbx-zvm/src/opcodes.rs:117–118 (inline NOTE comments)
WAS:     Two pieces of stale documentation contradicted the actual code.
         (1) lib.rs header table claimed ZBX-native opcodes were at 0xF0–0xF9
             with the explicit line "ZVM opcodes occupy 0xF0–0xF9 (previously
             INVALID in EVM)". This is doubly wrong: 0xF0–0xF5 are NOT
             invalid in EVM (they are CREATE/CALL/CALLCODE/RETURN/
             DELEGATECALL/CREATE2 — the system call range), and the actual
             code has always allocated ZBX-native at 0xC0–0xC9.
         (2) opcodes.rs inline comments on the CREATE/CALL declarations
             read "NOTE: in ZVM this is PAYID — CREATE moved to ZVM_CREATE"
             — referencing a never-implemented `ZVM_CREATE` symbol from an
             abandoned design draft.
PROBLEM: A developer trusting the lib.rs header would read "PAYID is at
         0xF0", write `PUSH1 0xF0` thinking they were calling the Pay-ID
         resolver, and instead emit a CREATE opcode. If/when ZVM is wired
         into a production execution path (currently it is not — production
         uses zbx-evm per S7-ARCH1), this becomes a fund-loss footgun: a
         contract intended to look up an address would instead deploy an
         empty contract from arbitrary stack contents.
FIX:     (1) lib.rs header table updated to show 0xC0–0xC9, plus an
             explicit Backwards Compatibility note enumerating the EVM
             system-call range (0xF0..=0xFF) so the "previously INVALID"
             confusion cannot reappear.
         (2) opcodes.rs NOTE comments replaced with a positive-statement
             block telling future readers NOT to reintroduce the remap
             ("existing Solidity contracts depend on these standard hex
             codes").
         Both edits are pure documentation — no code path changes, no
             opcode dispatch changes, no public API changes.
COMPILE: Sandbox SIGKILLs `cargo check -p zbx-zvm` (rocksdb pull via
         zbx-state, known environmental constraint). Edits are comment-
         only so compilation cannot regress; the data lines `CREATE =
         0xF0,` … `CREATE2 = 0xF5,` are byte-identical to before.
SEVERITY: LOW today (zbx-zvm not in production hot-path), but the same
         doc would become MEDIUM the moment ZVM lands in zbx-execution.
         Filed under the same release window as S7-ARCH1.

END SESSION 8 — POST-REVIEW CORRECTION + S7-ZVM-DOC1

================================================================================
SESSION 9 — S7-EVM2 closure: precompile dispatch in zbx-execution (2026-05-01)
================================================================================

This is the productivisation step for the Session 8 zbx-evm precompile
hardening. Without this fix, every zbx-evm precompile fix from S7-EVM1 was
defence-in-depth (dead code at runtime).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-EVM2 [HIGH → APPLIED] — top-level precompile dispatch in executor
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:   crates/zbx-execution/src/executor.rs (BlockExecutor::execute_tx,
         step 4 — EVM dispatch)
WAS:     Top-level transactions to precompile addresses 0x01..=0x09 silently
         no-opped. Step 3 of execute_tx classifies them as
         `is_pure_value_transfer = true` (because the precompile address
         account is not marked as a contract — precompiles have no deployed
         code). Step 4's short-circuit then ran:
            if is_pure_value_transfer || code_to_run.is_empty() {
                (ExitStatus::Succeeded, 0u64, Vec::<Log>::new())
            } else { ... EVM dispatch ... }
         So `tx.to = 0x000…0001` (ecrecover) returned Succeeded with no
         output, and the caller's intended computation never happened. The
         caller paid only intrinsic gas, never knew anything had failed.
         Combined with the absence of CALL/DELEGATECALL/STATICCALL opcodes
         in the zbx-evm interpreter, this made every EVM precompile
         unreachable in production.
FIX:     Inserted a precompile dispatch branch BEFORE the value-transfer
         short-circuit:
             if tx.tx.to.is_some()
                 && zbx_evm::precompiles::is_precompile(&callee)
             {
                 let forwarded = gas_limit.saturating_sub(intrinsic);
                 match zbx_evm::precompiles::call_precompile(
                     &callee, calldata, forwarded
                 ) {
                     Ok((_output, used)) =>
                         (ExitStatus::Succeeded, used, no logs),
                     Err(e) =>
                         (ExitStatus::Failed(e), forwarded /* burn all */,
                          no logs),
                 }
             } else if is_pure_value_transfer || code_to_run.is_empty() {
                 ... existing short-circuit ...
             } else {
                 ... existing EVM dispatch ...
             }
GAS:     Forwarded gas = gas_limit - intrinsic. On Ok, charges precompile-
         reported `used` (its canonical cost). On Err, charges full
         forwarded gas (mirrors zbx-vm/interpreter.rs line 159, which is
         standard EVM convention for precompile reverts — and matches the
         Session 8 architect-corrected gas-semantics note for S7-VM3).
VALUE:   Architect feedback: my initial comment incorrectly claimed value
         debit was being skipped for precompile targets. In fact step 6 of
         execute_tx already credits `to` (= the precompile address) with
         `value_u128` on success, which is exactly the Ethereum-mainnet
         behaviour — wei sent to a precompile is stranded at that address
         (the precompile has no withdrawal logic, so the funds become
         permanently locked). On revert, step 6's else branch refunds the
         value to the sender. The settlement path was already correct;
         only my comment was wrong. Comment updated to describe the actual
         value-flow.
OUTPUT:  Top-level call return data is not surfaced anywhere in this
         codebase's receipt path — the existing EVM dispatch arm also
         discards the interpreter `output` value. Matches Ethereum: top-
         level call return data is not preserved in receipts; callers
         needing return data must invoke through a wrapper contract.

ARCHITECT REVIEW: PASS. Branch ordering correct (precompile-first before
         value-transfer short-circuit). Gas accounting coherent. Output
         discard consistent with codebase conventions. tx.to = None
         (CREATE) deliberately not routed through precompile dispatch —
         CREATE is a different transaction kind. No new auth/injection
         /secret-handling risks introduced.

COMPILE: NOT verified in this sandbox. zbx-execution transitively pulls
         RocksDB (zbx-execution → zbx-state → rocksdb), and the bash
         sandbox SIGKILLs cargo when compiling librocksdb-sys (memory
         pressure). zbx-evm itself was re-checked clean. The public APIs
         used (`is_precompile`, `call_precompile`, `ExitStatus::Failed`,
         `debug!` macro from `tracing`) are all confirmed to exist via
         direct ripgrep into the source. Manual diff review + architect
         review are the verification proxies for this single edit.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-EVM2-TESTS [DEFERRED to node-team CI] — architect-prescribed test list
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The architect review of the S7-EVM2 fix prescribed three focused tests
that should be added in node-team CI (which doesn't have the rocksdb
SIGKILL constraint that prevents writing them in this audit sandbox):

  (a) `to = Address(0x00…0001)` (ecrecover) executes and charges the
      precompile-reported gas (~3000 gas), NOT 0. The receipt shows
      Succeeded.

  (b) Precompile `Err(_)` consumes ALL forwarded gas. Easiest harness:
      call `to = 0x00…0005` (modexp) which is now fail-explicit per
      S7-VM3, and assert `gas_used == gas_limit` and the receipt shows
      Failed(Precompile(...)).

  (c) Successful precompile call with non-zero `value` credits the
      precompile address balance with `value_u128` (Ethereum stranded-
      funds semantics). Easiest harness: call `to = 0x00…0004`
      (identity) with `value = 100` and assert
      `view.get_account(0x00…0004).balance() == 100` after execution.

These tests will exercise the new precompile branch, the gas-burn
on Err branch, and the value-stranding behaviour respectively. Filing
under the S7-EVM2 closure for traceability.

   - Net: ~3000 LOC of compiled-but-unreachable VM code.
COST RECOVERED: every S7-* precompile fix this audit had to be applied
   to 2-3 separate VM copies (S7-VM3 + S7-EVM1 + zvm mirror). Drift cost
   is superlinear in VM count and was directly responsible for the
   S7-ZVM-DOC1 doc-drift miss-and-catch and the S7-EVM2 silent-no-op
   bug class. One-VM workspace makes both impossible by construction.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
S7-ARCH1-A [HIGH, NEW — derived from S7-ARCH1 analysis]
   ALL ZBX-NATIVE OPCODES UNREACHABLE IN PRODUCTION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHERE:   crates/zbx-zvm/src/opcodes.rs  (PAYID 0xC0 .. ZVMLOG 0xC9)
         crates/zbx-zvm/src/precompiles.rs  (KZG 0x0B, ed25519 0x0D)
WHAT:    docs/ZVM.md advertises ten ZBX-native opcodes
         (PAYID, ZUSDBAL, ZBXPRICE, ZBXTIME, AASENDER, CHAINVER,
          BLOBFEE, PAYIDSET, ZBXBURN, ZVMLOG)
         plus two ZVM-only precompiles (KZG at 0x0B, ed25519 at 0x0D)
         as a flagship product feature ("ZBX Chain's execution
         environment ... a superset of EVM").
WHY:     These opcodes/precompiles ONLY exist in zbx-zvm, which has
         zero callers from zbx-execution. Production transactions
         dispatch through zbx-evm, whose opcode table treats 0xC0-0xC9
         as INVALID (revert with no return data) and whose precompile
         table only has 0x01-0x09.
IMPACT:  Any contract that compiles against the ZVM opcode set or
         calls 0x0B / 0x0D will revert on the live chain. The advertised
         feature set is undelivered. This is a launch-blocking marketing
         vs. implementation gap if ZVM features are part of the chain's
         value proposition.
FIX:     Two paths, see S7-ARCH1 §4 Option B/C:
         (B) Delete zbx-zvm + remove ZBX-native-opcode claim from
             docs/ZVM.md (defer feature to a future cycle).
         (C) Re-introduce the ZBX-native opcodes as a feature-gated
             extension on zbx-evm AFTER deleting zbx-zvm/zbx-vm. Do
             NOT keep the parallel-VM pattern.
SEVERITY:HIGH (advertised features missing in prod), but no fund-loss
         /signature-forgery vector — affected contracts simply revert
         instead of executing, so the failure mode is fail-closed.

SESSION 10 — VM call-family scope + small safe fixes (2026-05-01)
================================================================================

Context: Session 9 closed S7-EVM2 (precompile dispatch) and surfaced S7-ARCH1
(3-VM consolidation design). User chose **Option C** from the S7-ARCH1 design
doc — keep both `zbx-evm` AND `zbx-zvm` in production with full functionality —
which forces the question: what does "full functionality" actually mean given
the call-family gaps Session 9 surfaced?

This session investigates the gap honestly, delivers a comprehensive
implementation plan for node-team to execute multi-day, and applies the small
safe in-session fixes that don't require multi-frame consensus-critical work.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SESSION 10 INVESTIGATION RESULTS — "full functionality" scope
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

DISPATCH SURVEY (definitive grep across all 3 VM interpreters):

  zbx-vm/src/interpreter.rs:
    ZERO handlers in 0xF0-0xFF range. No CALL, CREATE, DELEGATECALL,
    STATICCALL, CALLCODE, CREATE2, SELFDESTRUCT, RETURN, REVERT.
    Despite the crate's claim of "complete EVM Cancun-era support".
    (Note: zbx-vm has 0 production callers per Session 7 dep audit, so
    this is dead-code fallout, not a live consensus issue.)

  zbx-zvm/src/interpreter.rs:
    Only RETURN (line 216) and REVERT (line 222). No CALL family,
    no CREATE family, no SELFDESTRUCT.
    PLUS: catch-all `_ =>` arm silently NOPed every unimplemented opcode
    and continued execution — see S7-ZVM-INVOPS below.

  zbx-evm/src/interpreter.rs (THE production VM, 13 callers):
    Only RETURN (byte 0xF3, line 264). Nothing else from 0xF0-0xFF.
    No CALL family, no CREATE family, no SELFDESTRUCT, no REVERT.

  zbx-zvm/src/executor.rs:
    Single-frame executor — calls ZvmInterpreter::new(ctx, host).run()
    once and returns. No nested call stack, no depth tracking, no
    state-snapshot/revert journaling. (S7-ZVM-EXEC1.)

  ZvmHost trait (crates/zbx-zvm/src/host.rs):
    Defined with full surface (balance, storage_load/store, code,
    code_hash, block_hash, resolve_pay_id, zusd_balance, zbx_price_usd,
    blob_base_fee, has_pay_id, burn_zbx, emit_zvm_log).
    Implemented by exactly ONE struct workspace-wide: MockZvmHost
    (in the same file). Zero production implementations exist.
    (S7-ZVM-HOST2.)

  MockZvmHost.code_hash:
    Uses sha2::Sha256 instead of keccak256. EVM convention (EXTCODEHASH
    opcode 0x3F, EIP-1052) is keccak256. If MockZvmHost is ever copied
    as a template for the production impl, this becomes a consensus
    break. (S7-ZVM-HOST1.)

  ZVM-native opcode handlers (PAYID/ZUSDBAL/ZBXPRICE/ZBXTIME/AASENDER/
  CHAINVER/BLOBFEE/PAYIDSET/ZBXBURN/ZVMLOG):
    All 10 ARE implemented (lines 232-298). Wiring is correct against
    the ZvmHost trait. The original Session 10 Stage-1 plan to "add
    these" was moot — they exist.

NET: every claim of "ZVM with full functionality" + "EVM-compatible" requires
~3500 LOC of carefully gas-conformant CALL/CREATE/journal/dispatcher work
across BOTH VMs. This is multi-day node-team work; it cannot be done blind
in the audit sandbox where rocksdb compile-cascade prevents verifying any
zbx-execution-touching change.

NEW FINDINGS DISCLOSED IN SESSION 10
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[CRITICAL] S7-EVM3 — CALL family entirely missing in BOTH zbx-evm + zbx-zvm
  WHERE:   crates/zbx-evm/src/interpreter.rs::step()
           crates/zbx-zvm/src/interpreter.rs::step()
  IMPACT:  Chain cannot execute any non-trivial Solidity contract. All
           inter-contract calls and CREATE deployments silently revert.
           ERC-20 transfer (which uses CALL internally) fails. Uniswap
           swaps fail. OpenZeppelin proxies fail. Effectively the chain
           ships with EVM == "single-frame arithmetic only".
  PLAN:    docs/proposals/S7-EVM3-call-family-implementation.md
           (11 workstreams W1-W11, ~3500 LOC, ~16 days, full gas tables,
           journal contract, multi-frame executor design, dispatcher,
           ProductionZvmHost, ethereum/tests harness, sequencing,
           architect-review checkpoints, rollback plan).
  STATUS:  DEFERRED — plan-doc delivered, node-team execution required.

[CRITICAL — FIXED THIS SESSION] S7-ZVM-INVOPS — silent-NOP catch-all
  WHERE:   crates/zbx-zvm/src/interpreter.rs::step() — final `_ =>` arm
  PRIOR:   `_ => { debug!(op = %op, "unimplemented opcode (NOP)"); }`
  IMPACT:  Every unimplemented opcode (including CALL/CREATE/etc that
           currently fall through here) was silently no-op'd while
           execution continued. A contract calling another contract
           via CALL would: skip the call entirely, continue executing
           as if the call had returned success with empty data, and
           leak the would-be-CALL stack arguments onto the operand
           stack — producing wildly wrong state transitions. Catastrophic
           consensus-break vector if a real CALL had ever reached prod.
  FIX:     Replaced with EVM-yellow-paper-conformant INVALID halt:
             return Ok(Some(self.finish(ExecutionStatus::InvalidOpcode(byte))));
           Burns remaining gas, halts current frame, parent frame sees
           the failure and reverts atomically.
  STATUS:  CLOSED. Pure-leaf compile-verify deferred (sandbox SIGKILL
           on rocksdb cascade affects zbx-zvm via zbx-state dep).
           Pattern is identical to existing RETURN handler at line 216,
           types match (byte: u8 → InvalidOpcode(u8)), tracing macro
           syntax verified valid. Architect review next.

[HIGH] S7-ZVM-HOST1 — MockZvmHost.code_hash uses SHA256 instead of keccak256
  WHERE:   crates/zbx-zvm/src/host.rs::MockZvmHost
  IMPACT:  Test-only impact today (MockZvmHost is the only impl). If
           used as a copy-paste template for the production ZvmHost
           (W8 of the S7-EVM3 plan), would silently produce wrong
           EXTCODEHASH return values — consensus break.
  FIX:     Switch to sha3::Keccak256 in MockZvmHost AND prescribe the
           same in W8 of the S7-EVM3 plan. Spec'd in the plan doc §9.
  STATUS:  DOCUMENTED — fix included in S7-EVM3 plan W8.

[HIGH] S7-ZVM-HOST2 — zero production ZvmHost implementations
  WHERE:   workspace-wide grep for "impl ZvmHost"
  IMPACT:  No way to actually use zbx-zvm in production today. Every
           ZVM opcode handler calls self.host.X() which only resolves
           to MockZvmHost in tests. Production transactions cannot
           reach zbx-zvm code paths even if S7-EVM3 dispatcher (W7) is
           wired, because there's no host to dispatch to.
  FIX:     New `crates/zbx-execution/src/zvm_host.rs` — ProductionZvmHost.
           Spec'd in detail in the S7-EVM3 plan §9 with full method-by-
           method delegation map.
  STATUS:  DOCUMENTED — closed by S7-EVM3 plan W8.

[HIGH] S7-ZVM-EXEC1 — single-frame ZVM executor (no nested calls)
  WHERE:   crates/zbx-zvm/src/executor.rs (66 LOC)
  IMPACT:  Even after CALL handlers are added (W4), the executor cannot
           manage frames. Need either recursion (Rust-stack risk at
           depth 1024) or a trampoline frame stack (recommended).
  FIX:     Replace single-frame executor with `Vec<CallFrame>`
           trampoline. Spec'd in the S7-EVM3 plan §6 with skeleton.
  STATUS:  DOCUMENTED — closed by S7-EVM3 plan W5.

[LOW — FIXED THIS SESSION] S7-ZVM-DOC2 — opcodes.rs file-header doc drift
  WHERE:   crates/zbx-zvm/src/opcodes.rs:1
  PRIOR:   //! ZVM opcode definitions — EVM (0x00–0xEF) + ZBX-native (0xF0–0xF9).
  IMPACT:  Both halves wrong — EVM is 0x00–0xFF address space (CALL
           lives at 0xF1, SELFDESTRUCT at 0xFF), and ZBX-native opcodes
           live at 0xC0–0xC9 (chosen specifically to avoid colliding
           with EVM system ops). S7-ZVM-DOC1 fixed lib.rs and an inline
           comment in Session 8 but missed this file-header.
  FIX:     Replaced header with accurate range + inline note that
           explains the prior wrong claim and references S7-ZVM-DOC1
           for cross-context.
  STATUS:  CLOSED. Pure comment fix.

================================================================================
SESSION 10 ADDENDUM — Architect-review follow-ups (2026-05-01)
================================================================================

Architect review of Session 10 deliverables returned PASS with three
actionable items. All three addressed in this addendum.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ITEM 1 [LOW → promoted to CRITICAL] — S7-EVM-INVOPS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ARCHITECT NOTE:
  "zbx-evm still has the `_ => {}` NOP behaviour that was just fixed in ZVM.
   Apply the InvalidOpcode fix to zbx-evm's catch-all arm for consistency."

PROMOTION RATIONALE:
  Architect rated this LOW (consistency), but on closer inspection it is
  CRITICAL: zbx-evm IS the production VM (13 callers from zbx-execution).
  The silent-NOP catch-all in zbx-evm is the same vulnerability class as
  S7-ZVM-INVOPS but in a code path that ACTUALLY runs in production. Every
  missing 0xF0-0xFF op (CALL/CREATE/etc — S7-EVM3) currently falls through
  this arm and silently NO-OPs while leaking call arguments back onto the
  stack. This is the literal mechanism by which S7-EVM3 manifests as
  "contracts silently misbehave" instead of "contracts cleanly revert".
  Without this fix, even after S7-EVM3 W1-W11 lands, any future hardfork
  opcode that isn't yet wired would silently NOP again — a permanent
  forward-compatibility consensus-break vector.

WHERE: crates/zbx-evm/src/interpreter.rs line 287 (now 290-306 after edit)

PRIOR:
  0xfe => return Err(EvmError::InvalidOpcode(0xfe)), // INVALID
  _ => {} // unimplemented opcode — treat as NOP for interface completeness

FIX (applied this addendum):
  0xfe => return Err(EvmError::InvalidOpcode(0xfe)), // INVALID
  _ => {
      // S7-EVM-INVOPS [CRITICAL fix]: yellow paper appendix H requires
      // unknown opcodes to halt with INVALID. Mirror the 0xFE arm above.
      return Err(EvmError::InvalidOpcode(byte));
  }

VERIFICATION:
  cargo check -p zbx-evm: PASS (zbx-evm is a pure leaf, sandbox-compilable).
  Only pre-existing unused-import warnings, none from this change.
  byte: u8 in scope from line 143; matches InvalidOpcode(u8) variant.
  step() returns Result<Option<ExitStatus>, EvmError>; existing 0xFE arm
  uses identical Err(EvmError::InvalidOpcode(_)) pattern at line 289.

  Note on gas semantics: existing 0xFE arm does NOT burn-all-gas (the
  run() loop returns gas_used = total - remaining at line 133, preserving
  remaining gas to the caller). My change is behaviourally identical to
  the 0xFE arm. If yellow-paper-strict gas-burn-all-on-INVALID is required,
  that is a SEPARATE finding affecting both the existing 0xFE arm AND my
  new catch-all — flag as future work S7-EVM-GAS-BURN if needed.

STATUS: CLOSED, code applied + compile-verified.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ITEM 2 [HIGH] — Missing precompile dispatch in CALL handler (plan §2.2)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ARCHITECT NOTE:
  "Plan §2.2 step 11 dispatches to target_code. If addr is a precompile
   (0x01-0x09), host.code(addr) returns empty Vec, and the plan treats
   empty code as 'no-op success' — but precompiles MUST execute their
   logic. Add: if is_precompile(addr) { dispatch_precompile(...); }"

WHERE: docs/proposals/S7-EVM3-call-family-implementation.md §2.2 (CALL
       skeleton step 10b/11)

FIX (applied this addendum, plan-doc patch):
  Inserted explicit step 10b BEFORE step 11 in the CALL skeleton:
    if zbx_evm::precompiles::is_precompile(&addr) {
        let precompile_result = zbx_evm::precompiles::dispatch(
            &addr, &calldata, forwarded
        );
        match precompile_result {
            Ok((output, gas_used)) => { /* write retdata, push 1 */ }
            Err(_) => { /* revert snapshot, push 0 */ }
        }
    }
  Plus added "Precompile note" to §2.3 CALLCODE to clarify that the same
  dispatch path applies (precompile output is storage-context-independent).
  STATICCALL inherits step 10b through the same code path. DELEGATECALL
  rarely targets precompiles in practice but the step-10b branch
  remains correct — precompile dispatch ignores the storage context.

STATUS: CLOSED, plan-doc patched.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
