================================================================================
SESSION 11 — Bridge cluster audit (2026-05-01)
================================================================================

Surface in scope:
  Rust:     crates/zbx-bridge/  (8 files, 857 LOC)
  Solidity: contracts/ZbxBridge.sol         (BSC-side lock+release+relayer)
            contracts/BridgeVault.sol       (BSC-side mint+lock with caps)
            contracts/BridgeMultisig.sol    (BSC-side N-of-M oracle quorum)
            contracts/ZusdPricePeg.sol      (peg monitoring stub)

20 findings: 4 CRITICAL, 7 HIGH, 7 MEDIUM, 2 LOW.

NOTE on severity for Rust findings: many are CONDITIONAL on the Rust crate
ever being wired into the production tx pipeline. As of this session, the
crate has ZERO workspace consumers (S11-BRIDGE-DEAD1) and 499 of its 857
LOC are not even declared as modules (S11-BRIDGE-DEAD2). So today, the
production bridge runs ENTIRELY through the Solidity contracts on BSC.
The Rust findings still matter because (a) the crate is published and may
be picked up by node-team tooling, and (b) anyone reading it as
"reference implementation" will absorb the bugs. They are flagged HIGH/
CRITICAL with the "(IF wired)" qualifier — meaning their severity becomes
exactly that the day they leave the orphan state.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
RUST CRATE FINDINGS (zbx-bridge)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[HIGH] S11-BRIDGE-DEAD1 — zero-consumer crate
   crates/zbx-bridge has NO workspace consumers (verified: grep across all
   Cargo.toml + use statements). The chain advertises "Cross-chain bridge
   for Zebvix — Ethereum, BSC, and Polygon asset bridging" (Cargo.toml
   description) but this Rust crate is unreachable from any tx pipeline.
   Production bridge is Solidity-only.
   → Fix: either delete (preferred) or wire into zbx-execution as the
     receipt-proof verifier for inbound bridge events.

[HIGH] S11-BRIDGE-DEAD2 — undeclared modules (499 LOC)
   src/lib.rs declares: error, multisig, proofs, relayer (4 modules).
   src/ contains 8 .rs files. Three are orphaned at module level:
     events.rs       (63 LOC)
     lock_unlock.rs  (300 LOC)  — heart of the bridge logic, dead
     validator_set.rs (136 LOC)
   Even if anyone imports zbx-bridge, they cannot call lock_unlock.
   → Fix: add `pub mod lock_unlock; pub mod validator_set; pub mod events;`
     to lib.rs OR delete the files.

[CRITICAL — IF wired] S11-BRIDGE-RUST-PROOF1 — wrong Merkle algorithm
   src/proofs.rs:25-50 verifies receipts using BINARY Merkle:
     current = if index % 2 == 0 { keccak(current||sibling) }
                                  else { keccak(sibling||current) };
     index /= 2;
   Ethereum's receipts trie is a Modified Merkle Patricia Trie (MPT) —
   variable-length keys, branch nodes with 17 children, RLP-encoded
   nibble paths. Binary Merkle proofs are STRUCTURALLY INCOMPATIBLE.
   If this code is ever wired up to verify real Ethereum receipt proofs,
   it will either always fail or accept maliciously crafted proofs.
   Comment in lib.rs says "On-chain Merkle proof verification for
   trustless security" — no trustless security exists today.
   → Fix: replace with a real MPT verifier. Reference: trin-execution,
     reth's mpt module, or geth's trie.VerifyProof. ~600 LOC.

[CRITICAL — IF wired] S11-BRIDGE-RUST-RELAY1 — id collision across directions
   relayer.rs:51-62: `BridgeRequest.id` derives from
     id_input = from || to || net_amount || block_number || timestamp || target_chain
   The `request_type` field (Deposit vs Withdrawal) is NOT included.
   Two requests with identical (from, to, amount, block, timestamp,
   target_chain) but opposite directions get the SAME id. The second
   submission hits `BridgeError::DuplicateRequest` and is rejected.
   Realistic scenario: a market-maker doing simultaneous lock+unlock at
   the same block to delta-hedge — one direction silently lost.
   → Fix: include `request_type as u8` in the id_input layout, bump
     id_input length to 85 bytes.

[CRITICAL — IF wired] S11-BRIDGE-RUST-RELAY2 — sig replay across directions
   relayer.rs:75-83: `msg_hash()` for the multisig signature includes:
     id || from || to || amount
   Missing: target_chain, request_type, block_number, timestamp.
   The id covers (from, to, amount, block, timestamp, target_chain) but
   NOT request_type (per S11-BRIDGE-RUST-RELAY1). So a Deposit signature
   is byte-identical to a Withdrawal signature for the same params. A
   relayer's sig collected for an inbound mint can be replayed to
   authorize an outbound unlock of the same amount.
   → Fix: include `request_type` in the msg_hash buffer.

[HIGH — IF wired] S11-BRIDGE-RUST-RELAY3 — no chain-id binding in sig
   relayer.rs:75-83: `msg_hash()` has no Zebvix chain-id binding. A
   signature collected on Zebvix mainnet (chainid 8989) is replayable
   on Zebvix testnet (7878) or any future fork that reuses the same
   MultisigKey set. Compare with the Solidity BridgeMultisig.sol which
   correctly binds both `block.chainid` (BSC) AND `sourceChainId`
   (Zebvix L1) into the digest — that fix is missing here.
   → Fix: include `CHAIN_ID` (and ideally a domain-tag like the Solidity
     `_DOMAIN_TAG` constant) in the msg_hash buffer.

[HIGH — IF wired] S11-BRIDGE-RUST-RELAY4 — execute() has no chain effect
   relayer.rs:140-151: `execute()` only sets `done.executed = true` and
   moves the request from pending → completed maps. No mint, no unlock,
   no balance write, no state-machine call. The whole "executor" is a
   pure HashMap juggler. If anyone wires this in expecting it to actually
   bridge funds, nothing happens — the user's lock is permanent dead
   weight on the source chain.
   → Fix: relayer.execute() must call into the receiving chain's mint
     handler (zbx-execution::apply_bridge_mint or similar) and the
     burning chain's unlock handler. Closed by S7-EVM3-style wiring doc.

[MEDIUM — IF wired] S11-BRIDGE-RUST-RELAY5 — Expired error never raised
   relayer.rs: `BridgeRequest.timestamp` exists; `BridgeError::Expired`
   exists; submit/confirm/execute NEVER check expiration. Old pending
   requests can be confirmed and executed indefinitely. If a confirming
   relayer set is rotated, OLD requests can be confirmed by the NEW
   set (because msg_hash doesn't bind to a specific signer set version).
   → Fix: enforce e.g. `block_number - req.block_number ≤ 7200` (~24h)
     on confirm/execute. Plus bind signer-set epoch into msg_hash.

[MEDIUM — IF wired] S11-BRIDGE-RUST-MS2 — DoS via single bad sig
   multisig.rs verify_threshold strict-mode REJECTS the entire batch if
   ANY one supplied signature is malformed/wrong-signer/duplicate. This
   is documented as an intentional anti-grief defense (re S4 finding M-07,
   AUDIT_2026-04-30.md) but it ALSO gives a single malicious relayer
   trivial DoS power: include one garbage sig and the whole batch is
   rejected, no quorum reached.
   This is a fundamental tension: strict mode prevents one class of
   grief (silent-drop hides bugs) but enables another (malicious-include
   breaks quorum). The Solidity BridgeMultisig solves it by accepting
   sigs ONE-AT-A-TIME (each submitMint is independent), not batched.
   → Fix: switch to per-signer accumulation API (one sig per call) so
     no relayer can taint others' submissions.

[LOW] S11-BRIDGE-RUST-MS1 — error fidelity loss
   multisig.rs verify_threshold returns `InsufficientConfirmations` for
   ALL error variants (unknown signer, duplicate, mismatch, bad sig).
   Operators cannot tell "bad signer key" from "not enough signatures
   collected yet" without log scraping.
   → Fix: add discrete error variants UnknownRelayer, DuplicateSigner,
     SignatureMismatch and return them directly.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SOLIDITY ZbxBridge.sol FINDINGS (BSC-side lock + release)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[CRITICAL] S11-BRIDGE-SOL-OUT1 — bridgeOut nonce collision
   Line 91-93:
     bytes32 nonce = keccak256(abi.encodePacked(
         msg.sender, token, amount, block.timestamp, block.number
     ));
   Two `bridgeOut` calls from same sender for same token+amount in the
   same block produce IDENTICAL nonce. No `processedNonces[nonce]`
   check on bridgeOut (only on bridgeIn). Both transferFrom succeed,
   both `BridgeOutInitiated` events fire with the SAME nonce.
   Off-chain relayer sees two events with one nonce. Behavior depends
   on relayer impl: most dedupe by nonce → ONE mint on the destination
   chain → one of the two user's locks is silently lost.
   Realistic: programmatic bridging (DEX integrations, MEV bots,
   batch-bridging UIs). Same-block same-amount is plausible.
   → Fix: add `mapping(address => uint64) public bridgeOutCount` and
     include `bridgeOutCount[msg.sender]++` in the nonce derivation.

[HIGH] S11-BRIDGE-SOL-OUT2 — no source-chain binding on bridgeIn
   bridgeOut hardcodes `targetChainId = 7878` in the emitted event but
   bridgeIn does NOT verify a "burn happened on chain X" claim. Any
   relayer-set with quorum can submit a `bridgeIn` purportedly from
   Zebvix while the actual burn happened on a DIFFERENT chain (or
   nowhere at all — relayers are trusted to attest to off-chain events).
   The signature digest includes `address(this), block.chainid, token,
   to, amount, nonce` — the SOURCE chain is not bound, so a sig for
   one Zebvix-instance is reusable for any future Zebvix-fork that
   reuses the same relayer-set.
   → Fix: bind a `sourceChainId` (set immutably at construction, like
     BridgeMultisig.sol already does) into the digest. Compare with
     contracts/BridgeMultisig.sol:S6-BM1 closure — same fix needed
     here, somehow missed in this contract.

[HIGH] S11-BRIDGE-SOL-MS1 — tally-griefing via first-sig (to, amount) lock
   contracts/BridgeMultisig.sol submitMint, lines 234-265:
     // 3. Lock canonical (to, amount) on first vote; reject mismatches.
     if (t.count == 0) {
         t.to = to; t.amount = amount;
     } else if (t.to != to || t.amount != amount) {
         revert MismatchedTally(zebvixSeq, to, amount);
     }
   The tally is keyed by `zebvixSeq` ALONE. If a malicious relayer
   front-runs honest submitMint calls for `seq=N` with their own
   (to=attacker, amount=1), the tally for seq=N is permanently locked
   to (attacker, 1). All later honest relayer sigs for the legitimate
   (to=alice, amount=100) revert with MismatchedTally. The malicious
   single sig will never reach quorum, but the seq is permanently
   bricked because there is NO `resetTally(seq)` admin function and
   votedBy/tally state survives setRelayers rotation.
   Effect: any single compromised relayer key can permanently brick
   ANY individual user's bridge withdrawal — alice's burn on Zebvix is
   processed (totalSupply reduced) but she never gets her wrapped ZBX.
   Repeatable: attacker can grief every seq the relayer set processes.
   → Fix: tally key must include (to, amount). Suggested:
       bytes32 tk = keccak256(abi.encode(zebvixSeq, to, amount));
       Tally storage t = tallies[tk];
     This way mallory's (seq, mallory, 1) creates a SEPARATE tally
     from honest (seq, alice, 100); both can independently progress.
     Vault-side replay protection (`_processedZebvixSeq[seq]`) still
     prevents the seq from being credited twice.
     ALTERNATIVELY (less invasive): add `cancelTally(seq)` admin
     function gated by onlyAdmin (timelock) to recover bricked seqs.

[MEDIUM] S11-BRIDGE-SOL-PAUSE1 — no pause on ZbxBridge
   ZbxBridge.sol has NO `paused` flag, no `setPaused` function, no
   `whenNotPaused` modifier. BridgeVault.sol on the other side has
   all three. Asymmetric: if the relayer set is compromised, BSC-side
   minting can be halted instantly via BridgeVault.setPaused, but the
   Ethereum/BSC-side LOCKING through ZbxBridge cannot be halted —
   users continue feeding the attack until governance executes a
   `setRelayers` rotation through the timelock (24-48h).
   → Fix: add the standard paused/setPaused/whenNotPaused pattern to
     ZbxBridge, gated `onlyAdmin` for unpausing and `onlyAdmin` (or
     a dedicated guardian) for pausing.

[MEDIUM] S11-BRIDGE-SOL-CAP1 — no per-window cap on lock direction
   ZbxBridge has `maxBridgeAmount[token]` (per-tx cap) but NO daily/
   window cap on the LOCK direction. BridgeVault.sol enforces both
   `dailyMintCap` and `perTxMintCap` for the OPPOSITE direction.
   If the contract gets exploited (e.g. via a forged-signature path
   we haven't found yet), an attacker can drain the entire lockedAmount
   in a single block. With per-window cap, damage is bounded.
   → Fix: mirror BridgeVault's dailyMintCap+perTxMintCap pattern on
     bridgeIn (and consider it on bridgeOut to throttle inbound flow).

[MEDIUM] S11-BRIDGE-SOL-MS2 — vault setter not init-once
   BridgeMultisig has `setVault(addr)` + `lockVault()` (two-step).
   Window of vulnerability: between deploy and `lockVault`, a
   compromised founder key can re-point `vault` to a malicious
   BridgeVault that mints to attacker on quorum. Best-practice for
   wiring this critical: pass `_vault` in the constructor (immutable),
   OR have `setVault` auto-fire `lockVault` atomically.
   → Fix: simplest: collapse setVault+lockVault into a single
     `function initVault(address v) external onlyFounder { require(vault
     == address(0)); vault = v; vaultLocked = true; emit VaultSet(v); }`

[LOW] S11-BRIDGE-SOL-V155 — v ∈ {0, 1} silently rejected
   ZbxBridge `_recoverWithLowS` accepts only v ∈ {27, 28}. Modern
   wallets and EIP-155 normalized signatures use v ∈ {0, 1, 27, 28,
   chainId*2+35, chainId*2+36}. `personal_sign` is always 27/28, so
   relayer EIP-191 sigs are fine — but if a relayer ever uses an
   EIP-712 client returning {0,1}, those sigs silently get treated
   as `signer == address(0)` and skipped, reducing effective relayer
   count without operator-visible diagnostics.
   → Fix: normalize v before ecrecover:
       if (v < 27) v += 27;
   Match ECDSA libraries / OZ ECDSA.recover behavior.

[LOW] S11-BRIDGE-SOL-MS3 — tallies storage bloat
   `mapping(uint64 => Tally) public tallies` is never cleaned. After
   N executed mints, N storage slots permanently consumed. At
   1M bridges over the lifetime of the chain, ~32 MB of state. Not
   exploitable, but state-bloat compounds with other mappings.
   → Fix (optional): `delete tallies[zebvixSeq]` after `executeMint`
     succeeds. Saves gas (refund) and bounds growth.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SOLIDITY ZusdPricePeg.sol FINDINGS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[MEDIUM] S11-PEG-STUB — adjustPeg() is a TODO no-op
   ZusdPricePeg.sol:62 has a literal `// Real impl: call vault.set
   StabilityFee() based on status.` comment with no implementation.
   `adjustPeg()` only emits events; it never calls back into the vault.
   Production deployment with this stub means ZUSD peg is effectively
   UNMANAGED — keeper bots calling adjustPeg() do nothing. Combined
   with CDP issuance, this makes the stablecoin peg purely cosmetic.
   → Fix: implement the actual fee adjustment, OR remove the stub
     and document that peg adjustment is a manual governance action
     until further notice. Either is acceptable; silent stub is not.

[MEDIUM] S11-PEG-ORACLE — single-point oracle, no staleness
   `IOracle_Peg(oracle).getPrice(zusd)` reads a single oracle without
   staleness check, deviation guard, or TWAP smoothing. If the oracle
   itself returns a stale or manipulated price, every consumer
   (`getPegStatus`, `newCdpAllowed`) gets garbage. `newCdpAllowed`
   gates CDP creation in production — manipulating oracle to read
   below EMERGENCY_BAND can DoS new CDPs.
   → Fix: integrate zbx-oracle-twap (the existing TWAP module) and
     enforce a max-staleness window (e.g. ≤ 600 sec).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

                       S11-BRIDGE-RUST-RELAY2, S11-BRIDGE-SOL-OUT1
                       (3 of 4 conditional on Rust crate being wired;
                        OUT1 is unconditional — production BSC contract.)

HIGH findings (7):     S11-BRIDGE-DEAD1, S11-BRIDGE-DEAD2,

                       (S11-BRIDGE-SOL-MS1 is the most actionable

                       S11-BRIDGE-SOL-MS2, S11-PEG-STUB, S11-PEG-ORACLE

                       S11-BRIDGE-SOL-MS3

Top three production-impact recommendations (order of urgency):
   1. Fix S11-BRIDGE-SOL-OUT1 (nonce collision) — Solidity 1-line
      change in ZbxBridge.bridgeOut. Same-block same-amount silent
      fund loss. Trivial to write, trivial to test.
   2. Fix S11-BRIDGE-SOL-MS1 (tally griefing) — Solidity ~10-line
      change in BridgeMultisig. Single relayer key compromise
      currently bricks user funds permanently.
   3. Fix S11-BRIDGE-SOL-OUT2 (source-chain binding) — mirror the
      sourceChainId immutable + digest binding from BridgeMultisig.sol
      (already done there per S6-BM1) into ZbxBridge.bridgeIn.

Then either fix-or-delete the orphan Rust crate (DEAD1+DEAD2). Today
it's not exploitable because it's unreachable, but it's an attractive-
nuisance target for "let's wire up the bridge" PRs that will absorb

## Session 13 — Headline result

A **NEW CRITICAL** finding was discovered while sweeping doc-drift:
**`S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT`** — the Rust workspace contains TWO different chain-id
constants, and 24 of 33 Solidity contracts plus all SDKs and most docs
were still referencing a third (older) value.

This invalidates the previous "ready to start devnet" position. The devnet
launch playbook (`docs/proposals/DEVNET-LAUNCH-PLAN-2026-05-01.md`) has been
gated behind a HARD BLOCKER notice until `S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT` is closed.

Estimated effort to close: 1.5-2 dev-days. Tracked as new task `P0-T06`
(to be added to `PHASE-PLAN-2026-05-01.md` in the next planning pass).

---

## Session 13 — STATE_OF_FINDINGS quick table (across all sessions)

| ID | Severity | Status (S13) | One-liner |
|----|----------|--------------|-----------|
| S6-V5  | LOW (was) → **HIGH (re-opened)** | PARTIAL — fixed Session 13 sweep | Solidity `@custom:zbx-chain` was claimed closed Session 6, but only 8 of 33 contracts actually got the update. Session 13 fixed the remaining 24 + 1 testnet-only contract. |
| S7-PROD1 | CRITICAL | **STILL OPEN** (different reason than originally) | `tx_root` is no longer `[0u8; 32]` — `compute_tx_root` exists in `crates/zbx-consensus/src/block_producer.rs:128`. BUT it uses **SHA256 over concatenated tx hashes**, not Keccak256-Merkle-Patricia-Trie. External Ethereum-style verifiers, light clients, bridges, and exchanges WILL reject blocks. Re-classification needed. |
| S7-EVM3 | CRITICAL | **STILL OPEN** (verified by spot-check) | Both `zbx-evm` and `zbx-zvm` recognize `Opcode::CALL/DELEGATECALL/STATICCALL/CREATE/CREATE2/REVERT` in dispatch tables (`opcodes.rs`) AND in gas tables (`gas.rs`), but the interpreter execution path (`interpreter.rs`) only handles `CALLER`/`CALLVALUE` info opcodes — **no match arm for the real CALL family**. Execution-path implementation is the actual missing piece. |
| S11-BRIDGE-SOL-OUT1 | CRITICAL | ✅ **FIXED** (S36-bridge-out-nonce sprint, 2026-05-02) | Pre-S36 nonce derivation `keccak256(abi.encodePacked(msg.sender, token, amount, block.timestamp, block.number))` collided whenever the same sender bridged the same amount of the same token twice in the same block (3-second BSC blocks → wallet retries and MEV bots routinely triggered this). On collision, `processedNonces[nonce]` got set on the FIRST `bridgeIn` and the SECOND user's funds permanently locked with no on-chain error path. S36 fix: NEW `mapping(address => uint256) public outNonces` (state var at `ZbxBridge.sol:78`) + bridgeOut nonce derivation rewritten to composite key `bytes32 nonce = keccak256(abi.encode(block.chainid, address(this), msg.sender, outNonces[msg.sender]++))` (lines 155-158). Collision-free by construction (per-sender monotone counter) AND replay-resistant across deployments (chainid + address(this) bind the nonce to this exact ZbxBridge instance — same protection bridgeIn already applies to its digest). `abi.encode` (NOT `abi.encodePacked`) ensures type-safe domain separation across (uint256, address, address, uint256). Backward compat preserved — nonce stays bytes32, event signature unchanged, processedNonces[] semantics unchanged, Rust relay needs zero migration. New nonce bit-pattern can never collide with old scheme entries. Storage cost regression: ~22k gas first call per sender, ~2.9k subsequent (standard cold/warm SSTORE) — acceptable trade-off for safety in bridge architecture. Architect verdict PASS — all 7 review criteria (a-g) addressed positively, 0 CRIT/HIGH findings: "(a) collision-free for ALL practical attacker-controlled inputs; (b) abi.encode correct for domain separation; (c) uint256 counter cannot overflow (2^256 calls infeasible); (d) cross-chain replay-safe — bridgeIn digest binding holds (`address(this), block.chainid, token, to, amount, nonce`); (e) backward-compat with existing processedNonces[] entries confirmed; (f) gas regression standard for safety; (g) S11-BRIDGE-SOL-OUT1 effectively eliminates wallet-retry/MEV-bot collision vector." |
| S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT | **CRITICAL (NEW)** | **OPEN** | See full entry below. |
| S13-DOC-DRIFT | HIGH (NEW) | PARTIAL — Session 13 sweep | 11+ docs/scripts were referencing stale chain IDs (7878/78780/78781/78787). Session 13 fixed configs, scripts, and 7 docs in place. SDKs and source-code crates intentionally deferred to S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT proposal. |

(All other S1-S12 findings unchanged from Session 12 closing snapshot.)

---

## S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT — full finding

**Severity:** CRITICAL (devnet-blocking, mainnet-blocking)
**Discovered:** Session 13, 2026-05-01, during DOC_STATUS sweep
**Status:** OPEN — proposal `docs/proposals/S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT-fix.md` to be authored
**Estimate:** 1.5-2 dev-days

### Symptom

The workspace ships TWO contradictory chain-id constants in production code:

```text
crates/zbx-types/src/lib.rs:30      pub const CHAIN_ID: u64 = 8989;   // mainnet
crates/zbx-zvm/src/lib.rs:63        pub const ZBX_CHAIN_ID: u64 = 7878; // WRONG
```

Plus hardcoded literals in:

```text
crates/zbx-zvm/src/context.rs:34, 58              chain_id: 7878
crates/zbx-vm/src/interpreter.rs:28               chain_id: 7878
crates/zbx-vm/src/context.rs:64                   chain_id: 7878
crates/zbx-tx/src/types.rs:16                     // 7878 (comment only)
crates/zbx-tx/src/legacy.rs:113                   chain_id: 7878
crates/zbx-tx/src/legacy.rs:137-138               fn chain_id_7878_for_zbx() …
                                                  assert_eq!(legacy_tx().chain_id, 7878);
crates/zbx-wallet/src/create_import.rs:40, 43     ZBX_DERIVATION_PATH "m/44'/7878'/0'/0/0"
                                                  ZBX_COIN_TYPE = 7878
tests/integration/zvm_test.rs:25                  assert_eq!(ZBX_CHAIN_ID, 7878);
tests/integration/evm.rs:18                       chain_id: 7878
tests/integration/da_test.rs:73, 102              chain_id: 7878
tests/integration/bundler_test.rs:68, 81, 144-152 op.hash(…, 7878)
tests/unit/payid.rs:128, 145                      chain_id: 7878
sdk/zebvix-js/src/{wallet,index,contract}.ts      chainId: 7878 / CHAIN_ID: 7878
sdk/ethers-zbx/src/{wallet,provider}.ts           chainId: 7878n / Network("zebvix", 7878)
```

### Operational impact

EIP-155 transaction signing uses chain_id as one of the inputs to the signing
hash. If the VM signs with `7878` while the chain-core enforces `8989`:

1. The chain-core's `apply_tx` rejects the signed tx (wrong chain_id).
2. If the chain-core doesn't enforce strictly, then EVERY signed tx replays
   trivially across both chains (devastating cross-chain replay).

Either branch breaks the network. This is a **devnet bring-up blocker**: a
devnet would APPEAR to start (genesis loads), and stay alive as a P2P
network, but no transaction would ever land.

### Why it wasn't caught earlier

- S6-V5 (Session 6) checked Solidity contract comments only. Did not look
  at Rust source code.
- The test `chain_id_7878_for_zbx` is self-fulfilling: the constructor
  hardcodes `chain_id: 7878` and then asserts it equals `7878`. Test
  passes because both sides of the equality are the same literal.
- `crates/zbx-types/src/lib.rs::CHAIN_ID` was changed (probably mid-Session-3)
  from 7878 → 8989 without a corresponding crate-wide `rg` sweep.
- Sandbox cannot build RocksDB-linked crates (SIGKILL on storage crates),
  so a full `cargo check --workspace` is gated on the build host. A passing
  partial build hid the const inconsistency.

### Required fix (proposal scope)

A proposal `docs/proposals/S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT-fix.md` should cover:

1. **Single source of truth.** Delete `crates/zbx-zvm/src/lib.rs::ZBX_CHAIN_ID`.
   Replace every `7878` literal with `zbx_types::CHAIN_ID` (compile-time const).
   For Solidity, define `uint256 public constant ZBX_CHAIN_ID = 8989` in a
   shared `ZbxConstants.sol` and import.

2. **BIP-44 coin type — special handling.** SLIP-0044 reservations are
   independent of chain ID. The wallet path `m/44'/7878'/0'/0/0` MUST stay
   stable to avoid invalidating every existing wallet derivation. Default
   recommendation: keep coin_type 7878, rename `ZBX_COIN_TYPE` constant to
   `ZBX_BIP44_COIN_TYPE` with a doc-comment explaining the decoupling, and
   add a CI guard so that `ZBX_BIP44_COIN_TYPE != zbx_types::CHAIN_ID` is
   intentional.

3. **SDK version bump.** zebvix-js and ethers-zbx must release a major
   version because client apps need to update their `chainId` literal.
   MetaMask/wallet integrations must re-add the network with the new ID.
   Documentation must include a migration note.

4. **Test fixtures.** Rename `chain_id_7878_for_zbx` → `chain_id_matches_const`.
   Replace literal asserts with `assert_eq!(legacy_tx().chain_id, zbx_types::CHAIN_ID)`.
   Add a new test that explicitly asserts `ZBX_CHAIN_ID` (if any sub-crate
   re-exports it) equals `zbx_types::CHAIN_ID`.

5. **CI guard.** Add a Clippy lint or a custom test that scans for stray
   `7878` and `78780` literals in `crates/`, `tests/`, `contracts/`, `sdk/`,
   `scripts/`, `monitoring/`, `k8s/`, `docs/` and fails the build. Allowlist
   only the BIP-44 coin type and historical changelog entries.

6. **Re-run full test suite on a build host.** Sandbox cannot link RocksDB.
   Schedule a VPS-side `cargo test --workspace --features integration` run
   before declaring this finding closed.

## Session 13 — Re-verification spot-checks of prior findings

### S6-V5 — chain_id Solidity comments (re-opened)

Session 6 closure log claimed `@custom:zbx-chain` was updated 7878 → 8989
"in both files". Session 13 spot-check ran `rg '@custom:zbx-chain' contracts/`
and found **8 of 33 contracts** had the new value, **24 had the old `7878`**,
and **1 had `78780`** (testnet-only contract). Status re-classified
LOW → HIGH, fixed in-place by Session 13 sweep.

Lesson: closure-log scope was understated. Future Solidity-wide sweeps
should run on `find contracts -name '*.sol'`, not on a hand-picked subset.

### S7-PROD1 — `tx_root` (re-classified, still OPEN)

Original (Session 7) finding: "`tx_root` is hardcoded `[0u8; 32]` in the
production block producer". Session 13 verification: `compute_tx_root`
function does exist (`crates/zbx-consensus/src/block_producer.rs:128`) and
IS called with the selected tx set (line 65). However the implementation:

```rust
use sha2::{Sha256, Digest};
let mut h = Sha256::new();
for tx in txs { h.update(tx.hash().0); }
H256(h.finalize().into())
```

uses **SHA-256** over a flat concatenation of tx hashes. Ethereum-compatible
verifiers (light clients, bridges, exchange node integrations) expect a
**Keccak-256 Merkle Patricia Trie** root keyed by RLP-encoded transaction
index. The function will be deterministic and the network will agree on
its own value, but external integrations will reject every block.

Status: STILL OPEN. PHASE-PLAN P0-T01 should now be re-titled
"Replace flat SHA-256 tx_root with Keccak-256 MPT root" instead of
"Replace zero tx_root".

### S7-EVM3 — CALL family in EVM/ZVM (verified OPEN)

Session 13 confirms via spot-check:

- `crates/zbx-evm/src/opcodes.rs:85-87` — opcode dispatch table HAS
  CREATE/CALL/DELEGATECALL/STATICCALL/CREATE2/RETURN/REVERT.
- `crates/zbx-evm/src/opcodes.rs:117-118` — gas table HAS CREATE/CALL
  with explicit gas costs.
- `crates/zbx-zvm/src/opcodes.rs:252-260` — same for ZVM.
- `crates/zbx-zvm/src/gas.rs:47` — `Opcode::CALL | Opcode::STATICCALL |
   Opcode::DELEGATECALL => 100`.
- `crates/zbx-zvm/src/interpreter.rs` only has match arms for `CALLER`,
  `CALLVALUE`, and other info opcodes. No arm for `CALL`, `DELEGATECALL`,
  `STATICCALL`, `CREATE`, `CREATE2`. Falling through means execution
  errors out at the unimplemented opcode.
- `crates/zbx-evm/src/executor.rs` has the same shape — listed in the
  dispatcher, missing in the executor.

Status: confirmed OPEN. PHASE-PLAN P0-T03 estimate (~16 dev-days)
remains correct.

### S11-BRIDGE-SOL-OUT1 — BSC nonce-collision (still OPEN, cosmetic patch only)

Session 13 patched the cosmetic `targetChainId = 7878` literal in
`contracts/ZbxBridge.sol::bridgeOut` (line 117 post-patch) to `8989` and
added a "must coordinate with Rust relay" comment. The underlying
nonce-derivation logic (`keccak256(abi.encodePacked(...))` over
sender/amount/nonce on the BSC side, vs. `block.chainid` included in the
ZBX-side nonce on line 138) is unchanged. The cross-side mismatch that
allows a relayer to either mint duplicates or drop legit deposits is
untouched. PHASE-PLAN P0-T02 still required.

### S6 closures (Session 6 sample) — verified

Spot-check ran `rg` for the fix-marker comments inserted in Session 6:
all 5 sampled markers (`// FIX-S6-V1`, `// FIX-S6-V2`, `// FIX-S6-V3`,
`// FIX-S6-V4`, `// FIX-S6-V6`) are still present in the codebase at the
recorded line numbers. No regression.

SESSION 13.1 — Architect-review addendum (post-T07)
================================================================================

T07 architect review of the Session 13 deliverables returned a **FAIL** verdict.
Six concrete defects identified and verified against actual source. Five fixed
in-place this session; one re-classified as deferred (because the architect's
recommended approach changed the proposal scope, not the underlying gap).

This addendum supersedes any conflicting statement earlier in the Session 13
entry. Where claims disagree, this addendum wins.

## S13.1 — verified architect findings

### A1 — DEVNET-LAUNCH-PLAN binary name + CLI subcommands wrong
Severity: CRITICAL (runbook is unrunnable as written today)
Status: documented (SECOND HARD BLOCKER box added to plan-doc)

The playbook and `scripts/testnet-deploy.sh` both call `zebvix-node`.
`node/Cargo.toml:2` declares `name = "zbx-node"` and `node/Cargo.toml:11`
declares the binary `[[bin]] name = "zbx-node"`. The binary `zebvix-node`
does not exist; running the playbook today would fail at the first node-start
step with `command not found`.

Subcommand shape (`keygen`, `send`, `validator-add`) referenced in the
playbook is also not in `node/src/main.rs`. The actual subcommand matrix
must be inventoried via `cargo run --release -p zbx-node -- --help` on a
build host before the playbook can be executed.

Fix in this session: SECOND HARD BLOCKER box added to plan-doc with explicit
correction list. Actual rewriting of Phase A/B/C/D commands is out of scope
for Session 13 because it requires building `zbx-node` on a host that supports
RocksDB linking (sandbox cannot, see PHASE-PLAN P0-T01 prerequisite section).

### A2 — RPC method name `zbx_validators` does not exist
Severity: HIGH (would silently return error to operator running the playbook)
Status: documented in SECOND HARD BLOCKER box

`crates/zbx-rpc/src/zbx_api.rs:13` registers `zbx_getValidatorSet`, NOT
`zbx_validators`. Phase B "verify validator set" command would return a
"method not found" JSON-RPC error. Similarly `net_listening` returns a
boolean; for a peer-count health check the operator wants `net_peerCount`
(returns hex-quantity). Both corrected in plan-doc.

### A3 — `crates/zbx-bundler/src/mempool.rs:88` chain_id 7878 missed surface
Severity: HIGH (extends `S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT` scope)
Status: confirmed; added to deferred-set list

Architect spot-check confirmed `mempool.rs:88` reads:

```rust
let key = hex::encode(op.hash(crate::ENTRY_POINT_ADDRESS, 7878));
```

The `add` and runtime-`hash` paths use the dynamic chain_id, but the
`drain_for_bundle()` indexing path hardcodes 7878. Result after
`S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT` lands: bundles produced from drain will have
hash-mismatched UserOps relative to what the EntryPoint expects. Bundler is
currently one of the 53 unwired crates (PHASE-PLAN P1A surface), so the
production blast radius is post-P1A only — but the fix MUST land in the
same patch set as `S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT` to avoid creating a known-broken
seam in the bundler.

Action: append this surface to the `S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT-fix.md` proposal
scope. NO fix attempted in Session 13 (atomicity rule applies).

### A4 — `S13-LEGACY-CHAINID-REPLAY` (NEW finding, HIGH)
Severity: HIGH (cross-network replay risk, mainnet-relevant)
Status: NEW, OPEN

`crates/zbx-rpc/src/eth_api.rs:242` documents (and the surrounding code
implements) explicit acceptance of `chain_id == 0` legacy transactions
"for compatibility with raw signers". Pre-EIP-155 transactions have no
chain_id binding, so a legacy-signed tx valid on devnet (8990) replays
trivially on mainnet (8989) and on any third-party chain that also
accepts legacy txs. This is the same class of risk as Ethereum's pre-EIP-155
era and the reason EIP-155 was adopted.

Recommended fix (out of S13 scope): either reject legacy txs entirely on
mainnet, OR accept only when explicitly enabled via config + document
the cross-network replay risk in operator docs. Default recommendation:
reject by default, opt-in for testnet/devnet only.

Action: file as `S13-LEGACY-CHAINID-REPLAY` and add to PHASE-PLAN P0
or P1A depending on the team's preferred sequencing. NOT a devnet-blocker
on its own — devnet bring-up can proceed without this fix.

### A5 — ZbxFaucet sed-order typo: `89890` instead of `8990`
Severity: LOW (cosmetic; was caught immediately by architect review)
Status: FIXED in this session

Session 13 batch sed used the regex
`s|@custom:zbx-chain  Chain ID 7878|@custom:zbx-chain  Chain ID 8989|g`
followed by
`s|@custom:zbx-chain  Chain ID 78780 (testnet only)|@custom:zbx-chain  Chain ID 8990 (testnet only)|g`.

The first regex is a prefix of the second target; sed ran left-to-right and
turned `Chain ID 78780 (testnet only)` into `Chain ID 89890 (testnet only)`
BEFORE the second regex got a chance to match its (now-stale) anchor.
Result: `contracts/ZbxFaucet.sol:8` ended up with `Chain ID 89890 (testnet only)`,
a value that doesn't exist anywhere in the workspace.

Fixed by a follow-up sed that explicitly maps `89890 (testnet only)` →
`8990 (testnet only)`. Verified: `rg '89890' contracts/ scripts/ docs/ config/`
returns zero hits.

Lesson for future sweeps: when one regex is a prefix of another, ALWAYS
write the longer-match regex first (or use word-boundary anchors). Add
to the S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT proposal's CI guard: a literal scan should be
case-sensitive and word-boundary-anchored, not just substring.

### A6 — DOC_STATUS table claim that `zbx-bridge` is "not in `node/Cargo.toml`"
Severity: MEDIUM (factual error inherited from Session 12 closure log)
Status: FIXED in this session (DOC_STATUS row 51 updated)

Session 12 closure stated that `zbx-bridge` is an orphan crate not
referenced from `node/Cargo.toml`. Session 13 spot-check shows
`node/Cargo.toml:27` does in fact declare
`zbx-bridge = { path = "../crates/zbx-bridge" }`. The crate is wired into
the node binary as a dependency. What's still unverified is *how deeply*
the binary actually USES it (RPC route registration? background task?
just a transitive dep with no live call site?).

Fixed in DOC_STATUS by updating the ARCHITECTURE.md row to call out the
correction explicitly and re-classify the action item as "confirm wiring
depth" instead of "add orphan footnote".

Lesson: closure-log claims about presence-or-absence in `Cargo.toml` files
should be backed by `rg <crate-name> path` evidence in the closure log,
not just a session author's recollection.

## S13.2 — what shipped this session

Executed the 12-phase plan in `docs/proposals/S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT-fix.md`
end-to-end. Locked values throughout the codebase:

- `CHAIN_ID_MAINNET = 8989`  (0x231D)
- `CHAIN_ID_TESTNET = 8990`  (0x231E, also serves devnet)
- `BIP44_COIN_TYPE_ZBX = 7878`  (SLIP-44 registered, **independent of chain ID**)

### Phase-by-phase delta

| Phase | Scope | Status |
|------:|-------|--------|
| 1 | Canonical consts in `crates/zbx-types/src/lib.rs` (+ deprecated `CHAIN_ID` alias). | ✅ |
| 2 | `Cargo.toml` dep wiring: `zbx-tx`, `zbx-config`, `zbx-wallet` now depend on `zbx-types`. | ✅ |
| 3 | Rust source crates: every `\b7878\b`/`\b7879\b`/`\b7880\b` chain-id literal removed from `crates/`. Bundler API change: `BundlerMempool::new(chain_id)` now requires explicit chain ID at construction (closes the missed `drain_for_bundle` surface flagged in S13.1). | ✅ |
| 4 | Test fixtures: `tests/integration/{bundler,evm,da,zvm}.rs`, `tests/unit/payid.rs` all route through `zbx_types::CHAIN_ID_MAINNET`. Zero literals remain in `tests/`. | ✅ |
| 5 | TS SDKs: `sdk/zebvix-js/src/{wallet.ts,index.ts,contract.ts}` and `sdk/ethers-zbx/src/{provider.ts,chain.ts}` updated; example dApp chain-id hex `0x1EC6 → 0x231D`. Major version bump to 1.0.0 marked as breaking. | ✅ |
| 6 | Solidity NatSpec: 4 contract files (`ZbxPayId.sol`, `ZbxBundler.sol`, `ZRC20Base.sol`, `interfaces/IZRC20.sol`) — `@custom:zbx-chain` tags now read `Chain ID 8989 mainnet / 8990 testnet+devnet`. Also fixed "Chain Chain" typo in `ZbxBundler.sol`. | ✅ |
| 7 | Ops configs: `monitoring/prometheus.yml`, `k8s/da-node.yaml`, `scripts/{snapshot,testnet-add-validator,da-submit}.sh`. | ✅ |
| 8 | Docs: 10 files (`crates/zbx-cli/README.md`, `docs/{EVM_COMPATIBILITY,RPC_API,NFT_STANDARD,DA_LAYER,API_REFERENCE,PAYID}.md`, `docs/proposals/ZEP-001-PAYID.md`, `contracts/{README,ZRC20Standard}.md`). `docs/BRIDGE.md` intentionally **skipped** — keeps stale-warning text per S11 caveat. | ✅ |
| 9 | **CI guard**: `scripts/check-chain-id.sh` added with `\b(7878\|7879\|7880)\b` regex + word-boundary anchor + explicit allowlist (SLIP-44 BIP-44 paths, RETIRED SDK constants, frozen audit/changelog/migration history, drift-proposal docs, `BRIDGE.md` stale notice). Runs clean today: **164 allowlisted hits, 0 violations** (158 pre-S13.2 + 6 from S13.2 audit/phase-plan additions). | ✅ |
| 10 | **This addendum** (S13.2 in `AUDIT_2026-04-30.md`). | ✅ |

| 12 | Architect review (`code_review` skill). | ✅ |

### What closes vs what stays open

**Closed (this session)**:
- S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT (5-value drift consolidated to 2-value canonical pair).
- S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT bundler `drain_for_bundle` missed surface (S13.1 finding).
- DEVNET-LAUNCH-PLAN HARD BLOCKER #1 (chain-id drift).

**Still OPEN — VPS verification required**:
- The sandbox **cannot** run `cargo build`/`cargo test` for `zbx-chain`
  (RocksDB SIGKILL on 2-vCPU dev container — same constraint as Session 13).
- Therefore the per-crate compile correctness of every chain-id refactor
  in Phase 3 is **structurally verified only** (rg-clean + dependency
  graph correct + canonical const imported). Runtime/test verification
  must happen on the build VPS (srv1266996 or equivalent) via:
  ```
  bash scripts/ci-check.sh           # full gate, no --quick
  bash scripts/check-chain-id.sh     # literal guard
  ```
  before tarball deploy. **Do not flip status to CLOSED until both pass on VPS.**

**Still OPEN (separate PHASE-PLAN tasks, not this session)**:
- S13-LEGACY-CHAINID-REPLAY (mainnet ↔ devnet/testnet cross-replay risk
  because devnet and testnet now share chain ID 8990). Mitigation requires
  per-network signing-domain salt or distinct EIP-155 v-derivation —
  scoped in PHASE-PLAN P0-T07.
- DEVNET-LAUNCH-PLAN HARD BLOCKER #2 (playbook ↔ `zbx-node --help` CLI
  subcommand mismatches) — independent of S13.

### Lessons / invariants going forward

1. **Single source of truth**: chain IDs live in `crates/zbx-types/src/lib.rs`
   only. Every other crate must import via `use zbx_types::CHAIN_ID_*`.
2. **CI guard is mandatory**: `scripts/check-chain-id.sh` MUST pass on
   every PR. New chain-id literals require either (a) using the canonical
   const, or (b) explicit allowlist entry with a one-line justification
   comment in the script.
3. **BIP-44 coin type 7878 is decoupled**: SLIP-44 registration cannot be
   changed without breaking every existing wallet/HD path. Renaming the
   constant from `CHAIN_ID` to `BIP44_COIN_TYPE_ZBX` removed the source
   of confusion that originally caused the 5-value drift.
4. **Word-boundary regex**: as flagged in S13.A5, all literal scanners
   must use `\b…\b` anchors so prefix matches (e.g. `78780` matching
   `7878`) don't cause silent false positives or negatives.
5. **Stale doc allowlist is fine — but document it**: history files
   (`AUDIT`, `CHANGELOG`, `HARDENING_TODO`, drift proposals) intentionally
   quote the legacy values to record what was wrong. The CI guard's
   explicit allowlist makes that intent auditable.

================================================================================
SESSION 14 — S13.2-A architect-review correction (`code_review` follow-on)
================================================================================

**Status**: Architect review of S13.2 (Phase 12) flagged 1 CRITICAL +
1 HIGH + 2 MEDIUM. All four are now resolved. Recording here for
audit trail and to keep the per-phase ✅ entries above honest.

### CRITICAL — bundler API break was incomplete

**Finding**: Phase 3 changed `BundlerMempool::add(op)` to drop the
explicit `chain_id` argument (chain id is now bound at construction
via `BundlerMempool::new(chain_id)`). The mempool itself was updated
correctly (`crates/zbx-bundler/src/mempool.rs:80`), but the call
site in `crates/zbx-bundler/src/rpc.rs:42` still used the old 2-arg
form `self.mempool.add(op, self.chain_id)?`. Additionally,
`BundlerRpc` stored its own `chain_id` field (`rpc.rs:17,21`),
which would re-introduce a dual source of truth.

This would have failed `cargo check` on the build VPS. The S13.2
addendum's row 3 ("closes the missed `drain_for_bundle` surface
flagged in S13.1") was therefore overstated until this fix landed.

**Fix** (`crates/zbx-bundler/src/rpc.rs`, this session):
- Removed `chain_id: u64` field from `BundlerRpc`.
- Removed `chain_id` parameter from `BundlerRpc::new`.
- Added `BundlerRpc::chain_id() -> u64` accessor that delegates to
  `self.mempool.chain_id()` so callers that genuinely need the chain
  id can ask the single source of truth.
- Updated `send_user_operation` to call `self.mempool.add(op)?`.
- Added doc-comment on `new()` explaining WHY chain_id is not a
  parameter (anti-S13-CHAIN-ID-DRIFT invariant).

**Verification**:
- `rg -n 'mempool\.add\s*\([^)]*,' --type rust` returns 0 matches
  across the whole tree.
- `bash scripts/check-chain-id.sh` still ✅ clean (164/0).
- `BundlerRpc::new` has zero external callers (it is not yet wired
  into `node/`), so the constructor signature change is safe.

### HIGH — audit closure overstatement

**Finding**: Same root cause as CRITICAL. The S13.2 row 3
"Bundler API change: ... closes the missed `drain_for_bundle`
surface" was true for the mempool but not for the rpc module that
called it. After the fix above, the row is now actually accurate.
This addendum block is the audit-trail correction.

### MEDIUM #1 — P0-T06 acceptance language did not match `ci-check.sh`

**Finding**: `PHASE-PLAN-2026-05-01.md` P0-T06 acceptance criterion
1 said `ci-check.sh` runs the "full test suite end-to-end". The
actual script (`scripts/ci-check.sh:84-105`) runs only:
1. `cargo check --features $FEATURES`
2. `cargo clippy --features $FEATURES -- -D warnings -A clippy::all`
3. `cargo test --lib --features $FEATURES` (library unit tests only)

Integration tests in `tests/integration/` are NOT exercised by
`ci-check.sh`. They require a separate `cargo test --features $FEATURES`
invocation on the VPS.

**Fix**: P0-T06 acceptance criterion 1 rewritten to enumerate the
3 actual stages and explicitly note that integration tests need a
separate invocation. (See `docs/proposals/PHASE-PLAN-2026-05-01.md`
post this commit.)

### MEDIUM #2 — stale CI-guard hit count in audit row 9

**Finding**: Row 9 said "158 allowlisted hits". After Session 14's
own additions to `AUDIT_2026-04-30.md` (this S13.2 block) and
`PHASE-PLAN-2026-05-01.md` (P0-T06/T07), the real count is 164.

**Fix**: Row 9 updated to "164 allowlisted hits, 0 violations
(158 pre-S13.2 + 6 from S13.2 audit/phase-plan additions)".

### Architect's two non-blocking observations (recorded, not fixed)

1. **Sequencing of P0-T07 (devnet ↔ testnet replay)**: architect
   notes that closing chain-ID drift first and replay-domain
   second is acceptable ONLY if public testnet exposure is gated
   on P0-T07 landing. P0-T07's existing `Why` block already states
   this; no edit needed, but flagging here so a future reader does
   not "launch testnet first, fix replay later".

2. **Replay-mitigation choice (Option A vs Option B)**: architect
   notes Option A (per-network 32-byte signing-domain salt) is

### Lessons added (extends Session 14 lessons block above)

6. **API breaks need a 2-pass `rg` sweep**: when changing a public
   function signature in a crate, run `rg -n 'fn_name\s*\(' --type rust`
   AND `rg -n 'self\.field_name\.fn_name' --type rust` to catch
   both direct calls and method-on-field calls. The Phase 3 closure
   passed the first sweep but not the second.
7. **`ci-check.sh --lib` is a smoke gate, not a full gate**:
   integration tests (and especially RocksDB-backed tests) need a
   dedicated full `cargo test` stage on the VPS. P0-T06 now
   reflects this distinction.

### Lesson #8 (extends Session 14 lessons)

8. **CI guards must match the language ecosystems they protect**: a Rust-only
   `\b…\b` regex is insufficient when the same forbidden literal can appear
   in TS (`7878n` BigInt), JS (`7878n`), Rust (`7878u64`), and Solidity
   contexts. Either run per-language regexes OR build one super-regex that
   accounts for all known integer/BigInt suffix forms. The latter is what
   S13.2-B finding #2 implements.

SESSION 14 — S13.2-C: Pre-VPS YELLOW → GREEN conversion (E + G)

## Trigger
Pre-VPS audit (S13.2-B) ended with 11 GREEN dimensions and 2 YELLOWs:
  - **E**: 87 orphan `.rs` files at `crates/<x>/src/*.rs` not declared via `mod`
    in their crate's `lib.rs`/`main.rs`. Cargo silently ignores them; risk =
    bitrot + "I thought that was wired" surprises.
  - **G**: `node/configs/devnet.toml` absent (testnet.toml + mainnet.toml
    present). Devnet had `config/devnet.toml` (alt preset) but not the
    operator-facing path used by `node/configs/`.

User directive: "convert both YELLOWs to GREEN before VPS deploy."

## Fix-G — `node/configs/devnet.toml` created

Mirrors `node/configs/testnet.toml` structure with devnet-appropriate values:
  - `chain_id = 8990` (rides testnet preset, per `Network` enum constraint)
  - `data_dir = "./data/devnet"` (local, not `/var/lib/`)
  - `is_validator = true`, `validator_key = "./data/devnet/validator.key"`
  - Ports: 30303 (p2p), 8545 (http), 8546 (ws), 9100 (metrics) — distinct
    from testnet's 30304/18545/18546/9001 to avoid collision
  - `bind_addr = "0.0.0.0"` (vs testnet's 127.0.0.1 + reverse proxy)
  - `rate_limit_rpm = 0` (unlimited), `nat = "none"`, `bootnodes = []`
  - WS enabled (testnet defers to v0.3)
  - Header comment links back to `CHAIN_ID_TESTNET` invariant.

## Fix-E — Orphan archival (87 files moved)

Strategy chosen after sampling 4 orphan files:
  - `zbx-types/native_addr.rs` (94 LOC) — clean
  - `zbx-types/zbx_nft.rs` (50 LOC) — has `u256_stub = u128` placeholder
  - `zbx-metrics/registry.rs` (16 LOC) — needs `lazy_static` + `prometheus`
  - `zbx-consensus/finality.rs` (34 LOC) — clean

Mixed compile-cleanness. Blind `pub mod x;` wiring of all 87 in a sandbox
that cannot build (RocksDB SIGKILL) = cascade-fail risk. Honest GREEN
path: **archive, don't wire, don't delete.**

Action:
  1. For each crate, computed declared mods via regex over `lib.rs`/`main.rs`.
  2. Created `crates/<crate>/src/_archive/` per affected crate (27 dirs).
  3. Moved every orphan top-level `.rs` into its crate's `_archive/` subdir.
  4. Wrote `crates/_ARCHIVE_MANIFEST.md` (121 lines) documenting:
     - Why archive (cargo ignores subdir files unless `mod`-declared → zero
       compile risk + dependency-pull guaranteed zero)
     - 3-step lifecycle policy (rewire / delete / CI-enforce)
     - Full inventory table (crate × file × LOC)
     - Empty `## Removed` section for future deletion log

Per-crate orphan distribution moved:
  zbx-mempool 9, zbx-admin 8, zbx-consensus 8, zbx-staking 6, zbx-p2p 5,
  zbx-pool 5, zbx-sync 5, zbx-contracts 4, zbx-metrics 4, zbx-storage 4,
  zbx-bridge 3, zbx-network 3, zbx-state 3, zbx-evm 2, zbx-indexer 2,
  zbx-oracle 2, zbx-rpc 2, zbx-sequencer 2, zbx-types 2, plus 9 crates × 1.

## Permanent CI guard — `scripts/check-orphans.sh`

Bash + embedded Python. Walks `crates/<crate>/src/*.rs` (maxdepth 1),
parses `mod X` decls from `lib.rs`/`main.rs`, exits 1 if any undeclared
file found at top level, exits 0 if clean. Files inside `_archive/`
explicitly exempt (subdir = invisible to cargo unless mod-declared).

Flags: `--verbose` (list every checked file), `--help` (usage).
Exit codes: 0 clean, 1 orphans found, 2 bad invocation.
Error message instructs operator on the two valid resolutions
(wire-via-mod OR archive-with-manifest-row).

Permissions: `chmod +x` applied.

## Re-verification — both guards GREEN

| Guard                     | Result                                          |
|---------------------------|-------------------------------------------------|
| `check-orphans.sh`        | ✅ clean (309 files checked, 0 orphans)         |
| `check-orphans.sh --help` | ✅ usage prints, exit 0                          |
| `check-chain-id.sh`       | ✅ clean (175 allowlisted hits, 0 violations)    |

Tree audit:
  - 378 files at `crates/<x>/src/*.rs` (top-level, all mod-declared)
  - 87 files at `crates/<x>/src/_archive/*.rs` (intentional backlog)
  - 27 `_archive/` subdirs created
  - 469 total `.rs` files in `crates/`

## What this closes vs what stays open

**Closes**:
  - YELLOW E (orphan files) → GREEN: 0 orphans, formal archive policy,
    CI enforcement script.
  - YELLOW G (devnet.toml absent) → GREEN: `node/configs/devnet.toml`
    present, mirrors testnet.toml pattern, distinct ports, doc-linked
    to `CHAIN_ID_TESTNET` constant.

**Still pending (off-sandbox, unchanged from S13.2-B)**:
  - VPS srv1266996 actual cargo build + integration test pass
    (sandbox's RocksDB SIGKILL still blocks local verify).
  - P0-T07 (devnet ↔ testnet shared-chain-id 8990 replay-domain ZEP).

## Lessons

  - **Don't wire orphans you can't compile.** The honest GREEN move is to
    classify (archive + manifest), not pretend (blind `mod` add).
  - **Cargo subdir invisibility is a feature here.** `_archive/` is safe
    precisely because cargo treats subdir files as dead weight unless a
    parent `mod.rs` opts them in. We rely on that.
  - **Audit metrics need teeth.** "0 orphans" is meaningful only if a CI
    script enforces it on every commit. `check-orphans.sh` is that teeth.

## S13.2-D — Production-hardening Phase A: Version Registry foundation (2026-05-01)

**Scope.** First-half landing of the 8-point production-hardening spec
attached by the user. Pure-additive type work in `crates/zbx-types`
(no consumer rewires, no cross-crate refactors). Sandbox cannot
full-build the workspace (`zbx-storage` RocksDB linker SIGKILL on
2-vCPU), so the deliberate strategy is: *every new abstraction must
land + test inside `zbx-types` first*, then later sessions wire it
into the consumer crates one by one with `ci-check.sh --lib` smoke
gates.

### What landed

| # | Type | File | LOC | Tests | Lines |
|---|------|------|-----|-------|-------|
| 1 | `Activation` + `ActivationSchedule` | `crates/zbx-types/src/activation.rs` | 409 | 13 | block-height feature gating; `[a-z0-9_-]+` name policy; strict-sort RLP; reschedule allowed (governance); deny-by-default for unknown features |
| 2 | `Flag` + `FeatureFlags` | `crates/zbx-types/src/feature_flags.rs` | 407 | 14 | non-consensus boolean toggles; bool RLP encoded as `0x00`/`0x01` (decode rejects any other byte); flip-either-direction allowed; deny-by-default for unknown flags |
| 3 | `StorageVersion` + `Migration` trait + `MigrationContext` + `MigrationPlan` | `crates/zbx-types/src/storage_version.rs` | 455 | 13 | newtype `u32` with `GENESIS=0` sentinel; saturating `next()`; trait-based migration scaffold; `MigrationPlan::validate` enforces strict adjacency (no gaps, no overlaps, no no-ops); concrete adapter lives in `zbx-storage` (pending) |
| 4 | `RegistryUpgrade` + `VersionRegistry` | `crates/zbx-types/src/version_registry.rs` | 351 | 13 | composite single-source-of-truth bundling `ModuleVersions` + `ActivationSchedule` + `FeatureFlags` + `StorageVersion`; **atomic** `apply()` (clone-and-swap on full success only); 4-item RLP list with fixed field order; nested invariant validation on JSON decode |

**Total:** 1622 LOC, 53 new tests, plus the 13 pre-existing
`module_version` tests = **66 tests, 100% passing in 0.04s**.

### Discipline (matched verbatim from `module_version.rs`)

1. **Public type with `#[derive(Serialize)]` only** — manual
   `Deserialize` via private `…Raw` shape, every decode path runs
   the shared `validate_*_name` function. Genesis files / state
   imports cannot smuggle in invalid identifiers.
2. **`[a-z0-9_-]+` name policy** centralised in three sibling
   `validate_*_name` functions. Future audit script will assert
   they share one regex.
3. **Strict-sort canonical RLP** — collection types
   (`ActivationSchedule`, `FeatureFlags`) decode-reject unsorted or
   duplicate rows so wire-form equality implies semantic equality.
4. **`#[serde(transparent)]` map newtypes** — JSON shape stays the
   plain `BTreeMap` users expect.
5. **Tests cover** valid construction, invalid construction (every
   rejection path), Display stability, single-row RLP round-trip,
   single-row RLP rejection of every invariant, registry RLP
   round-trip, registry RLP rejection of every invariant, JSON
   round-trip, JSON rejection of every invariant. `VersionRegistry`
   additionally tests the atomic-on-failure semantics by injecting
   a partially-bad `RegistryUpgrade` and snapshot-comparing.

### What this closes (8-point spec ↔ landed work)

| Spec point | Status |
|---|---|
| 1. Version Registry | ✅ DONE (`VersionRegistry` + `ModuleVersions` already existed, now composed) |
| 2. Block-Activation | ✅ DONE (`ActivationSchedule`) |
| 3. Feature Flags | ✅ DONE (`FeatureFlags`) |
| 4. Storage Versioning + Migration | ✅ DONE (types/trait scaffold; concrete RocksDB adapter pending in `zbx-storage`) |
| 5. Module Decoupling (`zbx-traits`) | ⏸ DEFERRED — multi-session refactor; needs separate ZEP |
| 6. Error Handling (panic-audit sweep) | ⏸ DEFERRED — next session; will run `rg -n 'unwrap\(\)\|expect\(\|panic!' crates/` and produce a per-callsite triage |
| 7. Multi-node | ⏸ DEFERRED — VPS-dependent ZEP (testnet bootstrap, peer discovery) |
| 8. Testing | ⏸ DEFERRED — needs P0-T07 replay-domain ZEP first to know what cross-network tests to write |

### What stays open (this audit row alone is not done-done)

- `zbx-state` must persist a `VersionRegistry` at a well-known
  state-trie key. ETA: next session, after `zbx-traits` sketch.
- `zbx-storage` must implement `MigrationContext` against RocksDB
  with snapshot/rollback semantics (the orchestrator boundary
  documented in `storage_version.rs`).
- Governance proposal codec must learn `RegistryUpgrade` as a
  first-class proposal kind. Likely a new transaction sub-type in
  `zbx-tx`.
- Per-network signing-domain salt (P0-T07) needs to ship before any
  testnet↔devnet co-running can be proven safe.
- VPS srv1266996 verify still pending for S13/S13.2/S13.2-A/B/C
  (off-sandbox compile + integration tests).

### Lessons / invariants captured

8. **`#[derive(Default)]` on a struct cascades into every field.**
   Initial `version_registry.rs` failed to compile because
   `StorageVersion(pub u32)` lacked `Default`. Fix: add `Default`
   to the newtype (yields `StorageVersion::GENESIS == default()`,
   which is semantically correct). General rule: any type embedded
   in a `Default`-derived struct MUST itself be `Default`-able, OR
   the outer struct MUST hand-write `Default`.

9. **Atomic-on-failure helpers MUST clone-and-swap.** The naive
   `apply()` shape "mutate self field-by-field, return on first
   error" leaks half-applied state into the registry on failure —
   a serious problem for governance (a malformed proposal would
   half-apply and corrupt state). The clone-and-swap pattern in
   `VersionRegistry::apply` is the consensus-safe template; copy
   it for any future composite mutation helper (e.g. when
   `zbx-state` gains a similar bulk-update method).

10. **Boolean RLP MUST be canonical.** `bool → u8 → rlp(0x00|0x01)`
    is the only valid encoding; any other byte (e.g. `0x05`) MUST
    be rejected on decode. Without this, two semantically-equal
    flag registries could disagree on `keccak256(rlp(reg))`,
    breaking the "registry hash = upgrade fingerprint" property.
    Tested explicitly in `feature_flags::tests::rlp_decode_rejects_invalid_bool_byte`.

### Verification

- `cargo test -p zbx-types --lib` — **66 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored**
- `bash scripts/check-orphans.sh` — **clean (313 files, 0 orphans)**
  (was 309; +4 new files, all directly wired into `lib.rs`)
- `bash scripts/check-chain-id.sh` — **clean (175 allowlisted, 0 violations)**
- LOC sum: 1622 lines across 4 files, all extending the
  `module_version.rs` discipline template.

### Sandbox blockers

- Full-workspace `cargo build` still fails on the `zbx-storage`
  RocksDB linker step (SIGKILL on 2-vCPU). All consumer-side
  wireup (state persistence, governance codec, migrations
  orchestrator) MUST land via VPS-driven sessions or via
  per-crate `--lib` smoke gates.
- Architect review of S13.2-D was *not* invoked this turn — the
  changes are pure-additive types matching an already-reviewed
  discipline (`module_version.rs` was audited in S14-Phase 12).
  User may request `architect` if desired.

## S14 EXTENSION — Phase B Step 1: Governance type (`UpgradeProposal`)

**Date:** 2026-05-01
**Spec item closed:** 8/10-point production-hardening — item 3 (Upgrade
Governance) at the **type-and-codec layer**. Persistence wiring + tx-kind
codec + on-chain executor remain DEFERRED to follow-up sessions.

### What landed

| File | LOC | Tests | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| `crates/zbx-types/src/governance.rs` | 1029 | 21 | New module — see breakdown below |
| `crates/zbx-types/src/lib.rs` | (+5) | — | `pub mod governance;` + 6 type re-exports |

### Public type surface (re-exported from `zbx_types`)

- `ProposalId(u64)` — newtype wrapper, `Display`/`From<u64>`/RLP/serde.
- `Vote { Yes, No, Abstain }` — RLP via `to_u8` discriminant {0,1,2}.
- `ProposalStatus { Pending, Scheduled, Executed, Failed, Rejected }` — RLP via
  `to_u8` discriminant {0..4}.
- `VoteTally { yes, no, abstain }` — passive aggregate computed by
  `UpgradeProposal::tally()`.
- `UpgradeProposal { id, module_name, new_version, activation_block, proposer,
  votes: BTreeMap<Address, Vote>, status }` — full proposal record with
  `cast_vote` (mutates while `Pending`), `finalize(validator_set_size,
  current_block)` (simple-majority threshold `yes*2 > set_size`,
  `Pending → Scheduled` if `activation_block > current_block` else
  `Pending → Rejected`), `mark_executed`, `mark_failed`,
  `target_module_version()`.
- `ProposalRegistry(BTreeMap<ProposalId, UpgradeProposal>)` — keyed registry
  with `insert`/`remove`/`get`/`get_mut`/`iter`/`len`, deterministic RLP
  encoding (sorted by `ProposalId`), JSON nested-invariant validation on
  decode.

### Determinism / canonicalisation discipline (matches `module_version.rs`)

1. **BTreeMap throughout** — votes keyed by `Address` (derives `Ord`),
   registry keyed by `ProposalId` — guarantees a single canonical RLP byte
   sequence regardless of insertion order.
2. **Sorted-rows-monotone-strict** — both `votes` decoder and
   `ProposalRegistry` decoder verify each row's key is **strictly greater**
   than the previous (`prev: Option<_>` cursor pattern), refusing duplicates
   and re-orderings.
3. **Field-count gate** — `UpgradeProposal::decode` checks `item_count() != 7`
   and rejects with `RlpIncorrectListLen`; `votes-row` decoder checks
   `item_count() != 2`; `ProposalRegistry::decode` defers per-proposal
   field-count check to nested `UpgradeProposal::decode`.
4. **Status discriminant range-check** — both `Vote::decode` and
   `ProposalStatus::decode` reject `u8` values outside the enum range with
   `RlpInvalidIndirection`.

### Address handling

`Address([u8; 20])` does **not** derive `rlp::Encodable`/`Decodable`. Local
helpers `append_address(s, a)` and `decode_address(rlp)` emit/parse the
20-byte raw payload as a single RLP byte-string. They are **not exported**
(intentionally crate-private — Address-level RLP belongs in a future
`zbx-codec` crate, not as a free function in `governance.rs`).

### Test surface (21 tests, all green)

| Group | Count | Coverage |
|---|---|---|
| `ProposalId` | 2 | Display, From<u64>, RLP, serde |
| `Vote` | 3 | discriminant range, RLP round-trip, decode rejects invalid byte |
| `ProposalStatus` | 3 | same shape as Vote |
| `VoteTally` | 2 | empty proposal tallies to zero, mixed votes tally correctly |
| `UpgradeProposal` | 7 | constructor validates non-empty module name, `cast_vote` rejects after `finalize`, replace-vote semantics, `finalize` simple-majority/Scheduled/Rejected branches, `mark_executed`/`mark_failed` only from `Scheduled`, `target_module_version` |
| `ProposalRegistry` | 4 | insert/remove/get/iter, RLP round-trip with mixed votes (the test that exposed the double-count bug), JSON round-trip, decode rejects out-of-order proposals |

### Lessons learned

**LESSON #11 — RLP `Encodable` delegation MUST use `inner.rlp_append(s)`,
NOT `s.append(&inner)`.**

Initial `ProposalId`, `Vote`, `ProposalStatus` Encodable impls used the
delegating pattern `s.append(&self.0)` (where `self.0` is a u64 / u8 inner).
This **double-counted** against the parent `RlpStream`:

- `s.append(&value)` body:
  1. `self.finished_list = false`
  2. `value.rlp_append(self)` — for u8/u64 this calls `encode_value(...)`
     which emits bytes but does NOT touch `finished_list`
  3. `if !self.finished_list { self.note_appended(1); }` ← +1 to parent

- When the *outer* `s.append(&proposal_id)` calls `proposal_id.rlp_append(s)`
  which itself calls `s.append(&self.0)`:
  - inner s.append → emits u64 bytes + `note_appended(1)` for parent
  - returns to `proposal_id.rlp_append` (now `finished_list = false`)
  - returns to outer `s.append(&proposal_id)`
  - outer's tail: `if !self.finished_list { note_appended(1) }` → +1 AGAIN

Net effect: every `ProposalId`/`Vote`/`ProposalStatus` field was counted as
**2 items** in its parent list. With `UpgradeProposal::rlp_append` declaring
`begin_list(7)`, the parent would close after only ~3.5 logical fields, and
trailing fields would leak into the registry's outer list. Symptom:
`ProposalRegistry::decode` saw `outer.item_count() == 2` and `row[0]` had
6 items not 7, returning `RlpIncorrectListLen`.

**Fix pattern (now codified across all 3 newtypes):**

```rust
impl Encodable for ProposalId {
    fn rlp_append(&self, s: &mut RlpStream) {
        // Direct delegation — `s.append(&self.0)` would double-count the
        // inner `note_appended(1)` against the outer parent list.
        self.0.rlp_append(s);
    }
}
```

The diagnostic that pinpointed this was a throwaway `debug_tests` module
that called `encode(&reg)` then walked the byte stream with
`Rlp::new(&bytes)` and printed `item_count()` per row. The signature
"declared N, got N-1, sibling appears as outer item" is the canonical
double-count fingerprint and is now in the lessons file.

**LESSON #12 — Free helpers like `append_address` / `decode_address` should
stay crate-private until at least 2 crates need them.** A future `zbx-codec`
crate is the right home, not a free function in `governance.rs`. Avoided
exporting these from `zbx_types` to prevent locking in the wrong API.

### What this does NOT close (still DEFERRED)

| Spec item | Status | Where it goes |
|---|---|---|
| Persist `ProposalRegistry` at well-known state-trie key | TODO | `zbx-state` follow-up |
| `RegistryUpgrade` as first-class tx kind (`TxKind::Governance`) | TODO | `zbx-tx` follow-up |
| On-chain executor (`finalize` driven by block-progression hook) | TODO | `zbx-consensus` follow-up |
| `mark_executed` driven by actual `MigrationContext::run_plan` success | TODO | `zbx-storage` follow-up + S14-Ext-A `MigrationPlan` integration |
| Validator-set source for `finalize(validator_set_size, ...)` | TODO | wired from `zbx-consensus` epoch state |
| Quorum/threshold parameter (currently hard-coded simple-majority) | TODO | governance config knob |
| Vote-weight by stake (currently 1-validator-1-vote) | TODO | requires stake-table integration |
| Slashing for double-vote (currently `replace` semantics) | OPEN QUESTION | governance ZEP |
| Architect review of governance.rs | NOT REQUESTED | user may invoke |

- `scripts/check-orphans.sh` — **clean (314 files, 0 orphans)** (+1 vs Phase A: governance.rs)
- `scripts/check-chain-id.sh` — **clean (175 allowlisted hits, 0 violations)** (unchanged)

## S15 — Production-hardening Phase 1 batch 2 (10-point security cleanup)

Context: P0 (proposer reorg + finality), P1 (peer eclipse), P2 (DeFi
TWAP), P3 (DeFi reentrancy guard) and P10 (centralised event registry)
were the five remaining gaps from the 10-point security review that had
no canonical types in `zbx-types`. The other five (P4-P9: VM host-policy,
mempool DoS bounds, oracle staleness, slashing evidence, Solidity
reentrancy) had already landed in earlier S15 batches. This block closes
the rest as **pure-additive** types in `zbx-types` so the existing
crates can adopt them without any cross-crate churn.

| Item | Module | Tests | LOC |

| 2 | `crates/zbx-types/src/proposer.rs` | 12 | ~310 |
| 2-sub | `crates/zbx-types/src/finality.rs` | 14 | ~330 |
| 3 | `crates/zbx-types/src/network.rs` | 15 | ~360 |
| 4+5 | `crates/zbx-types/src/defi.rs` | 16 | ~480 |
| 10 | `crates/zbx-types/src/events.rs` | 15 | ~430 |
| **Total** | | **72** | **~1910** |

matching `pub use` re-exports. No other crate modified — this batch is
strictly additive.

### Verification (sandbox, off-VPS)

- `cargo test -p zbx-types --lib` — **313 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored** in 0.17s
- `scripts/check-orphans.sh` — **clean (326 files, 0 orphans)** (+12 vs Phase B: 5 new modules + their referenced helpers)
- `scripts/check-chain-id.sh` — **clean (175 allowlisted hits, 0 violations)** (unchanged — none of these modules embed chain-id literals)

### Closes vs stays open

| Item | Concern (10-point review) | Closes | Stays open |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | Proposer reorg-resistance lacked typed seed/algorithm + finality status | Canonical `ProposerSeed`/`ProposerAlgorithm`/`Block-Proposer` types + `FinalityStatus`/`Vote`/`Checkpoint` with strict 2/3+1 quorum via `ValidatorSet::is_quorum`. | Wire `Checkpoint` into `zbx-consensus` block-progression hook; persist at well-known state-trie key in `zbx-state`. |
| 3 | Eclipse-resistance — no peer-quality scoring | `PeerId` (libp2p-style hash), `PeerScore` (i32), `PeerEvent`/`PeerScoringPolicy`/`PeerRecord`/`PeerBook`. Default policy reproduces the rule set in `docs/proposals/PEER-SCORING.md`. | Replace ad-hoc `HashMap<PeerId, i32>` in `zbx-network` with `PeerBook`; wire eviction into `swarm.rs`. |
| 4 | DeFi TWAP missing | `TwapWindow` (sliding-window per (token0,token1) pair, capacity-bound), `PriceObservation`, `compute()` returning `U256` average. Strict-monotonic timestamp + cumulative-price invariants enforced on `record()`. | Adopt in `zbx-defi`'s AMM crate; expose RPC `zbx_getTwap`. |
| 5 | DeFi reentrancy lacked typed guard | `ReentrancyMap` + `ReentrancyState` with per-contract `enter`/`exit` semantics. Independent state per contract address. | Adopt in `zbx-evm` host-context to back the existing Solidity `nonReentrant` modifier on the chain side. |
| 10 | No canonical chain-event registry | `EventDomain` (10 domains), `EventSeverity` (info/warn/critical), `EventDescriptor` (id, name, domain, severity, schema_version), `EventRegistry::installed_default()` returning the canonical 38-event catalogue. | Each crate that emits events should look up its `EventDescriptor` from the singleton registry instead of hard-coding strings. |

### Lessons added this batch

#### LESSON #11 (strict form)

`Encodable` impl on a newtype wrapper **must** use direct delegation
`self.inner.rlp_append(s)`, never `s.append(&self.inner)`. The latter
double-counts because the outer `s.append` runs the post-call
`note_appended(1)` branch after the inner already incremented its
parent's count. Symptom: `RlpIncorrectListLen` or `explicit panic` at
`stream.rs:293`. Confirmed in this batch on `PeerId`, `PeerScore`,
`PeerEvent`, `FinalityStatus`, `ProposerSeed`, `ProposerAlgorithm`,
`EventId`.

#### LESSON #13

Inside `s.begin_list(N)`, **every** field must go through `s.append(&x)`
to advance the parent's slot counter. Writing raw bytes via
`slice.rlp_append(s)` directly bypasses `note_appended`, so the parent
list never closes (or worse, stream silently malforms). When the field
is a non-Encodable primitive like `Address` or `U256`, wrap it in a
crate-local helper (`append_address`/`append_u256`) that calls
`s.append(&bytes)` on the byte slice — this counts as one slot AND
emits a properly-tagged RLP item.

Symptom that caught this in `defi.rs::TwapWindow`: 14/16 tests passed,
but `twap_rlp_round_trip` and `reentrancy_rlp_round_trip` panicked at
`stream.rs:293` with "explicit panic" because the address slots wrote
bytes without incrementing the `begin_list(5)` slot counter.

#### LESSON #14

`U256` from `primitive_types` has **no `rlp::Encodable` impl** in the
version pinned by the workspace catalog. Serialise as 32-byte
big-endian (canonical Ethereum form), pad-front on decode. `i32` is
likewise unsupported — bit-cast via `as u32` round-trip.

### What's deferred (next sessions / VPS / ZEPs)

- Wire `Checkpoint` into `zbx-consensus` block-progression hook
- Persist `Checkpoint` and `ProposerSeed` history in `zbx-state`
- Replace `zbx-network`'s ad-hoc peer scoring with `PeerBook`
- Adopt `TwapWindow` inside `zbx-defi` AMM and expose `zbx_getTwap` RPC
- Adopt `ReentrancyMap` inside `zbx-evm` host context
- Migrate per-crate event strings to `EventRegistry::by_name(...)` lookups
- VPS srv1266996 verify (off-sandbox compile + integration tests)
- P0-T07 devnet ↔ testnet replay-domain ZEP

### Architect review

Pending — pure-additive batch matching the Phase A / Phase B discipline
that was already audited. The five new modules (proposer, finality,
network, defi, events) are leaf additions to `zbx-types` with no
external callers yet. Architect review will be invoked after Phase 2

real adoption surface, not just type definitions.

### Tarball

`exports/zbx-chain-2026-05-01-S14-S13.2D.tar.gz`. User may request
`zbx-chain-2026-05-01-S15-P1B2.tar.gz` to ship the 10-point batch 2 to

## S15-P2 — ZUSD redemption re-enabled (S6-V2-FIXED)

**Status:** Implementation complete (testnet-grade). VPS forge verify pending. Architect review pending.

### Scope

Re-enables `ZusdVault.redeem()` (previously stub-and-revert per S6-V2 P1)
with a hint-based, monotonicity-checked design that ATOMICALLY updates
per-CDP `collateral` and `debt` records — the exact fix for the original
vault-drain bug.

### Files touched

| File | Change | LOC |
|---|---|---|
| `contracts/ZusdVault.sol` | Replaced stub + added admin functions + 4 state vars + 4 events | +220 / -24 |
| `contracts/interfaces/IZusdVault.sol` | New `redeem` signature + admin getters/setters + 4 events | +29 / -2 |
| `contracts/test/ZusdVaultRedemption.t.sol` | NEW — 12 tests with mock ZBX/oracle + 4 borrower forwarders | +387 |
| `docs/proposals/ZEP-005-ZUSD-REDEMPTION.md` | NEW — full design + safety invariants + mainnet checklist | +280 |
| `docs/ZUSD.md` | Removed stale "wallet-aware" liquidation section + added Redemption section | +63 / -28 |

### What this closes (vs what stays open)

| Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| S6-V2 stub-and-revert behaviour | ✅ CLOSED | `redeem()` now functional |
| S6-V2 vault-drain accounting bug | ✅ CLOSED | Per-CDP atomic update, see line 492-498 of ZusdVault.sol |
| `docs/ZUSD.md` doc/code mismatch (wallet-aware) | ✅ CLOSED | Section removed, vault v0.2 reconciled |
| Lower-bound peg defence | ✅ CLOSED | Redemption arbitrage now possible |
| Sorted-CDP linked list | ⚠️ DEFERRED | Hint-based instead; mainnet may upgrade |
| TWAP / multi-source oracle | ⚠️ DEFERRED | Single-source spot price; separate ZEP |
| Foundry invariant tests | ⚠️ DEFERRED | Unit tests cover scenarios; invariants next session |
| External audit | ⚠️ DEFERRED | Mandatory pre-mainnet, not pre-testnet |
| VPS `forge test` verification | ⚠️ DEFERRED | Sandbox cannot compile Solidity → user runs on VPS |
| Architect review | ⚠️ DEFERRED | Recommended before testnet deploy |

### Safety properties enforced on-chain

| # | Invariant | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|
| I1 | Vault solvency: `Σ cdp.collateral + leftover_returns == ZBX_in_vault` | All paths update `totalCollateral` symmetrically |
| I2 | Lowest-CR-first | `require(crBps >= prevCRBps)` in loop |
| I3 | Healthy-only | `require(crBps >= LIQUIDATION_RATIO)` per CDP |
| I4 | Per-CDP CR-monotone | Algebra: when CR ≥ 100% pre, CR-after ≥ CR-before |
| I5 | No dust CDPs | Cap `redeemFromCdp` to leave ≥ MIN_ZUSD_MINT or fully close |
| I6 | Reentrancy-safe | `nonReentrant` modifier (pre-existing) |
| I7 | Admin-only controls | `require(msg.sender == owner)` on setters |
| I8 | Spam-resistant | `MIN_REDEEM_AMOUNT = 10 ZUSD`, 0.5% fee |
| I9 | Gas-bounded | `MAX_REDEEM_ITER = 50` per call |
| I10 | Emergency-stoppable | `redemptionPaused` switch |

### LESSON #15 — disabled-and-honest is a temporary state, not a final one

The S6-V2 audit decision to revert in-body (rather than deploy broken code)
was correct — but it left ZUSD without a peg floor. A stablecoin without
redemption is a half-built bridge: structurally sound but missing a critical
load path. Re-enabling MUST be planned at the same time as disabling, and
the PR/ZEP that disables must include a deadline or follow-up issue
referencing the re-enable plan. We added this to `PHASE-PLAN-2026-05-01.md`
follow-ups.

### LESSON #16 — hint-based vs sorted-list is a real engineering choice, not a corner-cut

Liquity ships BOTH (a sorted-troves contract AND off-chain hints + on-chain
re-validation). For testnet, hint-based ALONE is acceptable because:
- The cost of bad hints = caller pays gas + reverts. Self-correcting.
- The cost of front-running a fair redemption order = bounded (next caller
  picks up the still-low-CR CDP and earns the same arbitrage).
- The on-chain monotonicity check makes "skip a low-CR CDP and target a
  healthier one" impossible.
The full sorted-list addition is an upgrade path, not a correctness gap.

### LESSON #17 — Solidity testing without forge in the sandbox

The sandbox has no `forge` binary (only Rust toolchain, hobbled by RocksDB
SIGKILL). Solidity changes therefore CANNOT be compile-tested locally. The
only safe mitigation is:
1. Read EVERY existing function in the file before editing (storage layout,
   modifiers, naming conventions).
2. Use only stable Solidity (^0.8.24) features — no exotic syntax.
3. Validate by mental compile-pass — pay extra attention to type widths,
   storage vs memory, payable-ness, override modifiers.
4. Hand all changes to VPS `forge test` before considering them verified.
5. Architect review BEFORE VPS push, not after.

### Re-run guards (post-edit)

```bash
scripts/check-orphans.sh   → 326/0  (unchanged — no new Rust files)
scripts/check-chain-id.sh  → 175/0  (unchanged — only Solidity touched)
```

1. **Architect review:** `await architect({task: "review S15-P2 ZUSD redemption re-enable", relevantFiles: ["zbx-chain/contracts/ZusdVault.sol", "zbx-chain/contracts/interfaces/IZusdVault.sol", "zbx-chain/contracts/test/ZusdVaultRedemption.t.sol", "zbx-chain/docs/proposals/ZEP-005-ZUSD-REDEMPTION.md"], includeGitDiff: true})`.
2. **VPS verify:** `forge test --match-contract ZusdVaultRedemptionTest -vvv` on srv1266996.
3. **Devnet deploy** with `redemptionPaused = true` initially; flip to `false` after smoke tests.
4. **Testnet deploy** only after ≥ 1 week clean devnet operation.
5. **Mainnet:** external audit + Foundry invariant tests + bug bounty + TWAP oracle (separate ZEPs) — DO NOT enable before all four.

### Findings + resolutions

| # | Sev | Finding | Resolution |

| M1 | MEDIUM | "Lowest-CR-first" was overstated — on-chain monotonicity check enforces ascending CR ONLY within caller-supplied hints; caller can OMIT a globally-lower-CR CDP and target a higher-CR one instead. | (a) Reframed docstring in `ZusdVault.sol` redeem (lines 372-400) as "ascending-CR WITHIN supplied hints" with explicit FAIRNESS DISCLOSURE block enumerating what the chain DOES vs DOES NOT guarantee. (b) Updated ZEP-005 §6 invariant table I2 + §7 limitations table row 1 with full mitigation breakdown. (c) Updated `docs/ZUSD.md` Safety table row to match. (d) Added test #15 `testOmittedLowerCrCdpStillRedeemable` asserting the documented behaviour so any future change to globally-lowest-first enforcement is caught. | M2 | MEDIUM | `totalDebt` and `totalCollateral` used clamp-to-zero saturating subtraction (`x > y ? x - y : 0`) which silently MASKS invariant violations under fee-accrual lag, where strict subtraction would let Solidity 0.8.24 auto-revert on impossible underflow. | Replaced 3 clamp sites with strict subtraction + explicit `require(x >= y, "...invariant broken")` preceding the subtraction. Added test #13 `testTotalDebtStrictInvariant` asserting `totalDebt == sum(cdp.debt)` in the no-fee-accrual case. |
| Info | LOW | Architect requested explicit test for sub-min debt reachable via `repay()` (not via fee accrual, which only grows debt). | Added test #14 `testSubMinDebtFromRepayThenRedeem` — opens CDP, repays down to 50 ZUSD (sub-min), verifies partial redemption REVERTS (would create dust) but full redemption succeeds. |
