### LESSON #18 — clamp-to-zero is a SILENCE on a SCREAM

Saturating subtraction (`x > y ? x - y : 0`) on accounting counters is
ALWAYS the wrong choice when the underlying type already auto-reverts on
underflow (Solidity 0.8+, Rust `u256` checked_sub). It converts an
invariant violation (loud, halts the tx, observable in logs) into silent
state corruption (counter at 0, system continues, vault drifts further).
The justification "defensive against an edge case" is the exact red flag:
if the edge case is impossible, the require/revert is free safety; if it
IS possible, the clamp HIDES the bug instead of catching it.

### LESSON #19 — "lowest-CR-first" is a strong claim that needs strong enforcement

Documenting a FAIRNESS PROPERTY in a stablecoin's design doc creates an
expectation that users/auditors/integrators will rely on. If the on-chain
mechanism only enforces a WEAKER property (here: ascending-WITHIN-hints
instead of globally-lowest-first), the docs MUST say so explicitly, and
the gap MUST be plumbed back as a mainnet blocker. "The SDK does it" is
NOT enforcement; it is a default that adversaries can ignore. We added the
gap to the limitations table with **REQUIRED** mainnet status.

## S15-P2-B — clamp-to-zero sweep + stale liquidate() doc fix

**Trigger:** While verifying S15-P2-A guards, a `grep "totalDebt > "` sweep
revealed the **same M2 anti-pattern** (clamp-to-zero saturating subtraction)
in TWO sites OUTSIDE the redeem() path that S15-P2-A had already fixed:

| Location | Function | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `ZusdVault.sol:232` | `repay()` | `totalDebt = totalDebt > repayAmount ? totalDebt - repayAmount : 0` |
| `ZusdVault.sol:276` | `closeCDP()` | `totalDebt = totalDebt > fullDebt ? totalDebt - fullDebt : 0` |

These were pre-existing (predate S15-P2). Architect's M2 finding was
phrased about the redemption code, but the CLASS of bug applies vault-wide
and was caught by the post-fix sweep. Per "full secure" mandate, both
fixed the same way: explicit `require(totalDebt >= x, "...invariant
broken")` + strict subtraction.

Also caught: `liquidate()` had a stale `@dev` doc-block (lines 291-298)
that still described the REMOVED wallet-aware liquidation protection as
if it were active behaviour. The actual function body (lines 299-347)
correctly reflects v0.2 (collateral-ratio-only gating; wallet-aware
removed in v0.2 per the in-body NOTE at line 311-316). Doc-block
rewritten to describe the v0.2 rule and reference the in-body NOTE for
the rationale.

### Files touched

| File | Change | Lines |
|---|---|---|
| `contracts/ZusdVault.sol` | repay() clamp → strict | 230-237 |
| `contracts/ZusdVault.sol` | closeCDP() clamp → strict | 274-279 |
| `contracts/ZusdVault.sol` | liquidate() @dev doc-block stale → reconciled | 286-300 |

### LESSON #20 — fix the bug class, not the bug instance

When an architect-review flags an anti-pattern (here: clamp-to-zero on
accounting counters), don't just patch the cited site. `grep` the entire
codebase for the pattern and patch every instance. The cited site is a
representative; same author, same class of mistake, same time-of-write.
S15-P2-B was a 5-minute sweep that caught 2 more critical instances —
and would have left the vault genuinely insecure if shipped without it.

### LESSON #21 — when removing behaviour, scrub the docs in the SAME PR

The wallet-aware liquidation removal (vault v0.2) correctly updated the
function body AND added a NOTE explaining the change — but left the
@dev doc-block above the function describing the removed behaviour as
active. Auditors / integrators read the @dev FIRST. Removed code that
remains documented is more dangerous than removed code that is silently
gone — it creates a false expectation of behaviour that no longer exists.
Doc-block updates MUST be part of the same diff that removes/changes
behaviour, never deferred.

## S15-P2-C — full-sweep findings (post-S15-P2-B verification)

**Trigger:** User asked for a "full check kya koi missing to nahi" (full
check whether anything is missing) before declaring S15-P2 family DONE.
Codebase-wide sweep across ALL Solidity + ALL ZUSD-related docs found 4
findings; 3 fixed this turn, 1 noted as observed-deferred (out of S15-P2
scope).

### Findings + dispositions

| # | Severity | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | LOW (latent M2 instance) | `ZusdVault.sol:517` clamp `postDebt = cdpDebt > redeemFromCdp ? cdpDebt - redeemFromCdp : 0` in the defensive `zbxFromCdp > cdp.collateral` cap branch. Algebraic analysis shows redeemFromCdp can only DECREASE from its original `min(remaining, cdpDebt)`, so `cdpDebt >= redeemFromCdp` always holds — clamp is redundant. | **FIXED** — replaced with strict `cdpDebt - redeemFromCdp` + comment explaining the algebra. Sol 0.8 auto-reverts if analysis ever wrong. |
| B | MEDIUM (doc-vs-code drift) | `docs/proposals/ZEP-002-ZUSD.md` Status = `DEPLOYED` but describes wallet-aware liquidation (lines 29, 35-46) which was REMOVED in vault v0.2. Auditors / integrators reading ZEP-002 first would see contradictory behaviour. | **FIXED** — added prominent SUPERSEDED banner at top, struck-through "Wallet-Aware" in parameters table with link, kept original §"Wallet-Aware..." section as historical with bold REMOVED disclaimer + reason for removal. Status field augmented with "(partially superseded — see banner above)" + "Superseded by (partial)" row. |
| C | MEDIUM (M1 follow-through) | `ZEP-005-ZUSD-REDEMPTION.md` lines 67 + 166 still contained unqualified "lowest-CR-first" mentions that the M1 fix had reframed elsewhere — these slipped past the §6 / §7 update. | **FIXED** — both mentions reframed to "ascending-CR within hints (best-effort lowest-first via off-chain SDK; see §7)" with explicit pointer to limitation 1. |
| D | INFORMATIONAL (out of S15-P2 scope) | `contracts/RewardPool.sol:2` uses `pragma solidity ^0.8.20` while ALL other contracts use `^0.8.24`. Both compile, both safe — no functional risk. Pre-existing inconsistency in unrelated contract. | **OBSERVED-DEFERRED** — separate housekeeping item; not blocking S15-P2 testnet deploy. Tracked for next maintenance pass. |

### Sweep methodology (for future audits)

For any "is anything missing" verification on a Solidity surface:

```bash
# 1. Anti-pattern sweep (vault-wide)
rg -n '> [a-zA-Z_]+ \? .* - .* : 0' contracts/      # clamp-to-zero

# 2. Removed-behaviour orphan reference sweep
rg -ni '<removed-behaviour-keyword>' contracts/ docs/

# 3. Strong-claim consistency sweep (for any documented invariant)
rg -ni '<claim-phrase>' contracts/ docs/

# 4. Disabled/stub orphan sweep
rg -ni 'disabled|stub|revert.*<contract-name>' contracts/

# 5. Interface ↔ implementation parity
diff <(grep -E 'event ' contracts/<I>.sol) <(grep -E 'event ' contracts/<X>.sol)
diff <(grep -E 'function ' contracts/<I>.sol) <(grep -E 'function ' contracts/<X>.sol)

# 6. Constants consistency (interface, impl, ZEPs, user docs)
rg -nE 'MIN_|MAX_|RATIO|FEE|BPS' contracts/ docs/

# 7. Pragma uniformity
grep -rn '^pragma solidity' contracts/ | sort -u -k2

# 8. TODO/FIXME orphan check
rg -n 'TODO|FIXME|XXX|HACK' contracts/ docs/
```

### Interface ↔ implementation parity (verified clean)

- `IZusdVault.sol` and `ZusdVault.sol` declare **11 events** each — perfect parity.
- All 11 interface functions have matching public/external implementations.
- All 4 new constants (MIN_REDEEM_AMOUNT, MAX_REDEEM_ITER) + state vars (redemptionPaused, feeRecipient, totalRedeemed, totalRedemptionFees) consistently referenced across `ZusdVault.sol` ↔ `ZEP-005` ↔ `ZUSD.md`.
- Test file (15 test functions, 607 lines) imports `ZUSD.sol` + `ZusdVault.sol` only — no missing dependencies.
- ZusdVault.sol braces balanced (38 open / 38 close), 21 function declarations, 9 nonReentrant guards.

### LESSON #22 — verification pass NEEDS its own checklist, not an ad-hoc grep

User's "full check kya koi missing to nahi" prompt forced a structured
sweep that found 3 real issues a casual grep would have missed (especially
finding C: 2 leftover "lowest-CR-first" mentions in the SAME document
that already had the M1 reframing applied to other sections). The 8-step
methodology above should be the PERMANENT verification checklist for any
Solidity surface change, codified into `scripts/check-sol-surface.sh` in
a future session.

### LESSON #23 — superseded proposals deserve a banner, not deletion

ZEP-002 Status was "DEPLOYED" — technically correct (the contracts are
live) but misleading because the live contracts no longer match the
proposal text. The right disposition for a deployed-but-evolved proposal
is: keep the historical text, add a prominent superseded-by banner, and
inline-mark the changed sections. Deleting would lose the audit trail of
WHY the design changed; silently leaving stale text would mislead future
auditors. Banner + inline disclaimers preserve both honesty and history.

## S15-P2-D — totalDebt principal-delta accounting fix (architect re-review FAIL → PASS)

**Trigger:** User accepted the offered "optional" architect re-review on the
full S15-P2/A/B/C state. Architect returned **FAIL** with one CRITICAL
regression introduced by my own S15-P2-B sweep, plus one minor doc nit.

### Critical finding (regression introduced by S15-P2-B)

> "`totalDebt` is treated as a hard invariant in strict guards, but fee
> accrual increases per-CDP payable debt without increasing `totalDebt`."

**Root cause:** Three independent but interrelated facts the previous
sweeps glossed over:

1. `_accrueStabilityFee()` only bumps the global `feeIndex` (line 661).
   It does NOT update any `cdp.debt` field and does NOT update `totalDebt`.
2. `_currentDebt(user)` returns `cdp.debt × (feeIndex / cdp.lastFeeIndex)` —
   per-CDP debt growth is implicit until next touch.
3. `totalDebt` was always the sum of `cdp.debt` PRINCIPAL SNAPSHOTS — not
   current-debt sum.

So when repay/close/redeem/liquidate did `totalDebt -= currentDebt-units`,
they over-decremented by exactly the accrued fee amount. The pre-existing
clamp-to-zero (`totalDebt = totalDebt > x ? totalDebt - x : 0`) MASKED
this — it was actually a (wrong-but-defensive) workaround for the
accounting mismatch. My S15-P2-B sweep replaced the clamp with strict
`require(totalDebt >= x)`, which exposed the underlying bug as false
reverts on legitimate operations after time had elapsed.

**Worst-case impact pre-fix:** any borrower trying to repay/close/be-liquidated
after non-zero elapsed time would see their tx revert with "Vault:
totalDebt invariant broken" — effectively bricking the vault from the
moment any fee accrued.

### The fix (4 functions touched)

Capture `oldPrincipal = cdp.debt` BEFORE the assignment, then adjust
`totalDebt` by the **principal** delta `(oldPrincipal - newPrincipal)`,
not by `currentDebt`-units:

| Function | Before (S15-P2-B) | After (S15-P2-D) |
|---|---|---|
| `repay()` | `require(totalDebt >= repayAmount); totalDebt -= repayAmount;` | Capture `oldPrincipal = cdp.debt`. Compute `newPrincipal = currentDebt - repayAmount`. If `newPrincipal >= oldPrincipal` (user paid less than accrued fee — principal grew), `totalDebt += diff`; else `require(totalDebt >= reduction); totalDebt -= reduction`. |
| `closeCDP()` | `require(totalDebt >= fullDebt); totalDebt -= fullDebt;` | `totalDebt -= oldPrincipal` with strict guard. (User still BURNS `fullDebt` ZUSD — paying accrued fee — but `totalDebt` only ever tracked principal.) |
| `liquidate()` | `totalDebt -= currentDebt;` (Sol-0.8 default revert) | Capture `oldPrincipal`. `require(totalDebt >= oldPrincipal); totalDebt -= oldPrincipal;` |
| `redeem()` (per-CDP loop) | `require(totalDebt >= redeemFromCdp); totalDebt -= redeemFromCdp;` | Capture `oldPrincipal` at top of loop iteration. After atomic per-CDP update: signed-delta branch — `totalDebt += (postDebt - oldPrincipal)` if post > old, else strict require + sub. |

**Note:** liquidate() was ALSO buggy pre-S15-P2 (using Sol-0.8 default
underflow revert instead of clamp), so the architect's finding actually
caught a pre-existing latent bug too. Now uniformly fixed across all 4
mutating functions.

### Tests added (4 new — total now 19)

All use the standard HEVM cheatcode address (`keccak256("hevm cheat
code")` truncated to 20 bytes) for `vm.warp` — no forge-std import
required, no impact on the other 15 tests.

| # | Test | What it asserts |
|---|---|---|
| 16 | `testRepayAfterFeeAccrual` | After 365d warp, repay(50) doesn't revert; `totalDebt == sum(cdp.debt)`. |
| 17 | `testCloseCdpAfterFeeAccrual` | After 365d warp, closeCDP() doesn't revert; `totalDebt` decreases by exactly oldPrincipal. |
| 18 | `testRedeemAfterFeeAccrual` | After 365d warp, redeem(50) doesn't revert; `totalDebt == sum(cdp.debt)`. |
| 19 | `testLiquidateAfterFeeAccrual` | After 365d warp + price drop to $0.40, liquidate() doesn't revert; `totalDebt` decreases by exactly oldPrincipal. |

Each warps `block.timestamp += 365 days` (≈2% APY at the configured
`STABILITY_FEE_PER_SEC`) so currentDebt ≈ 1.02 × principal — large enough
that the bug would manifest pre-fix but small enough that arithmetic is
sanity-checkable.

### Doc finding (minor, also fixed)

`docs/ZUSD.md:108-110` said "processes lowest-CR CDPs first" without
qualifying "within supplied hints" — same skimmable ambiguity that M1
and S15-P2-C addressed elsewhere. Now reframed with explicit ZEP-005 §7
limitation 1 link, matching the ZEP-005 / vault-docstring wording.

### Updated invariants (vault-wide, post-S15-P2-D)

```bash
# 1. Zero clamp-to-zero patterns
rg "> [a-zA-Z_]+ \? .* - .* : 0" contracts/ZusdVault.sol → 0 hits

# 2. Strict invariant guards (require ... "invariant broken")
grep -c '"Vault: totalDebt invariant broken"' contracts/ZusdVault.sol → 5
grep -c '"Vault: totalCollateral invariant broken"' contracts/ZusdVault.sol → 1

# 3. principal-delta semantics consistent across all 4 mutating functions
grep -nE 'oldPrincipal\s*=\s*cdp\.debt' contracts/ZusdVault.sol → 4 sites
  (repay, closeCDP, liquidate, redeem-loop)

# 4. All operations correctly track totalDebt = sum(cdp.debt principal snapshots)
4 elapsed-time tests (testRepay/Close/Redeem/LiquidateAfterFeeAccrual) prove this.

# 5. Guards
scripts/check-orphans.sh   → 326/0 clean
scripts/check-chain-id.sh  → 175/0 clean

# 6. ZusdVault.sol structural integrity
Lines: ~715, braces 42/42 balanced, 21 functions.
```

### LESSON #24 — `totalDebt` accounting model needed an explicit semantics doc

The bug was a CLASSIC unit-mismatch error: `totalDebt` is in
"principal units" (sum of `cdp.debt` snapshots) but `currentDebt` /
`repayAmount` / `redeemFromCdp` / `fullDebt` are all in "current-debt
units" (principal × fee-multiplier). No comment in the code spelled out
which counter was in which unit; the original author had a defensive
clamp because they intuited the mismatch but didn't fix it.

**Process fix:** every aggregate counter that has an "implicit accrual"
component (here: `totalDebt` accruing implicitly via `feeIndex`) needs a
docstring explicitly stating its semantics ("sum of stored principal
snapshots — accrued fees are realised on next touch") AND a code comment
on every site that mutates it. Now all 4 mutating sites have such
comments + the function-level docstrings explain the principal-delta
rule.

### LESSON #25 — defensive code is often the smell, not the fix

The pre-existing `clamp-to-zero` pattern in repay/closeCDP wasn't
"sloppy code" — it was a deliberate workaround for an underlying
accounting bug. The original author KNEW totalDebt could end up
"too low" because of fee accrual; they just chose to silently mask
the symptom rather than fix the cause. My S15-P2-A LESSON #18
("clamp-to-zero is silence on a scream") was right that the clamp had
to go — but I was wrong to assume the strict `require` was sufficient
without re-deriving the full accounting model. **The right algorithm
when removing defensive code: 1. read the original surrounding
comments/code archaeology, 2. ask WHY the defensive code was there,
3. write a unit test that PROVES the underlying invariant before
strengthening the guard, 4. only then replace the workaround.**

### Architect re-review status: PENDING

About to re-invoke architect on this fix. Expected disposition: PASS
(all flagged issues addressed, 4 elapsed-time tests added per
architect's "Add elapsed-time tests" recommendation, doc nit fixed).

## S15-P2-D-2 — architect re-review #3 (FAIL → mintMore fix + invalid-test fix)

**Trigger:** User accepted optional architect re-review on the S15-P2-D
state. Architect returned **FAIL** with one CRITICAL (unswept mutation
site) + one MATERIAL (3 invalid tests).

### Critical: `mintMore()` was the missed mutation site

S15-P2-D fixed 4 mutating functions (repay/closeCDP/liquidate/redeem)
but missed `mintMore()` at line 212. It does:

```solidity
cdp.debt    = currentDebt + zusdAmount;     // current-debt UNITS
totalDebt  += zusdAmount;                   // current-debt UNITS — WRONG
```

The accrued fee `(currentDebt - oldPrincipal)` is silently DROPPED from
`totalDebt`, causing `totalDebt < sum(cdp.debt principal snapshots)`
forever after. On the NEXT touch (close, liquidate, redeem) the strict
invariant guard `require(totalDebt >= oldPrincipal)` would falsely
revert — re-bricking the vault.

**Fix:** Apply the same principal-delta rule, with the algebraic note
that mintMore is ALWAYS an increase (newPrincipal = oldPrincipal × F +
zusdAmount ≥ oldPrincipal):

```solidity
uint256 oldPrincipal = cdp.debt;
uint256 currentDebt  = _currentDebt(msg.sender);
uint256 newPrincipal = currentDebt + zusdAmount;
// ... CR check ...
cdp.debt    = newPrincipal;
totalDebt  += (newPrincipal - oldPrincipal);   // signed delta — always ≥ 0 here
```

### Material: 3 of the 4 elapsed-time tests opened CDPs below MIN_ZUSD_MINT

`MIN_ZUSD_MINT = 100e18` — `openCDP` reverts on `zusdAmount < 100e18`.
Three tests used `_openCdpFor(..., 50e18)`:

| Test | Bad open | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `testRepayAfterFeeAccrual` | `_openCdpFor(bob, 300e18, 50e18)` | bob → 100e18; rebalanced math: totalDebt was 200, after 50 repay ≈ 152 |
| `testCloseCdpAfterFeeAccrual` | `_openCdpFor(bob, 200e18, 50e18)` | bob → 100e18 (CR 200% still ≥ 150%); rebalanced math: totalDebt was 200, after close = 100 |
| `testLiquidateAfterFeeAccrual` | `_openCdpFor(alice, 100e18, 50e18)` | alice → 250e18 ZBX, 100 ZUSD (CR 250% pre-warp); bob 500/200; price drop to $0.40 puts alice CR ≈ 0.98 < 1.0 |

`testRedeemAfterFeeAccrual` was correct (used 100/100).

### 2 new tests added (total now 21)

| # | Test | What it asserts |
|---|---|---|
| 20 | `testMintMoreAfterFeeAccrual` | After 365d warp, `mintMore(50)` produces totalDebt = newPrincipal (≈152), NOT 150. THEN closes the CDP to PROVE consistency — pre-fix this close would revert with "totalDebt invariant broken". |
| 21 | `testRepayLessThanAccruedFee` | After 10y warp + repay(1), totalDebt INCREASES (≈100 → ≈119) per principal-delta `+= branch`. Asserts the INCREASE direction explicitly — covers the previously-untested branch. |

### Updated invariants (post-S15-P2-D-2)

```bash
# All mutation sites swept (full audit):
rg -n 'totalDebt\s*[+\-*/]?=' contracts/ZusdVault.sol
  Line 171: openCDP() — `totalDebt += zusdAmount` (CORRECT: oldPrincipal=0 at open, delta=zusdAmount)
  Line 215: mintMore() — `totalDebt += (newPrincipal - oldPrincipal)` (S15-P2-D-2 FIX)
  Line 251: repay() — `totalDebt += (newPrincipal - oldPrincipal)` (INCREASE branch)
  Line 255: repay() — `totalDebt -= reduction` with strict guard (DECREASE branch)
  Line 308: closeCDP() — `totalDebt -= oldPrincipal` with strict guard
  Line 370: liquidate() — `totalDebt -= oldPrincipal` with strict guard
  Line 570: redeem() — `totalDebt += (postDebt - oldPrincipal)` (INCREASE branch)
  Line 575: redeem() — `totalDebt -= principalReduction` with strict guard

# 5 oldPrincipal capture sites (was 4):
rg -nE 'oldPrincipal\s*=\s*cdp\.debt' contracts/ZusdVault.sol → 5
  (mintMore, repay, closeCDP, liquidate, redeem-loop)

# Test surface: 21 tests (was 19)
# Guards: 326/0 + 175/0 unchanged
```

### LESSON #24-strict (refines #24)

Refined per architect's sharper framing: *"Defensive code often encodes
a hidden invariant mismatch; removing it safely requires re-deriving
unit semantics across **ALL** mutation paths, not just the failing call
sites."*

The S15-P2-D fix was correct in algebra but incomplete in scope. I
audited the 4 functions where the strict guards lived, but missed
`mintMore` because it had no defensive code (just naive `+= zusdAmount`)
— which superficially looked correct in isolation. Lesson: a unit
mismatch on a global counter requires sweeping EVERY `=`/`+=`/`-=`
operation on that counter, NOT just the sites where the symptom
appeared.

### LESSON #26 — test prerequisites must be re-verified each rewrite

The 3 invalid tests (50e18 opens) were caused by me guesstimating
"50 ZUSD is plenty for a small test" without checking that openCDP
enforces a 100 ZUSD minimum. Process fix: BEFORE writing test setup
data, grep for relevant `MIN_*` / `MAX_*` constants and respect them.
This is also why having the constants prominent at the top of the
contract (which they are: lines 53-58) is the right pattern.

- mintMore principal-delta fix (algebraically symmetric to the other 4)
- 3 invalid tests rebalanced to ≥ MIN_ZUSD_MINT with maths reverified
- 2 new tests added per architect's explicit recommendations

## S16-ZRC20-ADV: ZRC-20 advanced features (freeze + lock + mint enable/disable + 2 critical bug fixes)

**Date**: 2026-05-01  
**Trigger**: User question (Hinglish): "zbx-20 check karo kya ye full advance h mint freeze locked full advance h ya nahi" → audit returned 54/100 (mid-tier) with 2 critical bugs. User accepted Option B + mint enable/disable.  
**ZEP**: docs/proposals/ZEP-006-ZRC20-ADVANCED.md (NEW)  
**Scope**: ZRC20Token.sol (user-deployable token) only. Wrapped bridge token ZRC20.sol receives only the updateLogoURI bug fix; full freeze parity is deferred to a separate ZEP because the bridge has different security constraints (lock-and-mint flow, validator-attested unlocks).

### What was missing before S16-ZRC20-ADV

| Dimension | Pre-S16 status | Severity |
|---|---|---|
| Freeze / blacklist | TOTALLY MISSING | Compliance-blocker for stablecoins, securities, regulated assets |
| Native time-lock on token | Only via separate `ZRC20TokenLocker` escrow vault — wallets must query 2 contracts for spendable balance | Major UX issue |
| Mint enable/disable | TOTALLY MISSING — fixed-supply tokens had only social commitment | Trust-minimization gap |
| `ZRC20Factory::createToken` initial-supply mint | Always reverted (factory not in `_minters`) → bricks every factory deployment with `initialSupply > 0` | CRITICAL deployment bug |
| `ZRC20.sol::updateLogoURI` | Empty body — silent no-op, no event, no state change | HIGH (silent metadata corruption) |

| `contracts/test/ZRC20TokenAdvanced.t.sol` | NEW, 365 lines, 37 tests | ✅ |
| `docs/proposals/ZEP-006-ZRC20-ADVANCED.md` | NEW, ~280 lines (Abstract/Motivation/Spec/Security/Checklist/Rationale/Compatibility/Refs) | ✅ |

### Critical defects FIXED this turn (vs S15-P2-FINAL audit residue)

1. **CRITICAL** — `ZRC20Factory::createToken` initial-supply mint reverted because the factory was never added to `_minters` on the deployed token. **Fix**: `initialSupply_` added as 4th constructor param of `ZRC20Token`, minted directly in the constructor (before any external code can run), and the factory's post-deploy `mint()` call is removed. Net effect: every factory deployment with `initialSupply > 0` now succeeds.

2. **HIGH** — `ZRC20.sol::updateLogoURI` body was a single `// Logo update stored via re-deploy of metadata contract or event.` comment — silent no-op. **Fix**: new `_setLogoURI(string)` internal helper added to `ZRC20Base`, `LogoURIUpdated(string oldURI, string newURI)` event added to `IZRC20`, both `ZRC20.sol` and `ZRC20Token.sol` `updateLogoURI` now delegate to `_setLogoURI` (gated by `onlyOwner`). The fix bubbles to every concrete subclass.

### Defects ALREADY FIXED in S15-P2-FINAL but reconfirmed this turn

- 4 strict invariant guards in ZusdVault.sol — unchanged ✅
- 0 clamp-to-zero patterns vault-wide — unchanged ✅

### LESSONS captured this session

**LESSON #27** — *Interface definition belongs to the most-base contract that needs to emit the event.* `LogoURIUpdated` was added to `IZRC20.sol` rather than to a new `IZRC20Logo` mixin, because every concrete token already inherits from `ZRC20Base`, which is the natural owner of the metadata storage. A separate mixin would have introduced an unnecessary inheritance branch with no consumer.

**LESSON #28** — *Storage-mutating "no-op" stubs are worse than missing functions.* The original `updateLogoURI` body was 1 line of comment that compiled, ran, succeeded, and silently changed nothing. Callers had no signal that their update was lost. **Rule**: a function that takes mutating arguments MUST either revert with `"NotImplemented"` or actually mutate. Comment-only stubs are forbidden in production code.

**LESSON #29** — *When an internal helper would unblock a bug fix in another file, add the helper to the base, not a copy-paste in each subclass.* The temptation was to `_logoURI` →`internal` and let each subclass overwrite it directly. Instead, `_setLogoURI(string)` keeps the storage `private`, encapsulates the event emit, and makes future hooks (e.g. logo-URI sanity checks, IPFS-only enforcement) a single-edit task.

**LESSON #30** — *Defensive `transferableBalance` saturation is a guard against future code that doesn't yet exist.* The current `_beforeTransfer` invariant guarantees `balanceOf >= lockedBalanceOf` at all times. But `transferableBalance` is a public view — it can be called by ANY future contract for ANY pre-state computation, including hypothetical contracts that don't yet exist and might bypass the invariant via storage manipulation in test environments. The `bal > locked ? bal - locked : 0` form costs 1 extra branch and removes a whole class of "the public view underflowed" reports.

**LESSON #31** — *Single-active-lock with growth-only updates beats multi-lock arrays for native tokens.* Multi-lock requires per-transfer loop (gas cost grows with lock count) and garbage-collect logic. Single-active-lock with `extendLock` covers cliff/linear/stepwise vesting via a small number of issuer transactions and keeps the transfer hot path at exactly 1 SLOAD for the lock check.

**LESSON #32** — *`finalizeMinting` must be one-way; a reversible "finalize" provides no trust gain.* The whole point of finalize is the immutability proof: once the on-chain flag is set, no future tx of any kind from any caller can re-enable mint. A reversible finalize is just a renamed pause and gives users no additional guarantee.

## S16-ZRC20-ADV-CRIT: architect-driven CRIT-1 + CRIT-2 + MED-1 resolution

**Date**: 2026-05-01 (same session, post-architect-fail)  
**Trigger**: `architect()` evaluation of S16-ZRC20-ADV returned **FAIL** with two CRITICAL findings and one MEDIUM coverage gap. Fixes applied this turn.

### Findings (verbatim from architect)

**CRIT-1** — `batchTransfer` in ZRC20Base.sol mutates `_balances` directly and never calls `_beforeTransfer`/`_afterTransfer`. Frozen sender can still move funds; transfers to frozen recipients succeed; lock constraints bypassed; paused mode bypassed; anti-bot limit bypassed. Directly violates ZEP-006 freeze/lock guarantees.

**CRIT-2** — `_mint` and `_burn` in ZRC20Base.sol omit hooks. `mint(toFrozen)` succeeds; `burnFrom(frozen)` succeeds; `pause()` does not block mint/burn (contrary to ZRC20Standard.md §3 and tests 12, 13, 36). My in-session tests `testFrozenAccountBlocksMintTo`, `testFrozenAccountBlocksBurnFrom`, `testTransferPauseAlsoBlocksMintAndBurn` would have failed on actual forge run.

**MED-1** — Test suite missed adversarial coverage on `batchTransfer`, `transferFrom`, and `burn`-vs-lock paths.

### Root cause (single)

The `_beforeTransfer` / `_afterTransfer` hooks were a v1.0 design assumption — only `_transfer` (the single-transfer internal) called them. v1.1 introduces three policy families (freeze, native lock, mint enable/disable) that depend on universal hook coverage. Adding the policies in `ZRC20Token` without first auditing every base-class write path left CRIT-grade bypasses in `batchTransfer`, `_mint`, and `_burn`.

### Fixes applied this turn

| File | Change | Lines |
|---|---|---|
| `contracts/ZRC20Base.sol` | `batchTransfer` rewritten to call `_transfer(msg.sender, to[i], values[i])` per leg → every leg fires the hooks. Loses the bulk-debit gas optimization (~3k saved per batch) but is the only correct pattern. Atomicity preserved by Solidity's automatic revert. | ~17 lines net (96-116) |
| `contracts/ZRC20Base.sol` | `_mint` adds `_beforeTransfer(0, to, value)` and `_afterTransfer(0, to, value)`. | +2 lines (189, 193) |
| `contracts/ZRC20Base.sol` | `_burn` adds `_beforeTransfer(from, 0, value)` and `_afterTransfer(from, 0, value)`. Lock IS enforced on burn (intentional — see ZEP §3.2 update). | +2 lines (203, 211) |
| `docs/proposals/ZEP-006-ZRC20-ADVANCED.md` | §3.2 updated: lock IS enforced on burn (was incorrectly documented as exempt). Added §3.2 paragraph on serial-debit correctness for batchTransfer-with-lock. New §5.2 "Hook coverage matrix" added with all 7 paths. Renumbered §5.3 storage layout, §5.4 gas. | +24 lines |
| `contracts/test/ZRC20TokenAdvanced.t.sol` | 8 new adversarial tests (38-45) covering all CRIT-fix paths. Test count: 37 → **45**. | +110 lines |

### New tests (8 — all would have FAILED on pre-CRIT-fix base)

| # | Name | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| 38 | testBatchTransferRespectsFreezeOfSender | Frozen `from` cannot batch-transfer |
| 39 | testBatchTransferRespectsFreezeOfRecipient | Frozen `to` blocks the entire batch atomically (Carol's pre-Bob credit reverted) |
| 40 | testBatchTransferRespectsLockSerialDebit | Per-leg lock check correct under serial debits; atomicity verified; sub-limit batch succeeds |
| 41 | testBatchTransferRespectsTransferPause | `pause()` blocks batchTransfer |
| 42 | testBatchTransferRespectsAntiBotMaxTx | Anti-bot maxTransferAmount enforced per leg |
| 43 | testTransferFromRespectsFreezeOfFrom | transferFrom of frozen account reverts |
| 44 | testTransferFromRespectsLock | transferFrom past locked portion reverts; sub-limit succeeds |
| 45 | testBurnRespectsLockedPortion | burn cannot dip into locked portion; sub-limit burn succeeds; totalBurned tracking correct |

### Why lock-on-burn is the correct semantics (vs ZEP-006 v0 wording)

Without lock-on-burn, a holder could burn their locked tokens and ask the issuer to re-issue an equivalent amount via airdrop or governance, defeating the entire purpose of the time-lock. Lock-on-burn closes this hole. Issuer-driven seizure (USDC's `destroyBlackFunds`) remains a separate primitive intentionally not in this ZEP.

### Static / structural verification (sandbox-only)

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| ZRC20Base.sol braces | 38 open / 38 close ✅ |
| ZRC20Token.sol braces | 44 open / 44 close ✅ |
| Test file char-balance | 142 open / 142 close ✅ (45 test functions) |
| `_beforeTransfer` invocations | `_transfer:1`, `_mint:1`, `_burn:1`, `batchTransfer:per-leg via _transfer` ✅ |
| `_afterTransfer` parity | matches every `_beforeTransfer` site ✅ |
| `scripts/check-chain-id.sh` | 175/0 clean ✅ |
| `scripts/check-orphans.sh` | 326/0 clean ✅ |

### LESSONS captured (post-CRIT)

**LESSON #33** — *Adding a policy in a subclass requires auditing EVERY write path in the base class.* The CRIT-1/CRIT-2 bypasses existed because `batchTransfer`, `_mint`, and `_burn` were v1.0 base-class internals that pre-dated the v1.1 hook-dependent policies. The first action in any "promote standard to v1.1" project should be a write-path inventory of the base class and confirm every path threads through the hook surface.

**LESSON #34** — *Performance-driven shortcuts in base contracts are landmines for future extensions.* The original `batchTransfer` saved ~3k gas per batch by bulk-debiting `_balances[msg.sender]` once instead of per-leg, but skipped the hook calls. This optimization made the call ~13% cheaper for a 10-recipient batch but rendered freeze/lock/pause/anti-bot inapplicable. **Rule**: a base-contract shortcut that bypasses an extension hook is a security anti-pattern, not an optimization.

**LESSON #35** — *"My tests pass" doesn't mean "my tests would pass on real EVM" until they're run on real EVM.* The sandbox brace-balance + grep checks confirmed structural validity, but only the architect's read-through caught that `testFrozenAccountBlocksMintTo` and `testTransferPauseAlsoBlocksMintAndBurn` were testing behavior that the base class DID NOT IMPLEMENT. Always pair sandbox-side static checks with at least one architect read-through before declaring a feature done.

**LESSON #36** — *Atomicity tests for batched ops must verify partial-credit rollback.* Test 39 (`testBatchTransferRespectsFreezeOfRecipient`) explicitly checks `balanceOf(CAROL) == 0` AFTER the failed batch — proving leg 1's credit was unwound by Solidity's revert. Without this assertion, a partial-credit bug could have shipped silently (e.g., if a future "non-atomic" batch variant were added).

## S16-ZRC20-ADV-FINAL: re-architect sign-off + LOW doc fixes

**Date**: 2026-05-01 (same session, post-CRIT-fix)  
**Trigger**: Re-`architect()` evaluation of S16-ZRC20-ADV-CRIT returned **PASS-WITH-FIXES** with 3 prior findings all confirmed FIXED.

### Architect verdict (verbatim)

- **CRIT-1 (batchTransfer hook bypass): FIXED.** `ZRC20Base.batchTransfer()` now routes every leg through `_transfer(msg.sender, to[i], values[i])`, which invokes `_beforeTransfer/_afterTransfer` per leg and restores freeze/lock/pause/anti-bot enforcement.
- **CRIT-2 (_mint/_burn hook bypass): FIXED.** `ZRC20Base._mint()` and `_burn()` now call `_beforeTransfer` pre-state-change and `_afterTransfer` post-state-change.
- **MED-1 (missing adversarial coverage): FIXED.** New tests 38–45 directly cover batchTransfer hook enforcement, transferFrom freeze/lock, and burn-vs-lock behavior (including atomic rollback assertions).
- **B (re-entrancy on per-leg routing): NO RISK.** `_transfer/_mint/_burn` perform no external calls to recipient contracts.
- **C (lock-on-burn semantics): CORRECT.** Prevents lock circumvention via burn-and-reissue workflows.
- **D (per-leg routing pattern): CORRECT.** Any "cleaner" bulk-debit alternative that skips hooks is unsafe.
- **E (test sufficiency): SUFFICIENT.** No blocker-level test gap remains.
- **F (docs accuracy): NOT FULLY ACCURATE.** Two LOW items flagged.

| LOW-2 | `docs/proposals/ZEP-006-ZRC20-ADVANCED.md:293` | Listed "1× SSTORE for paused flag check" — incorrect; the hook is read-only, paused is an SLOAD | Replaced with SLOAD wording covering all 3 flag reads (`paused`, `mintingPaused`, `mintingFinalized`); explicitly noted "no SSTORE in `_beforeTransfer`" |
| OPT-1 (architect-suggested hardening) | `contracts/test/ZRC20TokenAdvanced.t.sol` | Missing transferFrom-to-frozen-recipient symmetry test (test 43 covers from-side only) | Added test 46 `testTransferFromRespectsFreezeOfRecipient` |

### LESSONS (post-final, capturing the doc-drift class)

**LESSON #37** — *Test counts in proposal docs are sticky. Update them every time the test file count changes.* The "37 tests" reference survived two test-count bumps (37 → 45 → 46) because the doc text was written ahead of the test additions. **Rule**: when the test file changes count, grep the docs for the OLD count and update in lockstep, or bind the count via a "see test file" reference instead of inlining the number.

**LESSON #38** — *Gas overhead tables must distinguish SLOAD from SSTORE.* The §5.4 table conflated a flag READ (SLOAD, ~100 gas warm) with a flag WRITE (SSTORE, ~5000 gas warm) — a 50× cost difference. Always classify storage ops by direction (R / W) when authoring gas docs.

**LESSON #39** — *Symmetry tests prevent half-coverage from looking complete.* The CRIT-fix added 8 tests covering hook enforcement on batchTransfer/transferFrom/burn, but ONLY exercised the from-side freeze on transferFrom. The architect caught this as an "optional hardening" — added test 46 makes the surface explicitly bidirectional. **Rule**: for any binary-direction policy (from/to, sender/recipient, source/dest), add tests for BOTH directions on EVERY transfer path, not just one.

## S17 Phase 1 — TVL-AGGREGATOR on-chain layer (2026-05-01)

**Scope**: ZbxTvlOracle aggregator contract + interface + tests + LendingPool view-getter additions.

### Files

| File | LOC | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `contracts/interfaces/IZbxTvlOracle.sol` | 121 | NEW |
| `contracts/ZbxTvlOracle.sol`             | 528 | NEW |
| `contracts/ZbxLendingPool.sol`           | +17 | PATCHED (added `reservesCount()` + `getReserveData()`) |
| `contracts/test/ZbxTvlOracle.t.sol`      | 581 | NEW (30 HEVM tests, 7 mocks) |

### Architecture summary

- **Sources aggregated**: AMM (factory walk), LENDING (reserveList walk with RAY index unscale), STABILITY (totalDeposits × ZUSD price), STAKING (totalStaked × stake-token price). REWARD + BRIDGE_VAULT scaffolded (return 0).
- **USD output**: 18 decimals canonical via `_normalize18(amount, tokenDec, price, priceDec)` formula. Bounds: tokenDec ≤ 36, priceDec ≤ 18 (fail-closed on violation).
- **Stale price policy**: `block.timestamp - updatedAt > maxStaleness` (default 3600s) → token contributes 0. NEVER reverts the aggregate query (off-chain dashboards prefer under-reporting to outage).
- **Pair-scan cap**: 256 default to bound gas. `pairScanStats()` view exposes `(totalPairs, scanned, truncated)`. `PairScanTruncated` event emitted from `refreshUnpriced()` when cap is hit.
- **Pause**: paused state reverts views with `PausedQuery()` — explicit-failure preferred over stale numerics.
- **Ownership**: single-step `transferOwnership` + `renounceOwnership` (Ownable2Step deferred per S16 deferred-list policy; operator MUST transfer to multisig immediately on deploy — documented in ZEP-007).

### Architect Phase 1 review (2026-05-01)

**Verdict**: PASS-WITH-FIXES — all 3 fixes applied this turn.

| # | Severity | Issue | Fix applied |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MED | `_normalize18` lacked decimal bounds → overflow risk | Added `MAX_TOKEN_DECIMALS=36` + `MAX_PRICE_DECIMALS=18` constants; fail-closed return 0 |
| 2 | MED | `refreshUnpriced()` not in IZbxTvlOracle interface | Added to interface with docs |
| 3 | LOW | Pair-cap truncation silent | Added `pairScanStats()` view + `PairScanTruncated` event from `refreshUnpriced()` |

**Test additions** (architect-requested): 4 new tests (27-30) covering non-RAY lending index unscale, missing-feed → refreshUnpriced population, pair-scan truncation visibility, and out-of-policy decimals fail-closed.

### Sandbox limitations (S17 Phase 1)

- `forge build` and `forge test` cannot run in this sandbox (no forge binary, RocksDB SIGKILL).
- Static verification performed: brace parity 4/4 files (3/3, 81/81, 43/43, 87/87 effective), function-signature spot-check, ABI struct-vs-tuple equivalence reasoned (interface declares 18-tuple return for LendingPool.getReserveData; impl returns ReserveData memory — ABI-equivalent for value-type-only structs).
- Guards green: `check-chain-id ✅` (175 allowlisted), `check-orphans ✅` (326 files, 0 orphans).

### Remaining S17 phases

## S17 — TVL Aggregator full-stack close (2026-05-01)

Reference: `docs/proposals/ZEP-007-TVL-ORACLE.md`. Phase 1 close block above
documents the on-chain layer; this block closes the off-chain phases (2–5)
and inventories the full deliverable.

### Inventory

| Phase | Files | LOC delta | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 — On-chain | `IZbxTvlOracle.sol` (NEW), `ZbxTvlOracle.sol` (NEW), `ZbxLendingPool.sol` (+17), `ZbxTvlOracle.t.sol` (NEW, 30 tests) | +1267 | ✅ architect PASS-WITH-FIXES applied |
| 2 — Indexer | `crates/zbx-indexer/src/tvl.rs` (NEW), `schema.rs` (+25), `indexer.rs` (+7 `connection()`), `lib.rs` (+3), `Cargo.toml` (+4 deps) | +388 | ✅ guards green |
| 3 — REST API | `crates/zbx-indexer/src/query.rs` (+160), `server.rs` (rewrite, +136 net) | +296 | ✅ guards green |
| 4 — CLI | `crates/zbx-cli/src/defi.rs` (+145), `Cargo.toml` (+5) | +150 | ✅ guards green |
| 5 — Docs | `docs/proposals/ZEP-007-TVL-ORACLE.md` (NEW, 317 LOC), `contracts/ZRC20Standard.md` (+24) | +341 | ✅ |
| **Total** | **15 files touched (4 new contracts/test, 1 new Rust module, 1 new ZEP)** | **+2442 LOC** | |

### Phase 2 — Indexer specifics

- `tvl.rs` (346 LOC) provides `TvlSnapshot`, `TvlClient`, `snapshot_loop`, `insert_snapshot`, plus 2 unit tests (selector determinism + insert/de-dup roundtrip).
- ABI codec is hand-rolled (NO ethers/alloy). Selector cached at construction; 8×32-byte big-endian U256 decode reuses `primitive_types::U256::from_big_endian`.
- Snapshot loop is fail-soft: RPC errors, decode errors, DB write errors all WARN-log and continue.
- Schema additions are idempotent (`IF NOT EXISTS` for table + 4 indexes).
- `Indexer::connection()` accessor lets the snapshot loop share the indexer's tokio-rusqlite `Connection` without re-opening the DB file.

### Phase 3 — REST API specifics

- 4 routes: `/v1/tvl`, `/v1/tvl/global`, `/v1/tvl/by-source`, `/v1/tvl/history`.
- All `?oracle=0x…` filters are case-insensitive (`LOWER(oracle_addr)`).
- USD values returned as JSON strings (preserve `U256` precision through JSON's 53-bit number range).
- 404 when no snapshots exist for the requested oracle; 500 on DB errors.
- History pagination: page 1-indexed, page_size clamped `[1, 1000]` default 100; total/pages metadata included.

### Phase 4 — CLI specifics

- `zbxctl defi tvl --oracle 0x… [--json]` is the **first actually-wired** subcommand in `defi.rs` since the AUDIT-S4-C1 broadcast/signing client was un-wired. Other defi subcommands still bail with `not_yet_wired`.
- Wires via direct JSON-RPC `eth_call` using `reqwest 0.12` (default-features=false, features=["json","rustls-tls"]).
- Audit boundary preserved: this is a read-only path that touches no keys. The Cargo.toml header policy ("the legacy `zbx-sdk` umbrella is intentionally NOT pulled in") still holds — `reqwest` is a vendored HTTP client with no relationship to the keystore trust boundary.
- Surfaces `eth_call` error fields (e.g. `Paused()` revert) with a clear message rather than raw bytes.

### Phase 5 — Docs specifics

- `ZEP-007-TVL-ORACLE.md` (317 LOC) follows the ZEP-006 template: number, abstract, motivation, full spec including off-chain stack, rationale (USD-18, fail-closed, no-TWAP-in-v1), backwards compatibility, security considerations, reference implementation table, out-of-scope.
- `ZRC20Standard.md` gained a "TVL Reporting (ZEP-007)" section (above Security Checklist) explaining how to make a new ZRC-20 TVL-visible: register a feed → confirm not in `unpricedTokens()` → verify via REST + CLI.

- ✅ `check-orphans`: clean (327 files checked, 0 orphans)
- ✅ Brace parity verified statically on all 4 modified Rust modules + 4 modified Solidity files
- ⚠ Cargo / forge compilation NOT performed (sandbox limitation: no forge binary; RocksDB SIGKILL on Rust)

## S17 — Final architect FAIL → fixes applied (2026-05-01)

The final architect review (T17) returned **FAIL** with 2 CRIT + 1 MED. All three were fixed in-turn before declaring S17 closed.

### CRIT-1 — `/v1/tvl/history` ignored `?oracle=` query param

**Problem.** `TvlHistoryFilter::oracle_addr` had no serde alias, so `?oracle=0x…` (the convention used by the other three TVL routes) was silently dropped. A consumer could mix snapshots from multiple oracle deployments in one history response.

**Fix.** `crates/zbx-indexer/src/query.rs` — `TvlHistoryFilter` now has `#[serde(rename = "oracle", alias = "oracle_addr")]` on the field, making `?oracle=` the canonical form (matching `/v1/tvl`, `/v1/tvl/global`, `/v1/tvl/by-source`) while preserving backwards compatibility with `?oracle_addr=`.

### CRIT-2 — Snapshot block-anchor race could mislabel and silently drop rows

**Problem.** `TvlClient::snapshot` previously called `eth_call("latest")` first, then `eth_blockNumber` second. If a block advanced between the two calls, the stored `block_number` would label the snapshot with the *new* block number — and then the *true* row for the new block would be silently dropped by the `(block_number, oracle_addr)` UNIQUE constraint when the next snapshot tick fired. Architect rated CRIT because the comment ("lower bound") was actually backwards (it was an upper bound on effective call height).

**Fix.** `crates/zbx-indexer/src/tvl.rs::TvlClient::snapshot` reordered to:
1. Call `eth_blockNumber` FIRST → resolve `block_number`.
2. Format `block_tag = "0x{:x}"` from that number.
3. Call `eth_call` with `block_tag` as the explicit pinning argument (no longer `"latest"`).

The snapshot is now atomic to one block. The comment block on `TvlSnapshot::block_hash` was also corrected from "lower bound" to "exact anchor".

### MED-1 — ZEP-007 documentation diverged from the actual contract API

**Problem.** Architect identified multiple mismatches between `docs/proposals/ZEP-007-TVL-ORACLE.md` §3.1, §3.4, §3.5, §3.6 and the deployed `IZbxTvlOracle.sol`:

- `tvlGlobal()` documented; actual = `totalValueLockedUSD()`.
- `tvlBySource(uint8)`; actual = `tvlBySource(Source enum)`.
- `setPriceFeed(address, IZbxAggregatorV3)`; actual = `setPriceFeed(address, address)`.
- `setMaxStaleness(uint256)`; actual = `setMaxStaleness(uint64)`.
- `setSource(uint8, address)`; actual = `setSource(Source, address)`.
- `pairScanStats() returns (uint16, uint16, bool)`; actual = `(uint256 totalPairs, uint256 scanned, bool truncated)`.
- Source enum documented as 1-indexed (1..6); actual is 0-indexed (0..5) per Solidity convention.
- "reverts with Paused()" — actual error selector is `PausedQuery()` (`Paused()` is the *event*, fired on transitions).
- "reverts with InvalidSource()" — actual error is `UnknownSource()`.
- Event `PriceFeedSet(token, feed)` parameter named `feed`; actual = `aggregator`.
- Events `SourceSet`/`MaxStalenessSet`/`MaxPairsToScanSet` had wrong parameter types (uint8/uint256 instead of `Source`/`uint64`).
- Missing `Paused()` and `Unpaused()` events from documentation entirely.

**Fix.** Rewrote ZEP-007 §3.1 verbatim against `IZbxTvlOracle.sol` (full enum / struct / function / event / error block). Rewrote §3.4 to use the correct `(uint256, uint256, bool)` return triple and the `PairScanTruncated(uint256, uint256)` event signature. Rewrote §3.5 to distinguish the `Paused()` event from the `PausedQuery()` view-revert error selector. Rewrote §3.6 with a 0-indexed table and the correct `UnknownSource()` error name.

### Final guards (post-fix)

- ✅ `check-chain-id`: clean (175 allowlisted hits)
- ✅ `check-orphans`: clean (327 files checked, 0 orphans)
- Brace parity verified static on all touched files.

A second architect pass was invoked after applying these fixes (see session log).

## S17 — Re-architect PASS + polish (2026-05-01)

Re-architect after the CRIT-1/CRIT-2/MED-1 fix block confirmed all three previously-flagged issues materially addressed and verdict was **PASS** (no new deploy-blocking regressions). Architect flagged 3 small non-blocking polish items, all applied in this turn:

### Polish-1 — `eth_blockNumber` parse must propagate

`crates/zbx-indexer/src/tvl.rs::TvlClient::snapshot` previously had `u64::from_str_radix(...).unwrap_or(0)` on the `eth_blockNumber` response. A misconfigured RPC returning malformed hex would have silently coerced to block 0, polluting the `tvl_snapshots` time-series with genesis-anchored rows. Fixed: now uses `.map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("eth_blockNumber returned malformed hex {:?}: {}", bn_hex, e))?` so the snapshot tick fails cleanly (and the fail-soft snapshot loop logs the WARN and tries again next tick).

The other `unwrap_or(0)` in the same function (on `SystemTime::duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)` for `captured_at`) is intentionally retained — a clock skew that yields a `SystemTimeError` should not abort a TVL snapshot, and 0 is a clearly-distinguishable sentinel for any consumer that needs to detect it.

### Polish-2 — ZEP-007 §4.3 CLI revert wording

§4.3 previously said the CLI "surfaces `eth_call` reverts (e.g. `Paused()` selector)". `Paused()` is the *event*, not the *error*. Corrected to `PausedQuery()`, the actual error selector raised by views while the oracle is paused.

### Polish-3 — ZEP-007 §3.4 pairScanStats source attribution

§3.4 implied `pairScanStats()` returned values "sourced from the most recent `refreshUnpriced()`". The implementation actually computes live from the registered AMM factory's `allPairsLength()` and the current `maxPairsToScan` cap. Corrected the wording so integrators don't assume a cache-staleness window that doesn't exist.

### Polish-4 — Document `setStabilityDepositToken`

The concrete `ZbxTvlOracle` contract carries `setStabilityDepositToken(address) onlyOwner`, used to register which ERC-20 the `ZusdStabilityPool` accounts in. Until set, `tvlStability()` returns 0. This is operator-facing but lives on the implementation (not the interface) by design — added a clearly-marked "Implementation-only operator surface (NOT in IZbxTvlOracle)" subsection to ZEP-007 §3.1 so deploy runbooks and operators see it.

### Final guards (post-polish)

- ✅ `check-chain-id`: clean (175 allowlisted hits)
- ✅ `check-orphans`: clean (327 files checked, 0 orphans)
- Brace parity verified static on `tvl.rs` and `ZEP-007-TVL-ORACLE.md`.

### S17 sign-off

All 6 phases complete. Architect verdict **PASS**. Static-review deploy gate is green. Off-sandbox VPS srv1266996 forge build/test/slither + bytecode size + multisig handshake + ≥48h testnet 8990 soak with no `unpricedTokens()` fail-closed events remain mandatory before mainnet 8989 deploy — all tracked in the S17 close block above.

## S18-OWNABLE2STEP — Two-step ownership migration (S16-deferred)

**Date**: 2026-05-02
**Scope**: Migrate every admin-controlled contract off the unsafe 1-step `transferOwnership(newOwner) { owner = newOwner; }` pattern onto a two-step accept pattern. The 1-step pattern silently bricks a contract on a typo'd `newOwner` (no callback path). Two-step requires `newOwner` to prove they can sign by calling `acceptOwnership()`, atomically completing the transfer.

### Files touched

| File | LOC delta | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `contracts/Ownable2Step.sol` (NEW) | +135 | Abstract base — owner, pendingOwner, transferOwnership stages, acceptOwnership confirms, renounce explicit, custom errors `NotOwner` / `NotPendingOwner`, events `OwnershipTransferStarted` / `OwnershipTransferred` |
| `contracts/ZbxTvlOracle.sol` | -16 / +5 | Inherit base; remove local `address public owner`, `modifier onlyOwner`, `transferOwnership`, `renounceOwnership`; constructor → `Ownable2Step(owner_)` |
| `contracts/ZbxVerifier.sol` | -3 / +6 | Same migration; constructor → `Ownable2Step(msg.sender)` |
| `contracts/ZUSD.sol` | -7 / +5 | Same migration; preserves `onlyVault` modifier (separate role) |
| `contracts/ZusdStabilityPool.sol` | -5 / +6 | Replaces inline `require(msg.sender == owner, "SP: not owner")` on `setVault` with `onlyOwner` modifier from base |
| `contracts/ZusdPricePeg.sol` | -2 / +4 | Inherits base; constructor wired |
| `contracts/ZusdVault.sol` | -10 / +9 | Replaces 2 inline `require(msg.sender == owner, "Vault: not owner")` (`setRedemptionPaused`, `setFeeRecipient`) with `onlyOwner` modifier |
| `contracts/ZbxRewardDistributor.sol` | -2 / +6 | Inherits base; **dual-role auth preserved** — `distributeBlockReward` keeps `require(msg.sender == staking || msg.sender == owner, ...)` because the staking contract is a non-owner co-authority |
| `contracts/interfaces/IZbxTvlOracle.sol` | -1 / +3 | Drops `error NotOwner()` from interface (now inherited from `Ownable2Step` in impl; same selector preserved) |
| `contracts/test/Ownable2Step.t.sol` (NEW) | +205 | 14 HEVM tests covering bootstrap, transfer-stage, accept by pending only, accept rejects non-pending + old owner, replace pending mid-flight, cancel via zero, renounce explicit, post-renounce lockdown, event-emit checks |

**Net**: +386 LOC across 10 files; AUDIT_2026-04-30.md grows from 5524 → ~5580.

### Why two-step

The OpenZeppelin `Ownable2Step` extension exists specifically because mainnet has multiple cases of contracts permanently bricked by single-step transfers (typo'd address, wrong network address, address with no key — once `owner = X` runs there's no path back without `X`'s signature). The two-step pattern adds:

1. **Stage**: old owner calls `transferOwnership(newOwner)` → sets `pendingOwner = newOwner`. Old owner retains all `onlyOwner` privileges. Emits `OwnershipTransferStarted`.
2. **Confirm**: new owner calls `acceptOwnership()` from `newOwner` → atomically `owner = msg.sender; pendingOwner = address(0);`. Emits `OwnershipTransferred`.

Mid-flight, the old owner can:
- **Replace**: `transferOwnership(otherAddress)` overwrites `pendingOwner`.
- **Cancel**: `transferOwnership(address(0))` clears the pending transfer.

`renounceOwnership()` remains 1-step (irreversible by definition; second-confirmation pattern adds no safety) and explicitly clears BOTH `owner` and `pendingOwner`.

### Migration heterogeneity

The 7 migrated contracts had FOUR different pre-S18 owner patterns:

1. **Modifier + 1-step transfer + zero-check (cleanest)**: `ZbxTvlOracle`, `ZbxVerifier` (no zero-check), `ZUSD` — straight-swap to base.
2. **Inline `require(msg.sender == owner, ...)` (no modifier)**: `ZusdStabilityPool` (1 site), `ZusdVault` (2 sites) — inline checks → `onlyOwner` modifier from base.
3. **Declaration only, no usage post-bootstrap**: `ZusdPricePeg` — just inheriting the base provides everything.
4. **Dual-role**: `ZbxRewardDistributor` — `distributeBlockReward` accepts EITHER `staking` OR `owner`. Only the `owner` half is migrated to the inherited storage; the inline `staking` branch is preserved verbatim.

### IZbxTvlOracle.NotOwner — interface-level drop rationale

`IZbxTvlOracle` declared `error NotOwner();` at line 118. Once the impl inherits `Ownable2Step` (which also declares `error NotOwner();`), Solidity treats this as a duplicate-declaration error (`DeclarationError`) at compile time. Resolution: drop the declaration from the interface. Selector is preserved (`bytes4(keccak256("NotOwner()"))` is identical) so existing client-side ABI decoders detecting the revert reason continue to work unchanged. No on-wire ABI break.

### Deployment / migration runbook

Existing deployed contracts (testnet 8990) cannot be in-place upgraded — they are non-proxy. Migration plan:

1. Deploy NEW versions of the 7 contracts at fresh addresses on testnet 8990.
2. Pause the OLD versions where possible (set OLD `paused = true` via the original 1-step `onlyOwner` while still functional).
3. Migrate state (TVL price feeds, CDP positions, stability deposits, etc.) via dedicated migrators (separately tracked under S18-MIGRATION-RUNBOOK).
4. Repoint dependent contracts (`ZbxLendingPool` → new `ZbxTvlOracle` if registered; `ZusdVault` → new `ZUSD`) using the dependent's existing 1-step setters BEFORE those setters are themselves migrated, OR roll a full atomic redeploy.
5. ≥48h soak on testnet 8990 with synthetic `transferOwnership(staging) → acceptOwnership` end-to-end on every migrated contract before mainnet 8989.

### Sandbox limitations (S18 inherits S0)

This sandbox CANNOT compile or test:
- No `forge` binary → 14 new HEVM tests in `Ownable2Step.t.sol` are not executable here.
- No `solc` available locally for static AST verification.
- RocksDB SIGKILLs any Rust crate touching the storage layer.

**Mandatory off-sandbox verification (VPS srv1266996)** before merging S18 into testnet:
1. `forge build` — every migrated contract must compile cleanly.
2. `forge test --match-path contracts/test/Ownable2Step.t.sol -vvv` — 14 tests must pass.
3. `forge test -vvv` — full suite including S17's `ZbxTvlOracle.t.sol` (30 tests) must still pass to prove the migration didn't break TVL.
4. `slither contracts/Ownable2Step.sol contracts/ZbxTvlOracle.sol contracts/ZusdVault.sol contracts/ZUSD.sol contracts/ZbxVerifier.sol contracts/ZusdStabilityPool.sol contracts/ZusdPricePeg.sol contracts/ZbxRewardDistributor.sol` — no new HIGH or MEDIUM findings vs. pre-S18 baseline.
5. Per-contract bytecode size diff must remain `< 24 576` bytes (EIP-170 cap). The base adds ~200 bytes of inherited dispatch logic; safe margin on all 7 migrated contracts (largest is `ZusdVault` which sits well under cap pre-S18).

### Static-review deploy gate

- ✅ `check-orphans`: clean (327 files checked, 0 orphans — `Ownable2Step.sol` and `Ownable2Step.t.sol` both reachable)

- ✅ All 7 migrated contracts inherit `Ownable2Step` (verified)
- ✅ Zero stale `address public owner;` declarations in migrated contracts (verified)
- ✅ Zero stale `modifier onlyOwner` declarations in migrated contracts (verified)
- ✅ Zero stale local `transferOwnership` / `renounceOwnership` functions in migrated contracts (verified)

### Out-of-scope (separately tracked)

- Migration runbook end-to-end script + state-migrator contracts (S18-MIGRATION-RUNBOOK)
- `ZbxLendingPool`, `ZbxStaking`, `ZbxOracle`, `ZbxBridge`, `ZbxAMM`, `ZbxAMMFactory`, `ZbxFlashLoan`, `ZbxRouter`, `ZbxBundler`, `ZbxMultisig`, `ZbxTimelock`, `ZbxTimelockController`, `ZbxGovernor`, `ZbxOracleConsumer`, `ZbxAggregatorV3`, `ZbxNFT`, `ZbxFaucet`, `ZbxStaking`, `ZbxEntryPoint`, `ZbxPaymaster`, `ZbxPayId`, `ZbxSmartWallet`, `BridgeMultisig`, `BridgeVault`, `RewardPool`, `TokenRegistry`, `Multicall3`, `ZRC20*` — auditing these for owner-pattern usage is S18-PHASE-2 (separately tracked); they were either out-of-scope for this S16-deferred close (V1 of `ZbxPayId` uses `address public immutable owner`, intentional; smart wallets use `onlyOwnerOrEntryPoint` which is a different model; multisigs use threshold not single-owner) or had no owner-pattern.

### S18-POLISH-1 — Integration test gap closed

Architect PASS-conditional review of the S18 migration flagged ONE non-blocking polish item: "no explicit integration tests were added for migrated contracts' auth behavior (especially ZbxRewardDistributor dual-role path and constructor wiring differences like ZbxTvlOracle(owner_))." Architect confirmed "this is a gap, but not by itself a proven blocking defect."

Polish applied: NEW `contracts/test/Ownable2StepMigration.t.sol` (218 LOC, 11 integration tests) covering the architect's specific concerns:

| # | Test | Concern addressed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `test_TvlOracle_ConstructorTakesExplicitOwner` | Verify ZbxTvlOracle's distinct `constructor(address owner_) Ownable2Step(owner_)` propagates the explicit arg, NOT defaults to msg.sender |
| 2 | `test_TvlOracle_RejectsZeroOwnerInConstructor` | Verify base bootstrap require-string surfaces correctly through a derived contract |
| 3 | `test_TvlOracle_TwoStepHandshakeEndToEnd` | End-to-end transferOwnership → acceptOwnership → former-owner-locked-out on a real migrated contract (proves the abstract base wires correctly when inherited) |
| 4 | `test_RewardDistributor_OwnerIsDeployerAfterMigration` | Verify constructor `Ownable2Step(msg.sender)` propagates through inheritance |
| 5 | `test_RewardDistributor_StakingCanCallDistribute` | Dual-role gate accepts STAKING (verified via try/catch boundary on auth-string) |
| 6 | `test_RewardDistributor_OwnerCanCallDistribute` | Dual-role gate accepts inherited `owner` (the migration's storage-slot read works) |

| 9 | `test_StabilityPool_MultiInheritance_OnlyOwnerOnSetVault` | Multi-inheritance (ReentrancyGuard + Ownable2Step): the inherited onlyOwner modifier from the second base correctly reads the inherited owner storage slot |

Notes on the dual-role tests (5 + 6): `distributeBlockReward` may revert downstream on the FAKE_ZBX transfer, but the test assertions check ONLY that the auth-string reason is NOT raised. This isolates the auth gate from incidental post-auth reverts.

## S19-PAYABLE-CALL — `.transfer(...)` → `.call{value:...}("")` migration (S16-deferred B)

**Date**: 2026-05-02
**Scope**: Migrate every native-ETH outbound transfer off the legacy
`payable(x).transfer(amount)` form (gas-stipended to 2300, historically
broken by EIP-2929 cold-account access cost rebalancing) onto
`(bool ok, ) = payable(x).call{value: amount}(""); require(ok, "...");`
which forwards all remaining gas.

### Why now

The 2300-gas stipend on `.transfer(...)` was sized in 2016 against a
storage-write cost of 5000 gas. EIP-2929 (Berlin, 2021) made the FIRST
`SLOAD` / `SSTORE` against a cold storage slot cost 2100 / 22100 gas
respectively. A receiving contract that does ANY non-trivial bookkeeping
(`balance += value`, an event emit with two indexed params, a multi-sig
nonce bump) will now revert on `.transfer(...)` even though it has
plenty of gas to spare in `.call(...)`. This silently bricks fund flows
to multi-sigs and ERC-4337 smart wallets — a Tier-0 issue for an L1.

### Sites migrated (4 actual native-ETH `.transfer` calls — ERC20 `.transfer` is unchanged)

| File | Function | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `ZbxBundler.sol:deregisterBundler` | refund staker | CEI preserved + new `nonReentrant` defense-in-depth |
| `ZbxBundler.sol:slash` | send slashed amount to immutable owner | CEI preserved (state decrement before send); owner is immutable, no role-rotation risk |
| `ZbxSmartWallet.sol:validateUserOp` | pay EntryPoint prefund | ERC-4337 §5 canonical pattern: forward all gas, suppress return bool, `EntryPoint` itself verifies receipt and reverts the UserOp on failure (a `require(ok, ...)` here would violate the validateUserOp "MUST NOT revert except on signature/nonce mismatch" rule) |
| `ZbxPayId.sol:withdraw` | drain registration-fee balance to immutable owner | New `nonReentrant`; owner is immutable so target is fixed at deploy |

### ReentrancyGuard inheritance added

`ZbxBundler` and `ZbxPayId` did not previously inherit any reentrancy
primitive (none of their flows did external calls before S19). With the
move to `.call` they now do, so both gain `import { ReentrancyGuard }
from "./libraries/ReentrancyGuard.sol";` and `is ReentrancyGuard`. The
existing single-slot `_status = 1/2` library at
`contracts/libraries/ReentrancyGuard.sol` is reused (already in
production use by `ZusdStabilityPool`, `ZRC20Staking`, `ZbxRouter`).

`ZbxSmartWallet` is intentionally NOT given `ReentrancyGuard`:
- `validateUserOp` is restricted to `msg.sender == entryPoint`, which is
  itself a singleton set at deploy time and audited.
- ERC-4337 §5 EntryPoint already enforces that `validateUserOp` can be
  called only once per UserOp. Adding `nonReentrant` would cost a
  storage slot and gas without addressing any threat the EntryPoint
  doesn't already mitigate.
- The other smart-wallet flows (`execute`, `executeBatch`) are already
  guarded by `onlyOwnerOrEntryPoint` and use raw `.call` correctly.

### CEI ordering audit (per migrated site)

| Site | State write before send | Reentrancy via `.call` recipient | Worst-case impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deregisterBundler` | ✅ stake=0, active=false | Receiver could re-enter via `receive()` → call `deregisterBundler()` again. `nonReentrant` blocks; even without it, second call reverts on `require(bundlerActive[msg.sender])`. | None |
| `slash` | ✅ stake -= amount | Owner is immutable. Receiver could call back into any other `external` fn but state already settled. | None |
| `withdraw` (PayId) | (none — drains balance) | Owner is immutable. `nonReentrant` blocks recursive `withdraw`; second call would `address(this).balance == 0` no-op anyway. | None |
| `validateUserOp` (SW) | (none — no state mutated) | EntryPoint is singleton + audited. Per ERC-4337, EntryPoint will not re-enter during prefund. | None |

### Tests

NEW `contracts/test/PayableCallMigration.t.sol` (~280 LOC, **9 HEVM
tests** + 4 helper contracts):

| # | Test | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `Bundler_Deregister_HappyPath_EoaReceiver` | Basic refund path on a plain payable receiver |
| 2 | `Bundler_Deregister_SucceedsOnGasGriefingReceiver` | The whole point of S19 — a receiver that consumes >>2300 gas in `receive()` succeeds under `.call`, would have failed under `.transfer` |
| 3 | `Bundler_Deregister_RevertsOnRejectingReceiver` | `.call` surfaces receiver reverts via `require(ok, ...)`; state must be untouched (atomic) |
| 4 | `Bundler_Deregister_NonReentrant` | Reentrancy attacker that tries to re-enter `deregisterBundler` from `receive()` is blocked |
| 5 | `Bundler_Slash_TransfersToOwner` | Owner receives slashed amount; bundler stake decrements correctly |
| 6 | `Bundler_Slash_RevertsOnRejectingOwner` | `.call` surfaces owner-revert cleanly via `require(ok, ...)` |
| 7 | `PayId_Withdraw_HappyPath` | Owner drains registration-fee balance |
| 8 | `PayId_Withdraw_OnlyOwner` | Non-owner cannot drain |
| 9 | `PayId_Withdraw_RevertsOnRejectingOwner` | `.call` surfaces rejecting-owner revert; balance unchanged on atomic revert |

`ZbxSmartWallet.validateUserOp` is exercised via the existing ERC-4337
EntryPoint integration tests in `ZbxEntryPoint.t.sol` (`forge test`
will cover it transitively); a dedicated unit test would require a
full mock EntryPoint setup that already exists in that file.

### Static-review deploy gate

- ✅ `check-chain-id`: clean (175 allowlisted hits)
- ✅ `check-orphans`: clean (`PayableCallMigration.t.sol` reachable; no new orphans)

### S19-EXTENSION-1 — Additional `payable.transfer` sites discovered

After the initial S19 migration the post-edit `rg 'payable\([^)]+\)\.transfer\('` audit revealed **3 additional sites** not in the original master plan (which listed 4). Per the user mandate ("ye sab fixed and secure karo"), the migration was extended to cover all 7 sites in a single S19 close.

| File:line | Function | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| `ZbxFaucet.sol:76` | `withdraw()` (owner drains faucet balance) | `.transfer` → `.call`; reused the file's pre-existing inline `nonReentrant` modifier (already decorating `request()`). No `ReentrancyGuard` library import needed because the inline guard predates this work. |
| `ZRC20Factory.sol:68` | `createToken()` (refund excess `msg.value` to caller) | `.transfer` → `.call` |
| `ZRC20Factory.sol:72` | `createToken()` (forward `creationFee` to treasury) | `.transfer` → `.call` |

#### ZRC20Factory CEI gotcha (resolved)

`createToken()` had broken CEI ordering pre-S19: refund + treasury transfers happened BEFORE the `allTokens.push(token)` / `tokensByCreator[msg.sender].push(token)` state writes. Under the legacy `.transfer(...)` 2300-gas stipend the receiver could not realistically reenter, so the ordering was practically (if not theoretically) safe. Under `.call(...)` a malicious caller's `receive()` could reenter `createToken` and spawn additional tokens BEFORE the first call's state was recorded, double-counting array indexes and breaking enumeration invariants for indexers (`/v1/tokens?creator=...`).

**Fix**: added `import { ReentrancyGuard } from "./libraries/ReentrancyGuard.sol";`, made `ZRC20Factory is ReentrancyGuard`, and decorated `createToken` with `nonReentrant`. Functional ordering is preserved (refund → fee → CREATE2 deploy → register) so:
- CREATE2 salt + address determinism remain identical
- `TokenCreated` event order remains identical
- Off-chain indexers do not need a re-sync

#### Inline-guard heterogeneity note

The codebase now has TWO reentrancy-guard mechanisms:
1. The shared library `contracts/libraries/ReentrancyGuard.sol` (used by `ZusdStabilityPool`, `ZRC20Staking`, `ZbxRouter`, NEW `ZbxBundler`, NEW `ZbxPayId`, NEW `ZRC20Factory`).
2. Inlined `_status = 1/2` patterns in `BridgeVault`, `ZbxAMM`, `ZbxEntryPoint`, `ZbxFaucet`, `ZbxLendingPool`, `ZbxStaking`, `ZusdVault`.

The S19 close intentionally did NOT migrate the inlined guards onto the library, even where the file (`ZbxFaucet`) was being touched. Rationale: scope creep risk + brace-parity ripple across 7 high-touch files. A dedicated hardening PR (separately tracked: "S19-FOLLOWUP-INLINE-GUARDS") will lift all 7 onto the library so the audit surface collapses to a single file. The library docstring already calls out this pending migration.

### Final S19 site inventory (7 of 7 migrated)

| # | File:line | Function | New guard added | CEI status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ZbxBundler.sol:96` | `deregisterBundler` | library `nonReentrant` | ✅ already correct |
| 2 | `ZbxBundler.sol:137` | `slash` | (owner-only path; no new guard needed) | ✅ already correct |
| 3 | `ZbxSmartWallet.sol:81` | `validateUserOp` (prefund) | (none — ERC-4337 §5 forbids reverts here; EntryPoint enforces) | ✅ no state mutation |
| 4 | `ZbxPayId.sol:273` | `withdraw` | library `nonReentrant` | ✅ no state mutation |
| 5 | `ZbxFaucet.sol:81` | `withdraw` | inline `nonReentrant` (pre-existing in file) | ✅ no state mutation |
| 6 | `ZRC20Factory.sol:68` | `createToken` refund | library `nonReentrant` (NEW for whole fn) | ✅ now correct (was broken under .call without guard) |
| 7 | `ZRC20Factory.sol:72` | `createToken` treasury fee | library `nonReentrant` (NEW for whole fn) | ✅ now correct |

- ✅ Brace parity: all 6 touched files (`ZbxBundler`, `ZbxSmartWallet`, `ZbxPayId`, `ZbxFaucet`, `ZRC20Factory`, `PayableCallMigration.t.sol`) balanced
- ✅ ZERO `payable(...).transfer(...)` remaining anywhere in `contracts/*.sol`
- ✅ `ReentrancyGuard` correctly imported in 3 inheritors (`ZbxBundler`, `ZbxPayId`, `ZRC20Factory`); `ZbxFaucet` correctly reuses its inline guard

### S19-POLISH-1 — Test logic fixes per architect review

Architect review #1 returned **FAIL (deploy-gate)** on test logic correctness while passing the contract migration itself. Two material test issues identified and fixed in-place:

#### Polish-1a: `test_Factory_NonReentrant_PreventsDoubleSpawn` — wrong revert expectation

**Problem**: My original test expected the outer `createToken` to revert with `"ZRC20Factory: treasury fee failed"`. This was logically inconsistent with the attacker's design: `FactoryReentrancyAttacker.receive()` swallows the inner reentry revert via `try/catch`, so `receive()` itself returns normally. The treasury `.call` to the attacker therefore returns `TRUE`, and the outer `createToken` SUCCEEDS. The test as written would have spuriously passed only if `expectRevert` matched the WRONG revert (or worse, masked a real bug).

**Fix**: Test now asserts:
1. Outer `createToken` returns a non-zero `tok` address (success).
2. `factory.allTokens(0) == tok` AND `factory.allTokens(1)` reverts out-of-bounds (proves exactly one token, no double-spawn).
3. `attacker.attemptedReentry()` is true.
4. `attacker.reentryReverted()` is true.
5. **NEW**: `attacker.capturedRevertReason()` equals `"ReentrancyGuard: reentrant call"` — this is the canonical message from `contracts/libraries/ReentrancyGuard.sol`. The exact-match assertion proves the guard fired and not some other check (e.g. CREATE2 collision, downstream `require`).

`FactoryReentrancyAttacker` was extended with `string public capturedRevertReason` and the `try/catch Error(string)` capture path.

#### Polish-1b: `test_Bundler_Deregister_NonReentrant` — non-specific assertion

**Problem**: Original test relied on `expectRevert(bytes("ZbxBundler: stake refund failed"))`. While the assertion would pass IF the guard fired (because the attacker's reentry would revert → outer `.call` → outer `require(ok, ...)` → "stake refund failed"), it would ALSO pass for any other cause that made the inner call revert (e.g. the `require(bundlerActive[msg.sender], ...)` check, since state was already cleared). The architect noted this proves "failure propagation, not specifically nonReentrant".

**Fix**: Same pattern as Polish-1a:
1. `ReentrancyAttackerBundler` extended with `try/catch Error(string)` to swallow + capture inner revert reason.
2. Outer `attackDeregister()` now SUCCEEDS (attacker returns normally → outer `.call` true).
3. Test asserts canonical `"ReentrancyGuard: reentrant call"` exact match.
4. Plus state-cleared assertions on the OUTER deregister to prove it ran to completion.

This conclusively distinguishes guard-blocked reentry from any other revert source.

- ZRC20Factory `nonReentrant` is sufficient; full CEI reorder is optional hardening, not required.
- ZbxBundler.slash without nonReentrant is acceptable (immutable owner, owner-only auth, state decrement before send).
- `ok;` in ZbxSmartWallet is canonical eth-infinitism style; Solidity 0.8.24 accepts.
- ZbxFaucet inline-guard heterogeneity is acceptable for this scoped migration; uniform migration is deferred to S19-FOLLOWUP-INLINE-GUARDS.
