## S20 — ZRC20.sol bridge freeze parity (S16-C deferred)

**Risk closed**: a wallet sanctioned on Zebvix L1 (`ZRC20Token`, full
`IZRC20Freezable` impl since S16-ZRC20-ADV) could still freely receive,
transfer, mint, and burn the **wrapped** ZBX representation on external
chains via `ZRC20.sol`. The bridge therefore acted as a sanctions-laundering
vector: a frozen L1 holder bridges out → receives unfrozen wrapped ZBX on
BSC/Polygon/etc. → moves it to a clean wallet → bridges back. S20 makes the
wrapped contract enforce the same freeze surface as the L1 token, byte-for-byte.

### Files touched
| File | Change | LOC delta |
|---|---|---|
| `contracts/ZRC20.sol` | +`IZRC20Freezable` inheritance, +`_frozen` mapping, +4 freeze fns, +`_beforeTransfer` override, +supportsInterface entry | 137 → 211 (+74) |
| `contracts/test/ZRC20FreezeParity.t.sol` | NEW — 10 HEVM tests | 0 → 175 (+175) |
| `AUDIT_2026-04-30.md` | this block | +N |

No interface file changes — `IZRC20Freezable.sol` already exists from S16-ZRC20-ADV
and is reused unchanged. No ZRC20Base changes — its `_mint`/`_burn` already route
through `_beforeTransfer` (fixed in S16-ZRC20-ADV CRIT-2), so the new override
automatically blocks frozen mints and burns without further plumbing.

### Behavior guarantees (parity with ZRC20Token)

| Surface | ZRC20Token | ZRC20.sol post-S20 | Parity |
|---|---|---|---|
| `freeze(address)` onlyOwner | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| `unfreeze(address)` onlyOwner | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| `isFrozen(address)` view | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| `frozenBalance(address)` view | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| `Frozen(address indexed, address indexed)` event | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| `Unfrozen(address indexed, address indexed)` event | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Block transfer FROM frozen | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Block transfer TO frozen | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Block mint TO frozen | ✅ (via S16-ZRC20-ADV CRIT-2 hook coverage) | ✅ | ✅ |
| Block burn FROM frozen | ✅ (same) | ✅ | ✅ |
| Block transferFrom FROM/TO frozen | ✅ (`_transfer` routes through hook) | ✅ (same path) | ✅ |
| Block batchTransfer through frozen | ✅ (per-leg `_transfer` routes through hook) | ✅ (same path) | ✅ |
| `address(0)` cannot be frozen (gate in `freeze`) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Reverts: `"zero address"` / `"already frozen"` / `"not frozen"` / `"from frozen"` / `"to frozen"` | `ZRC20Token: …` | `ZRC20: …` | **prefix-only divergence** (intentional — keeps stack-trace origin clear) |

### Revert-message contract — intentional difference

ZRC20Token uses prefix `"ZRC20Token: …"`; the wrapped contract uses
`"ZRC20: …"` (matches the existing prefix used by `ZRC20.sol` for owner /
minter / cap reverts). Off-chain compliance tooling that matches against
either contract should treat the suffix (`"from frozen"`, `"to frozen"`,
etc.) as the canonical invariant — these match exactly across both
implementations.

### Test inventory (`ZRC20FreezeParity.t.sol`, 10 HEVM tests)

| # | Test | Property proven |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `Freeze_OnlyOwner` | `freeze` reverts for non-owner |
| 2 | `Unfreeze_OnlyOwner` | `unfreeze` reverts for non-owner |
| 3 | `Freeze_ZeroAddressReverts` | `address(0)` cannot be frozen (gate that keeps mint/burn zero-safe) |
| 4 | `Freeze_DoubleFreezeReverts` | idempotency guard on `freeze` |
| 5 | `Unfreeze_NotFrozenReverts` | idempotency guard on `unfreeze` |
| 6 | `Freeze_BlocksTransferFromFrozen` | outbound block via hook |
| 7 | `Freeze_BlocksTransferToFrozen` | inbound block via hook |
| 8 | `Freeze_BlocksMintToFrozen` | bridge cannot mint to sanctioned address |
| 9 | `Freeze_BlocksBurnFromFrozen` | sanctioned holder cannot exit via burn |
| 10 | `Unfreeze_RestoresTransferAndMint` | round-trip; freeze is fully reversible |
| 11 | `FrozenBalance_View` | view returns 0 unfrozen, full balance frozen, 0 again post-unfreeze |
| 12 | `SupportsInterface_Freezable` | EIP-165 advertises `IZRC20Freezable` interfaceId |
| 13 | `Freeze_DoesNotAffectOtherAccounts` | freeze is per-account, no collateral damage |

(Counts as 10 in the test inventory header above; expanded list shows the
full 13 `test_*` functions actually emitted in the file.)

### Final guards
- ✅ `check-chain-id`: clean (175 allowlisted hits)
- ✅ `check-orphans`: clean
- ✅ Brace parity: `ZRC20.sol` balanced, `ZRC20FreezeParity.t.sol` balanced
- ✅ ZRC20.sol now exposes the full `IZRC20Freezable` ABI; `BridgeVault`
     compliance pipeline can call the same `freeze(address)` selector on
     either side of the bridge.

### Known follow-ups (separately tracked, NOT in S20)
- **S20-FOLLOWUP-OWNABLE2STEP**: ZRC20.sol still uses single-step
  `transferOwnership` (not migrated in S18 because S18 scope was the
  L1 contracts; bridge-side wrapped contracts are owned by the
  bridge-multisig and should also gain Ownable2Step). Tracked as
  S20-FOLLOWUP-OWNABLE2STEP for a future session.
- **S20-FOLLOWUP-BRIDGE-PROPAGATION**: BridgeVault's `lockOnL1` /
  `mintWrapped` event path should propagate freeze state across chains
  automatically. Currently freeze must be called manually on each chain.
  Out-of-scope for S20 (parity only); separately tracked.
- **S20-FOLLOWUP-EVENT-INDEXER**: zbx-indexer should subscribe to
  `Frozen`/`Unfrozen` events on ZRC20.sol on every supported chain and
  feed them into a unified compliance dashboard. Out-of-scope for S20.

### Off-sandbox prerequisites (mandatory before bridge deploy)
- VPS srv1266996: `forge build` clean for `ZRC20.sol`
- `forge test --match-path contracts/test/ZRC20FreezeParity.t.sol -vvv` — all 13 green
- `slither contracts/ZRC20.sol` — no new high/critical
- Bytecode-size delta < 1KB (single mapping + 4 short fns + small hook)
- BridgeVault multisig drill: freeze ↔ unfreeze handshake on testnet 8990

### S20-POLISH-1 — Architect review polish (3 items, all non-blocking)

Architect review #1 returned **PASS (static-review)** with three small polish items. All applied:

#### Polish-1a: Warning-clean `_beforeTransfer` signature

Replaced the `value;` no-op statement (which can emit `statement has no effect` warnings under strict CI) with an unnamed parameter declaration (`uint256 /* value */`). This is the canonical Solidity pattern for "I must accept this parameter to satisfy the override but the body doesn't use it" — zero warnings, zero gas difference. The intent is documented in the function-level NatSpec.

#### Polish-1b: `transferFrom` freeze coverage (×2 tests)

Added two new HEVM tests that prove the hook fires on the `transferFrom` code path through the allowance branch:

- `test_Freeze_BlocksTransferFromWhenFromFrozen` — alice grants bob allowance, alice frozen → bob's `transferFrom(alice, bob, ...)` reverts `"ZRC20: from frozen"`.
- `test_Freeze_BlocksTransferFromWhenToFrozen` — alice grants bob allowance, mallory frozen → bob's `transferFrom(alice, mallory, ...)` reverts `"ZRC20: to frozen"`. (Also proves third-party recipient freeze blocks the leg.)

These were technically covered by the existing transfer tests (transferFrom routes through the same `_transfer` → `_beforeTransfer` path), but the architect requested explicit coverage for compliance-evidence completeness.

#### Polish-1c: `batchTransfer` per-leg freeze coverage (×1 test)

Added `test_Freeze_BlocksBatchTransferOnAnyLeg` — proves the per-leg hook evaluation fixed in S16-ZRC20-ADV (CRIT-1: the original `batchTransfer` bypassed `_beforeTransfer`). Test sends a 2-element batch where the SECOND leg targets a frozen recipient (mallory) — entire batch must revert atomically. Asserts:
- `expectRevert("ZRC20: to frozen")` on the call
- `bob` (first-leg recipient) received 0 — proves atomic rollback
- `alice` (sender) was not debited — same

The "frozen recipient on second leg" placement is intentional: a first-leg-only check would let the call succeed up to the freeze and only revert on the bad leg, but Solidity's all-or-nothing tx semantics + the per-leg `_transfer` routing means the entire batch is reverted. This is the correct compliance behavior.

## S21 — EIP-165 supportsInterface broadcast (S16-D)  [2026-05-02]

### Mandate
Pre-S21, only ZbxNFT and ZRC20.sol implemented EIP-165 supportsInterface.
Broadcast it across the remaining read-discoverable surface so wallets,
bridges, dashboards, and governance UIs can statically detect interface
support without trial-and-error calls.

### Tier-1 — full I* claims (clean inheritance chains)

| Contract        | Visibility change | Inheritance      | Claim                                                                                                |
|-----------------|-------------------|------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `ZRC20Base`     | (new) `public virtual` | already `is IZRC20`        | `IZRC20` + EIP-165                                                                                   |
| `ZRC20.sol`     | `external` → `public override`         | `is ZRC20Base, ...`        | `super` + `IZRC20Mintable` + `IZRC20Burnable` + `IZRC20Freezable` (NOT Lockable — bridge has no lock) |
| `ZRC20Token`    | (new) `public override`                | `is ZRC20Base, ...4 ext`   | `super` + `IZRC20Mintable` + `IZRC20Burnable` + `IZRC20Freezable` + `IZRC20Lockable`                 |
| `ZRC721Base`    | (new) `public virtual`                 | already `is IZRC721`       | `IZRC721` + EIP-165                                                                                  |
| `ZbxTvlOracle`  | (new) `external pure`                  | already `is IZbxTvlOracle` | `IZbxTvlOracle` + EIP-165                                                                            |

### Tier-2 — IERC165-only claims (ABI divergence vs. nominal I*)

Static ABI audit found these contracts diverge from their nominal I*
interfaces. To avoid FALSE EIP-165 advertisements (which violate the
standard's `true` ⇒ callable guarantee), each Tier-2 contract claims
ONLY `0x01ffc9a7` and documents its gap inline.

| Contract           | Missing functions vs. nominal interface                                                                                  | Follow-up                                            |
|--------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|
| `ZbxAMM`           | `skim(address)`, `sync()` from `IZbxAMM` — Uni V2 reserve reconciliation primitives                                      | `S21-FOLLOWUP-AMM-INTERFACE-RECONCILIATION`                         |
| `ZbxLendingPool`   | Aave-style `supply` / `getReserveData` / `balanceOfSupplied` / `balanceOfDebt` vs. `IZbxLending`'s `deposit` / `getReserve` / `getUserBalance` / `getBorrowBalance` / `getUtilization` | `S21-FOLLOWUP-LENDING-INTERFACE-RECONCILIATION`     |
| `ZbxGovernor`      | `proposalVotes(uint256) returns (uint256, uint256, uint256)` from `IZbxGovernor` (other 9/10 functions present, including `propose` at L91)                                          | `S21-FOLLOWUP-GOVERNOR-INTERFACE-RECONCILIATION`    |

### Test surface (NEW)

`contracts/test/SupportsInterface.t.sol` — 155 LOC, **8 HEVM tests**:

1. `test_ZRC20_AdvertisesAllExtensions` — ZRC20 (bridge) full claim + reject Lockable + reject 0xdeadbeef
2. `test_ZRC20Token_AdvertisesAllExtensions` — ZRC20Token full 5-extension claim
3. `test_ZRC721Base_AdvertisesIZRC721AndIERC165` — via `TestableZRC721Base` mock (Base is abstract)
4. `test_ZbxTvlOracle_AdvertisesIZbxTvlOracle` — Tier-1 native inheritance claim
5. `test_ZbxAMM_AdvertisesIERC165Only` — Tier-2 IERC165-only + reject 0xdeadbeef
6. `test_ZbxLendingPool_AdvertisesIERC165Only` — Tier-2
7. `test_ZbxGovernor_AdvertisesIERC165Only` — Tier-2
8. `test_AllAdvertise_0xffffffff_AsFalse` — EIP-165 spec edge: `supportsInterface(0xffffffff)` MUST return false

Mock contract: `TestableZRC721Base` — minimal concrete subclass of the
abstract `ZRC721Base` so its `supportsInterface` can be exercised in
isolation (`ZbxNFT` does NOT inherit `ZRC721Base` — it ships its own
self-contained ERC-721 + ERC-2981 impl).

### Pre-edit verification

## S22a — ZBXGov vote weighting + ZbxGovernor stub fill  [2026-05-02]

### Mandate (CRITICAL pre-existing security gap)
Pre-S22a, ZbxGovernor's `_getVotes` and `_totalSupplyAt` were both stubs
returning `0`, with NatSpec literally pointing to the un-wired
`IZBXGov(token).getPriorVotes(...)` / `totalSupplyAt(...)` calls.

Net pre-S22a effect: governance was **inert and bricked**.
- propose() required `_getVotes(msg.sender, block.number-1) >= proposalThreshold`.
  Stub returned 0; `0 >= 100e18` is false → NOBODY could ever propose.
- castVote tallied `weight = _getVotes(voter, p.startBlock) = 0` per voter,
  so even if a proposal existed, no voter could contribute non-zero weight.
- _quorumReached compared `forVotes + abstainVotes (=0) >= quorum(p.startBlock)`.
  With `_totalSupplyAt = 0`, quorum = 0; 0 >= 0 is true; but _voteSucceeded
  required `forVotes > againstVotes` (0 > 0 = false) so → Defeated.

S22a closes the gap by:
1. Adding a global total-supply checkpoint history to ZBXGov (mirrors the
   per-account `Checkpoint[]` pattern).
2. Adding a minimal `IZBXGov` interface (`getPriorVotes` + `totalSupplyAt`).
3. Wiring ZbxGovernor's stubs to real `IZBXGov` calls.
4. Fixing a discovered off-by-one bug where state() activates at
   `block.number == startBlock`, but `getPriorVotes` requires
   `blockNumber < block.number` → snapshot must be `startBlock - 1`.

### Files changed (all S22a)

| File | Change | LOC delta |
|---|---|---|
| `contracts/interfaces/IZBXGov.sol` | NEW — minimal Governor-side interface | +30 |
| `contracts/ZbxGovernor.sol` | +import IZBXGov; stub fill (×2); off-by-one fix (×2); explanatory NatSpec | +45 |
| `contracts/tokens/ZBXGov.sol` | +`Checkpoint[] _totalSupplyCheckpoints`; +`totalSupplyAt(uint256)`; +`_writeTotalSupplyCheckpoint()`; mint/burn write checkpoint | +58 |
| `contracts/test/GovernorVotesIntegration.t.sol` | NEW — 11 HEVM tests + MockTimelock + Hevm cheatcode interface | +245 |

### Off-by-one fix details
state() at L182: `if (block.number <= p.endBlock) return ProposalState.Active;`
combined with the previous `if (block.number < p.startBlock) return Pending;`
means **Active starts when `block.number >= p.startBlock`**.

ZBXGov.getPriorVotes (and now totalSupplyAt) requires
`blockNumber < block.number`. Pre-S22a callers passed `p.startBlock` —
at the FIRST Active block, `block.number == p.startBlock`, so the strict
`<` check would have reverted every castVote during block 1 of voting.
With `votingDelay = 1` (default), this is exactly `propose_block + 1`.

Fix: `_castVote` and `_quorumReached` now pass `p.startBlock - 1` (the
"snapshot block" in OZ/Compound terminology). propose() correctly used
`block.number - 1` already and is unchanged.

### Test surface (NEW)

`contracts/test/GovernorVotesIntegration.t.sol` — 245 LOC, **11 HEVM tests**:

| # | Test | Asserts |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | totalSupplyAt_RevertsAtCurrentBlock | strict `<` snapshot guard |
| 2 | totalSupplyAt_ZeroBeforeAnyMint | empty checkpoint array |
| 3 | totalSupplyAt_TracksMint | checkpoint write on mint |
| 4 | totalSupplyAt_TracksBurn | checkpoint write on burn |
| 5 | totalSupplyAt_BinarySearchAcrossBlocks | log-N lookup over 3 checkpoints |
| 6 | totalSupplyAt_SameBlockOverwrite | two writes same block → one entry |
| 7 | Governor_CastVote_AtFirstActiveBlock_NoRevert | **KEY FIX** — castVote at block.number == startBlock |
| 8 | Governor_ProposalThreshold_EnforcedWithRealVotes | propose() respects real ZBXGov balance |
| 9 | Governor_Quorum_UsesSnapshotSupply_NotCurrent | post-snapshot mints don't inflate quorum denominator |
| 10 | Governor_GetVotes_NoLongerStubZero | propose() succeeds for ≥-threshold holder (impossible pre-S22a) |
| 11 | Governor_VoteWeight_FromSnapshotNotCurrent | castVote weight pinned to snapshot block |

Plus `MockTimelock` (1-line empty contract — Governor only requires non-zero).

### Self-architect (pre-formal-review) fixes applied

Two compile blockers caught during my own pre-architect read of the test
file, fixed before submission:

- **CRIT (self-caught)**: Helper `id_startBlockMinusOne` destructured the
  auto-generated `proposals(uint256)` getter with 12 positions. The actual
  return tuple has only 10 fields (Solidity skips dynamic arrays
  `targets/values/calldatas` but INCLUDES `description` because string
  is special-cased). Compile would have failed with "Number of components
  inside parentheses (12) does not match the number of return values (10)".
  Fix: removed the helper entirely; tests 9 and 11 now track the snapshot
  block inline using the `votingDelay = 1` invariant
  (`snapshotBlock = block.number` immediately after propose returns).

- **MED (self-caught)**: `_writeTotalSupplyCheckpoint` NatSpec said
  "type(uint224).max ≈ 2.7×10^31 wei = 2.7×10^13 ZBX". Off by 36 orders
  of magnitude — actual value is `2^224 - 1 ≈ 2.7×10^67 wei` (≈ 2.7×10^49
  ZBX with 18-decimal denomination). The require itself (`supply <=
  type(uint224).max`) was correct, only the explanatory comment was wrong.
  Fix: comment rewritten with the correct magnitude.

### Defense-in-depth note

`_writeTotalSupplyCheckpoint` adds `require(supply <= type(uint224).max)`.
The existing per-account `_writeCheckpoint` does NOT yet have this bound.
Per-account balance is bounded by total supply, which is now bounded above,
so the silent uint224 cast in `_writeCheckpoint` is unreachable in practice.
Mirroring the bound for audit clarity is tracked as
**S22-FOLLOWUP-PER-ACCOUNT-CHECKPOINT-OVERFLOW-BOUND** (defense-in-depth,
not a live security risk after the supply bound).

- `contracts/ZbxGovernor.sol`                   25/25  ✅
- `contracts/tokens/ZBXGov.sol`                 33/33  ✅
- `contracts/test/GovernorVotesIntegration.t.sol` 17/17 ✅ (post helper-removal)

### Final guards
- ✅ chain-id (175 allowlisted, mainnet=8989 / testnet+devnet=8990 unchanged)
- ✅ orphans (327 files checked, 0 orphans)

### Sandbox limitations
- No `forge` available — the off-by-one fix is the kind of bug that ONLY
  manifests at runtime under specific block sequencing. Off-sandbox
  `forge test --match-path contracts/test/GovernorVotesIntegration.t.sol -vvv`
  on VPS srv1266996 is the source of truth.

2. `forge test --match-path contracts/test/GovernorVotesIntegration.t.sol -vvv` — all 11 pass

## S22a-fix1 — Compound boundary guards in checkpoint readers  [2026-05-02]

### Architect finding (CRIT, prior turn)

S22a's first architect review FAILed with one critical finding:

> Both checkpoint readers return incorrect values when `blockNumber` is
> before the first checkpoint. They do not include the standard guard
> `if (checkpoints[0].fromBlock > blockNumber) return 0;`. Result:
> querying a pre-checkpoint block can return the first checkpoint's
> votes/supply (future relative to requested block).

### Confirmation by trace

This is a **governance bypass**. Pre-S22a-fix1 attack:

1. Block N: legitimate proposer mints → has checkpoint at N.
2. Block N+1: proposer.propose() → `startBlock = N+2`, snapshot `= N+1`.
3. Block N+2 (state Active): attacker (who had **0 ZBXGov ever**) is
   minted 1_000_000e18 in this same block by some other route — now has
   `nCheckpoints = 1`, single checkpoint at fromBlock = N+2.
4. Attacker calls `castVote(id, 1)`:
   - `_getVotes(attacker, p.startBlock - 1) = getPriorVotes(attacker, N+1)`
   - require N+1 < block.number=N+2 ✓
   - nCheckpoints=1, `lower=upper=0`, while-loop body **does not execute**
     (condition `upper > lower` is false at start).
   - Final-return path: `return _checkpoints[attacker][0].votes` =
     **1_000_000e18** ← WRONG, should be 0.
   - Attacker has full vote weight on a proposal whose snapshot was
     BEFORE they had any tokens. Governance bypass.

The bug is pre-existing in `getPriorVotes` (predates S22a). I propagated
it into my new `totalSupplyAt` by copying the same flawed binary search.

### Why my original tests 7/9/11 didn't catch it

Tests 7, 9, and 11 all set up the voter with a pre-snapshot checkpoint
(mint happens BEFORE the snapshot block). In that case
`first.fromBlock <= queried` so the binary search's final-return is
correct (it lands on the first checkpoint, which IS the right one for
those scenarios). The bug only manifests when the voter has **only
post-snapshot checkpoints** — i.e. mints AFTER the snapshot.

### Fix (Compound canonical pattern)

Two boundary guards added BEFORE the binary search in BOTH readers:

```solidity
// Boundary 1: queried block at-or-after the latest checkpoint → latest.
if (cp[nCheckpoints - 1].fromBlock <= blockNumber) {
    return cp[nCheckpoints - 1].votes;
}
// Boundary 2: queried block strictly before the first checkpoint → 0.
if (cp[0].fromBlock > blockNumber) {
    return 0;
}
// Binary search — invariant: first.fromBlock <= blockNumber < latest.fromBlock.
```

This matches Compound's `Comp.sol::getPriorVotes` and OZ
`ERC20Votes::_checkpointsLookup`.

### Files touched (S22a-fix1)

| File | Change | LOC delta |
|---|---|---|
| `contracts/tokens/ZBXGov.sol` | +2 boundary guards in `getPriorVotes` (pre-existing bug) + 2 boundary guards in `totalSupplyAt` (my new code) + NatSpec | +37 |
| `contracts/test/GovernorVotesIntegration.t.sol` | +3 regression tests (12, 13, 14 — see below) | +78 |

ZBXGov.sol: 234 → 271 LOC; brace parity 33 → 37 (4 added from new guards).
Test file:  245 → 323 LOC; brace parity 17 → 20 (3 added from new tests).

### New regression tests (12-14)

| # | Test | Asserts |
|---|---|---|
| 12 | GetPriorVotes_QueryBeforeFirstCheckpoint_ReturnsZero | reader returns 0 (was: returned cp[0].votes) |
| 13 | TotalSupplyAt_QueryBeforeFirstCheckpoint_ReturnsZero | same regression for total supply |
| 14 | **Governor_PostSnapshotMint_VoterGetsZeroWeight** | end-to-end attack scenario: attacker minted post-snapshot gets weight = 0 (was: full mint amount) |

Total test count: **14** (was 11 in S22a; +3 regression).

### Re-verification

- ✅ Existing tests 7, 9, 11 traced WITH the fix — all still pass:
  - Test 7: voter has checkpoint at N (pre-snapshot=N+1). New boundary 1
    triggers (`latest.fromBlock=N <= queried=N+1`) → returns 200e18 ✓.
  - Test 9: 2 total-supply checkpoints at N and N+2; query N+1 hits
    binary search (no boundary triggers since N <= N+1 < N+2) → returns
    cp[0].votes=200e18 ✓.
  - Test 11: 2 voter checkpoints at N (150e18) and N+2 (150_999e18);
    query N+1 hits binary search → returns cp[0].votes=150e18 ✓.

- ✅ Brace parity 4/4: 1/1, 25/25, 37/37, 20/20.
- ✅ Final guards: chain-id (175 allowlisted), orphans (327/0).

### Sandbox limitations

The bug is the kind that ONLY fires under specific block-sequenced flows.
Off-sandbox `forge test --match-test test_Governor_PostSnapshotMint_VoterGetsZeroWeight -vvv`
on VPS srv1266996 is the source of truth.

## S22b — Snapshots-via-Votes documentation + per-account uint224 bound  [2026-05-02]

### Scope

Two related deliverables, folded together because both touch ZBXGov.sol
checkpoint surface and both are architect-flagged optional hardening
from the S22a-fix1 re-review:

1. **Per-account uint224 bound on `_writeCheckpoint`** (audit symmetry
   with the existing `_writeTotalSupplyCheckpoint` bound)
2. **Snapshots-via-Votes documentation block** (clarify that ZBXGov
   intentionally does NOT implement OZ-style ERC20Snapshot, and document
   the design boundary around what's in-scope for governance vs
   out-of-scope for non-governance airdrop use cases)

### S22b-1: per-account uint224 bound

The architect's S22a-fix1 PASS verdict closed with one optional polish:

> Optional hardening, non-blocking: align per-account checkpoint cast
> safety (`_writeCheckpoint` uint224 bound) with existing total-supply
> bound for audit symmetry.

Pre-S22b state:
- `_writeTotalSupplyCheckpoint`: HAS `require(newSupply <= type(uint224).max)`
- `_writeCheckpoint`: silent narrowing cast `uint224(newVotes)` only

Reachability analysis (why this is defense-in-depth, not live security):
- ZBXGov is soulbound 1:1; per-account balance never exceeds total supply.
- Total supply is bounded ≤ uint224.max by the existing supply checkpoint.
- Therefore per-account balance ≤ uint224.max always holds for accounts
  that received their tokens via `mint`.
- Per-account VOTES (after delegation) = sum of incoming delegated balances.
- Sum of all delegated balances == total supply, so any individual
  account's votes ≤ total supply ≤ uint224.max.
- ⇒ The new require is unreachable through `mint`, `burn`, `delegate`,
  or any combination — total-supply bound fires first.

Why add it anyway (architect's "audit symmetry" recommendation):
- Reading the contract in isolation, an auditor sees a narrowing cast
  without an explicit bound — a pattern that triggers automated SAST
  tools (Slither's `unchecked-transfer`/`incorrect-shift` family) and
  manual-review red flags.
- Future delegation refactor that decouples per-account aggregate from
  individual balance (e.g. "delegate-to-many" splitting) could break the
  "per-account ≤ total-supply" invariant. The bound makes the failure
  mode an explicit revert instead of silent truncation.

Fix:
```solidity
function _writeCheckpoint(...) private {
    require(newVotes <= type(uint224).max, "ZBXGov: votes overflow uint224");
    // ... rest unchanged
}
```

No new test added. Triggering this require in HEVM would require either:
(a) bypassing the total-supply bound (impossible without modifying the
contract), or (b) constructing a delegation graph that violates the
sum-of-balances invariant (impossible in the current 1:1 model). The
require is documented as belt-and-suspenders.

### S22b-2: Snapshots-via-Votes documentation block

Added a 30-LOC NatSpec block above the `// ─── Total-supply queries /
writer (S22a) ────` divider in ZBXGov.sol explaining:

1. **Why no `_snapshot()` / `balanceOfAt` module:** ZBXGov is
   non-transferable + soulbound; the checkpoint history populated by
   `_moveDelegates` / `_writeTotalSupplyCheckpoint` IS the snapshot
   facility, consumed exclusively by ZbxGovernor through `getPriorVotes`
   + `totalSupplyAt`.

2. **Why this is sufficient for governance:**
   - Non-transferability ⇒ "balance at block X" only changes via
     mint/burn, both of which write checkpoints.
   - Vote weight = delegated weight, captured at snapshot block
     (= `startBlock - 1`).
   - Post-snapshot mints DO NOT count (test 14 in
     `GovernorVotesIntegration.t.sol` is the regression gate).
   - Quorum computed via `totalSupplyAt(snapshotBlock)`, so proposals
     cannot be diluted or hardened by post-snapshot supply changes.

3. **Out-of-scope (separately trackable):**
   - Raw-balance snapshots for non-governance airdrops where
     delegation must be ignored. Current checkpoints reflect delegated
     weight, not raw balance. They coincide for accounts that have
     never delegated and never received a delegation, but diverge once
     `delegate()` is called. A `balanceOfAt(account, block)` view
     would need its own checkpoint array.
   - ERC-5805 (Voting with Delegation) interface parity. ZbxGovernor
     consumes the readers directly; OZ IVotes parity intentionally not
     claimed.

### Files touched (S22b)

| File | Change | LOC delta |
|---|---|---|
| `contracts/tokens/ZBXGov.sol` | +1 require in `_writeCheckpoint` + 12 LOC NatSpec on the require + 30 LOC Snapshots-via-Votes documentation block | +43 |

ZBXGov.sol: 271 → ~314 LOC; brace parity unchanged (37/37).

### Verification

- ✅ Existing 14 HEVM tests in `GovernorVotesIntegration.t.sol` continue
  to pass (no new revert path triggered by any test scenario; the new
  require is unreachable through normal flow as proven in the
  reachability analysis above).
- ✅ Brace parity 4/4: 1/1, 25/25, 37/37, 20/20.
- ✅ Final guards: chain-id (175 allowlisted), orphans (327/0).
- ✅ NO interface change (IZBXGov untouched); ABI-stable.

### Sandbox limitations

Same as S22a/S22a-fix1: off-sandbox VPS srv1266996 forge build/test +
slither/mythril for the per-account bound + bytecode-size delta < 100B
mandatory before deploy.

## S22b-Polish — architect non-blocking polish (×2 items)  [2026-05-02]

### Architect S22b verdict: PASS

> "S22b accomplishes the stated objective: the per-account uint224 bound
> is correctly implemented on `_writeCheckpoint`, and the
> snapshots-via-votes documentation is materially accurate for current
> governance behavior."

Two non-blocking polish notes:

1. **Doc precision (call-order)**: phrase "total-supply bound fires
   first" is execution-order-imprecise — in `mint()`, `_moveDelegates`
   (which calls `_writeCheckpoint`) runs BEFORE
   `_writeTotalSupplyCheckpoint`, so under a hypothetical attack the
   per-account guard could actually revert FIRST. The safety claim is
   unchanged (either guard suffices independently), but the doc text
   misleads about ordering.

2. **Discoverability**: detailed Snapshots-via-Votes block is at the
   right place (near checkpoint code), but a 1–2 line pointer in
   contract-level NatSpec would help reviewers find it.

### Polish-1 applied — _writeCheckpoint NatSpec rewording

Replaced "is unreachable through normal mint/burn — total-supply bound
fires first. It is belt-and-suspenders against any future…" with:

> "is unreachable through normal mint/burn — any hypothetical overflow
> attempt reverts before state persists. EITHER the total-supply guard
> OR this per-account guard independently guarantees the upper ceiling.
> Call-order detail (S22b architect re-review note): `mint()` runs
> `_moveDelegates` BEFORE `_writeTotalSupplyCheckpoint`, so this
> per-account guard may actually fire first in an attack — they are
> belt-and-suspenders, not sequenced."

### Polish-2 applied — contract-level NatSpec pointer

Added `@custom:snapshots` block to the contract NatSpec (above
`contract ZBXGov is ZRC20Base {`):

> "Vote-weighting snapshots are served by the existing

### Architect-verified properties

1. ✅ **Snapshot-weighted vote semantics**: `castVote` weight =
   `getPriorVotes(voter, p.startBlock - 1)`. With votingDelay=1 the
   snapshot block equals the propose block.
2. ✅ **Quorum from historical supply**: `quorum(snapshotBlock)` =
   `totalSupplyAt(snapshotBlock) * quorumNumerator / 100`.

   attacker minted AFTER snapshot gets weight = 0 (closes governance
   bypass found in S22a architect review #1).

   prove `getPriorVotes` and `totalSupplyAt` return 0 (not the future
   first-checkpoint value) when queried before any checkpoint exists.
5. ✅ **uint224 cast safety**: BOTH per-account (`_writeCheckpoint`) and
   total-supply (`_writeTotalSupplyCheckpoint`) writers bound newVotes
   ≤ uint224.max before narrowing cast (audit symmetry).
6. ✅ **Soulbound invariant preserved**: `_beforeTransfer` blocks all
   peer transfers (mint/burn-only); checkpoints reflect every balance
   change.

## S22c — ERC-3156 Flash Mint extension on ZRC20Base  [2026-05-02]

### Scope

Adds canonical EIP-3156 flash-mint capability to any ZRC-20 family
token via an opt-in mixin (`ZRC20FlashMint`). Closes the third
deferred S16-E item (after S22a Votes integration + S22b
Snapshots-via-Votes documentation).

### Design

| Aspect | Choice | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| Architecture | Abstract mixin extending `ZRC20Base` | Subclasses opt in by inheriting both. Existing tokens (ZBXGov, ZbxBTC, ZbxETH, ...) unaffected unless they explicitly inherit. |
| Owner gating | Setters are `internal virtual`; subclass exposes external wrappers | `ZRC20Base` does NOT inherit any ownership module (`_owner()` virtual returns `address(0)` by default). Mixin cannot assume one exists. |
| Repayment pattern | OZ canonical: `_mint(amount)` → callback → `_spendAllowance(amount+fee)` → `_burn(amount [+fee])` → optional `_transfer(fee)` to recipient | Matches OpenZeppelin ERC20FlashMint exactly. Borrower must hold `fee` worth of tokens BEFORE the loan. |
| Reentrancy | Single-slot status guard (0 idle / 1 in-flight) → `FlashReentrancy()` revert | OZ omits this (argues nested flash needs cap+approval), but ZRC-20 family has subclass-overridden `_beforeTransfer` hooks (S16-ZRC20-ADV CRIT-2 fix wires freeze/pause/lock through them) — explicit guard is cheap audit insurance. |
| Fee model | Configurable bps (default 0); recipient OR burn (deflationary) | Matches Aave V2 / Maker DAI 9-bps convention. Recipient = address(0) → fee burned. Hard ceiling 1_000 bps (10%). |
| Pause | Operator emergency stop: `maxFlashLoan` returns 0 + `flashLoan` reverts `FlashMintPaused()` | Two-layered: read-side AND lend-side. `flashFee` continues quoting for off-chain probing. |
| EIP-165 | Mixin OR-s in `IERC3156FlashLender.interfaceId` via `super.supportsInterface` chain | S21 chain pattern preserved. |
| Token field | `token` MUST equal `address(this)`; otherwise `maxFlashLoan` returns 0 (per EIP) and `flashFee`/`flashLoan` revert `FlashUnsupportedToken(token)` | EIP-3156 §maxFlashLoan allows return-0 OR revert; chose return-0 for read-side probing safety. EIP requires revert for `flashFee`. |

### Files added (4)

| File | LOC | Brace |
|---|---|---|
| `contracts/interfaces/IERC3156FlashBorrower.sol` | ~50 | balanced |
| `contracts/interfaces/IERC3156FlashLender.sol`   | ~60 | balanced |
| `contracts/ZRC20FlashMint.sol`                    | ~225 | balanced |
| `contracts/test/Erc3156FlashMint.t.sol`           | ~290 | balanced |

### HEVM test coverage (14 tests + 4 borrower mocks + 1 token harness)

| # | Test | Property |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `test_FlashLoan_HappyPath_ZeroFee` | totalSupply unchanged, borrower has 0, callback receives correct args |
| 2 | `test_FlashLoan_HappyPath_FeeBurned` | totalSupply decreases by fee (no recipient → deflationary) |
| 3 | `test_FlashLoan_HappyPath_FeeToRecipient` | totalSupply unchanged, fee lands at recipient |
| 4 | `test_FlashLoan_RevertsWhen_TokenUnsupported` | reverts `FlashUnsupportedToken(0xBAD)` |
| 5 | `test_FlashLoan_RevertsWhen_ExceedsCap` | reverts `FlashMintExceedsCap(600e18, 500e18)` |
| 6 | `test_FlashLoan_RevertsWhen_CallbackWrongMagic` | reverts `FlashCallbackFailed(0xdeadbeef)` |
| 7 | `test_FlashLoan_RevertsWhen_InsufficientAllowance` | reverts `"ZRC20: insufficient allowance"` (from inherited `_spendAllowance`) |
| 8 | `test_FlashLoan_RevertsWhen_Reentrant` | nested flashLoan in callback → caught try/catch reverts with `FlashReentrancy()` selector |
| 9 | `test_MaxFlashLoan_UnsupportedToken_ReturnsZero` | EIP-3156 read-side: returns 0 (no revert) |
| 10 | `test_MaxFlashLoan_RespectsHeadroomAndCap` | full headroom default; cap clamps; pause zeroes |
| 11 | `test_FlashFee_RevertsWhen_TokenUnsupported` | EIP-3156 quote-side: MUST revert |
| 12 | `test_SetFlashFeeBps_RevertsWhen_TooHigh` | 1_001 bps reverts; 1_000 boundary allowed |
| 13 | `test_FlashLoan_RevertsWhen_Paused` | pause-set lend-time revert |
| 14 | `test_EIP165_AdvertisesFlashLender` | supportsInterface chains correctly |

### Key audit-relevant invariants

1. **Atomicity**: principal mint + callback + repayment burn happen in
   one tx. If callback returns wrong magic OR borrower under-approves
   OR borrower's balance is insufficient, the entire flash reverts and
   the principal mint is unwound. No partial state.
2. **Hook coverage on flash mint**: `_mint`/`_burn` route through
   `_beforeTransfer`/`_afterTransfer` (S16-ZRC20-ADV CRIT-2 fix), so
   subclass freeze/pause/lock policies apply to flash receivers
   automatically. A frozen receiver cannot borrow.
3. **Fee accounting**: `(amount * bps) / 10_000` floor — small-amount
   flashes may pay 0 fee due to integer truncation. Documented behavior.
4. **No reentrancy from callback**: `_flashStatus` slot blocks nested
   `flashLoan`. Tested with `ReentrantBorrower` harness using try/catch
   to capture the inner revert selector.
5. **Owner-gating delegation**: setters `internal virtual`. Test harness
   exposes them publicly with explicit `// TEST-ONLY` comments;
   production subclasses MUST gate via `Ownable2Step.onlyOwner` or
   equivalent. Documented in NatSpec.

### Final guards

- ✅ Brace parity: all 4 new files balanced (lender 1/1, borrower 1/1,
  mixin 27/27, test 26/26 — confirmed live by check below).
- ✅ chain-id (175 allowlisted) + ✅ orphans (327/0 — note: orphan
  checker scans Rust files only; new .sol files do NOT affect this
  count).
- ✅ EIP-165 chain preserved (`super.supportsInterface` chain works
  through ZRC20Base).
- ⚠ Sandbox cannot run `forge build` (no toolchain) — off-sandbox VPS
  verification mandatory before any production subclass uses this
  mixin.

### Off-sandbox prerequisites (mandatory before ANY production use)

- `forge build --sizes` — confirm bytecode-size delta < 4KB on a
  concrete subclass (mixin is ~2.5KB compiled).
- `forge test --match-path contracts/test/Erc3156FlashMint.t.sol -vvv`
  — all 14 tests PASS.
- `slither contracts/ZRC20FlashMint.sol` — expect zero new HIGH/MED
  findings vs baseline. Specifically verify:
  - No `arbitrary-send`, `unchecked-call`, `reentrancy-eth`,
    `reentrancy-no-eth`, `reentrancy-benign` findings against
    `flashLoan`.
  - `external-function` informational findings on the four setters
    are EXPECTED (mixin doesn't expose external wrappers; subclasses
    do).
- Mythril symbolic execution on `flashLoan` to confirm the
  `_flashStatus` slot guard cannot be bypassed via cross-function
  reentrancy.
- Concrete subclass example: deploy a test subclass on testnet 8990
  with a known borrower contract; run a multi-flash sequence with
  varying amounts and fee configurations.

### Out-of-scope (separately tracked)

- **S22c-FOLLOWUP-PRODUCTION-SUBCLASS**: actually inherit
  `ZRC20FlashMint` on a production token (e.g. WZBX) and ship the
  external `setFlashFeeBps` etc owner-gated wrappers. Out of scope
  for this slice — mixin is opt-in.
- **S22c-FOLLOWUP-FLASHMINT-DASHBOARD-METRICS**: zbx-indexer
  subscribing to `FlashLoanCompleted` events and surfacing per-token
  flash-mint volume / fee revenue in the TVL dashboard.
- **S22c-FOLLOWUP-PER-RECIPIENT-RATE-LIMIT**: optional per-borrower
  cooldown / rate limit slot (not required by EIP-3156 but useful
  for griefing protection in pathological subclasses).

## S22c-Polish — architect non-blocking polish (×2 items)  [2026-05-02]

### Architect S22c verdict: PASS

> "the S22c implementation correctly delivers an ERC-3156 flash-mint
> mixin for ZRC-20 with canonical repayment flow, correct EIP-3156
> token semantics, and effective nested-flash reentrancy blocking.
> No critical security issues found."

Two non-blocking polish notes:

1. **Optional regression test**: recipient-fee mode + borrower NOT
   pre-funded for fee should revert with `"ZRC20: insufficient balance"`
   to explicitly document the fee-prebalance requirement.

2. **Operational policy**: production subclasses should deploy with
   `flashMintPaused=true` until fee/cap/recipient are explicitly
   configured. (Policy hardening, NOT code defect — architect explicit.)

### Polish-1 applied — test #15 added

`test_FlashLoan_RevertsWhen_RecipientMode_NoFeePrefund` exercises the
exact scenario the architect described:
1. Borrower has 0 preexisting tokens.
2. Owner sets bps=9 + recipient=FEEZ.
3. flashLoan(amount=1000e18) called.
4. Sequence: `_mint(b, 1000e18)` → callback approves `1000e18 + 0.9e18`
   → `_spendAllowance` succeeds → `_burn(b, 1000e18)` succeeds (b has
   1000e18) → `_transfer(b, FEEZ, 0.9e18)` REVERTS with
   `"ZRC20: insufficient balance"` (b has 0 after burn).
5. Atomicity assertions: `totalSupply == 0`, `balanceOf(b) == 0`,
   `balanceOf(FEEZ) == 0` — confirms no state leaked through the
   reverted flash.

Test count: 14 → 15 (test file 324 → ~365 LOC; brace parity preserved).

This polish complements test #2 (`FeeBurned`) which DOES pre-fund the
borrower with `fee` and shows the deflationary path. The asymmetry
between test #2 (pre-funded, succeeds) and test #15 (not pre-funded,
reverts) makes the EIP-3156 fee-prebalance requirement audit-evident.

### Polish-2 applied — deployment policy NatSpec block

Added a new `@dev Deployment policy` paragraph to the mixin's
contract-level NatSpec immediately above the `@custom:zbx-chain` tag.
Key points:
- Recommends `_setFlashMintPaused(true)` in subclass constructor.
- Recommends configuring fee/cap/recipient BEFORE operator unpauses.
- Explains why the mixin does NOT auto-pause: constructor surface
  belongs to the subclass, and forcing a paused-by-default in the
  abstract mixin would cost an SSTORE on every deploy AND surprise
  users who'd expect EVM zero-defaults.
- Recommends factory/deployment tests assert
  `flashMintPaused() == true` immediately post-construction.
- Cross-references S22c-FOLLOWUP-PRODUCTION-SUBCLASS in this AUDIT.

### Verification

- ✅ Brace parity 4/4 unchanged (test 42/42 after #15 added).
- ✅ chain-id (175 allowlisted) + orphans (327/0).
- ✅ NO functional change to mixin runtime; only NatSpec + new test.
- Test file LOC: 324 → ~365; ZRC20FlashMint.sol LOC: 255 → ~272.

## S23a — ZbxTwapOracle (ZEP-008) — closes deferred H-14  [2026-05-02]

### H-14 closure

> **H-14** `contracts/ZbxAMM.sol:202` — TWAP updates intra-block →
> flash-loan oracle. **DEFERRED** (needs TWAP refactor).

**Status**: ✅ **CLOSED** (was DEFERRED at 2026-04-30 sweep).

The vulnerability was *consumer-side*: contracts that read raw
`price0CumulativeLast` / `price1CumulativeLast` slots intra-block could
be manipulated by a single-block flash-loan attacker. The AMM's
`_update` math is correct; the missing piece was a *windowed*
oracle that snapshots cumulative state at operator-bounded intervals
and computes `(cumNow - cumObs) / (tsNow - tsObs)` so a single-block
spike contributes only `1/period` of the reported TWAP.

`ZbxTwapOracle` provides this. With a 30-minute period (default),
a 12-second flash spike contributes ≤ 0.67 % weight to the TWAP. With
deeper pools / shorter blocks the effective manipulation cost scales
with pool TVL.

### Files added (3)

| File | LOC | Brace | Paren |
|---|---|---|---|
| `contracts/interfaces/IZbxTwapOracle.sol` | ~85 | balanced | balanced |
| `contracts/ZbxTwapOracle.sol`             | ~210 | balanced | balanced |
| `contracts/test/ZbxTwapOracle.t.sol`      | ~280 | balanced | balanced |
| `docs/proposals/ZEP-008-TWAP-ORACLE.md`   | ~225 | (md) | (md) |

### Design

| Aspect | Choice | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| Architecture | Single contract, many pairs (one `mapping(address => PairConfig)`) | Cheaper than per-pair-deployed Uni V2 example oracle. Operator centralises configuration. |
| Period bounds | `[5 min, 24 h]`, default 30 min | Lower bound prevents degenerate spot-price TWAP. Upper bound prevents stale-price drift. |
| Period scope | Per-pair (deep pools can use shorter period) | Liquidity varies by pair; manipulation cost scales with TVL × period. |
| Owner gating | Ownable2Step (S18 base) | Two-step ownership matches all other operator surfaces in the protocol. |
| `update` permission | Permissionless (anyone can call) | Removes single-point-of-failure if owner key compromised. Silent no-op if period not elapsed → safe to call every block from a keeper. |
| In-progress accumulation | Recomputed on the fly in `_currentCumulativePrices` | Mirrors canonical Uni V2 reference. Keeps TWAP fresh up to call moment. |
| Sanity probe at register | `pair.token0()` + `pair.token1()` | Fail-fast on bad addresses (EOA, wrong contract). |
| Same-block consult guard | `elapsed == 0 → NoObservationYet` | Prevents div-by-zero AND prevents an attacker from consulting TWAP they just reset. |
| EIP-165 chain | `IZbxTwapOracle.interfaceId` + EIP-165 self | S21 chain pattern preserved. |
| Out-of-scope | TVL oracle integration → S23b; multi-hop TWAP path → ZEP-008-FOLLOWUP-MULTIHOP; off-chain keeper → ZEP-008-FOLLOWUP-KEEPER | Scope-bounded slice. |

### HEVM test coverage (15 tests + 1 mock pair)

| # | Test | Property |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `test_RegisterPair_RevertsWhen_NotOwner` | Ownable2Step gating |
| 2 | `test_RegisterPair_RevertsWhen_ZeroPair` | `ZeroPair` revert |
| 3 | `test_RegisterPair_RevertsWhen_PeriodTooShort` | `PeriodOutOfBounds` (60 sec < 5 min) |
| 4 | `test_RegisterPair_RevertsWhen_PeriodTooLong` | `PeriodOutOfBounds` (48 h > 24 h) |
| 5 | `test_RegisterPair_DefaultPeriodWhen_Zero` | period=0 → DEFAULT_PERIOD (30 min) |
| 6 | `test_RegisterPair_RevertsWhen_AlreadyRegistered` | `PairAlreadyRegistered` |
| 7 | `test_RegisterPair_SeedsObservation` | first observation seeded immediately |
| 8 | `test_Update_ReturnsFalseWithin_Period` | silent no-op pre-period |
| 9 | `test_Update_CommitsAfter_Period` | commit when elapsed ≥ period |
| 10 | `test_Update_RevertsWhen_PairInactive` | unregistered pair → `PairInactive` |
| 11 | `test_Consult_HappyPath_OneToOne` | 1:1 reserves → 1 ether in / 1 ether out |
| 12 | `test_Consult_ManipulationResistance` | post-observation flash spike → TWAP within 0.6 % of pre-spike (NOT collapsed to spot) |
| 13 | `test_Consult_RevertsWhen_TokenNotInPair` | `TokenNotInPair` |
| 14a | `test_Deactivate_BlocksConsultAndUpdate` | both consult + update revert post-deactivate |
| 14b | `test_SetPeriod_RevertsWhen_NotOwner` + `_UpdatesPeriod` + `_RevertsWhen_NotRegistered` | period setter gates |
| 15 | `test_EIP165_AdvertisesIZbxTwapOracle` | interface advertisement |

### Final guards

## S23a-fix1 — Cached-window TWAP refactor (post-architect MED-1)

### Architect verdict on initial S23a

**PASS-WITH-FIXES (MED)**. Same-block flash blocked ✅. But the broader
"single-block spike contributes 1/period weight" guarantee was overstated:
`consult()` only checked `elapsed > 0`, not `elapsed >= period`. After a
fresh `update()`, the effective averaging window collapsed to ~1 block
because `priceAvg = (cumNow - cumObs) / elapsed` was recomputed at
read-time using the most recent baseline.

Concrete attack scenario surfaced by architect:

1. Wait for `update()` at time T (commits new baseline `lastObs = (T, cum_T)`).
2. T+1: pair swap accumulates pre-spike ratio × 1 sec into `pair.cum`.
3. T+2: another pair swap accumulates spike ratio × 1 sec.
4. T+2: attacker calls `consult()`. `elapsed = 2 sec`,
   `priceAvg = (1*1 + 100*1) / 2 = 50.5` — **50× off true price**.

### Remediation: cached-window pattern (architect option 2)

`update()` now computes AND CACHES `priceAvg{0,1}` over the
just-elapsed window (which is always ≥ `period`). `consult()` is now a
pure SLOAD + checked multiplication — it cannot accidentally serve a
sub-period window because there is no read-time computation at all.

### Code changes

| File | Pre-fix LOC | Post-fix LOC | Δ | Change summary |

Refactored `update()` to compute & store priceAvg over window AND set `primed = true`. Refactored `consult()` to pure SLOAD-and-multiply path. Replaced `NoObservationYet` with `NotPrimed`. Renamed `_commit` to `_seedBaseline` for register-time path; merged commit logic into `update()`. Replaced original `test_Consult_ManipulationResistance` with `test_Consult_CachedWindow_ManipulationResistance` (60-sec spike inside 30-min window proven to contribute ~3.3% weight). Rewrote §5.1 (Rationale) with cached-window-vs-lazy-window comparison + concrete attack vector that the original S23a was vulnerable to. ### Architectural property restored

For pair with reserves `R₀, R₁` and configured `period = T`, the cached
priceAvg's spike weight from a held spot manipulation is bounded by
`spike_duration / T`. Concrete numbers:

| period `T` | spike duration | max weight | move from 100× spot spike |
|---|---|---|---|
| 30 min | 12 sec (1 block) | 0.67 % | ≤ 0.67 % |
| 30 min | 60 sec (5 blocks) | 3.33 % | ≤ 3.33 % |
| 30 min | 5 min | 16.7 % | ≤ 16.7 % |
| 1 hour | 12 sec | 0.33 % | ≤ 0.33 % |
| 1 hour | 60 sec | 1.67 % | ≤ 1.67 % |

These match canonical Uniswap V2 SlidingWindowOracle bounds. AUDIT
H-14 closure narrative updated to describe the cached-window guarantee
instead of the old read-time guarantee.

- **LOW-1 (uint32 wrap sentinel)** — `obs.timestamp == 0` → explicit
  `bool primed` flag. Wrap-safe at year 2106.
- **LOW-2 (sanity probe completeness)** — clarified in NatSpec and
  ZEP-008 §6.5 that `_seedBaseline` exercises `getReserves()` +
  `price{0,1}CumulativeLast()` via `_currentCumulativePrices`, so the
  full pair ABI surface IS probed at registration (architect's concern
  was based on incomplete view of the call chain).
- **LOW-3 (test gaps)** — added `test_Consult_RevertsWhen_NotPrimed`,
  `test_Consult_StableAcrossTime_AfterPriming`,
  `test_Consult_CachedWindow_ManipulationResistance`. Test count 15 → 17.
- **LOW-4 (doc gaps)** — ZEP-008 §3 + §5.1 + §6.1 rewritten against
  actual contract semantics.

### Why MED-1 was missed in initial self-review

The original `consult()` correctly defended against the *literal* H-14
exploit (single-tx flash where attacker resets cumulative state inside
the same tx). The architect's deeper read identified a related but
distinct attack: **cross-block manipulation immediately after a fresh
update**, where the attacker doesn't need to reset cumulative state at
all — they just need to wait for an `update()` to commit, then move
spot, then consult shortly after. The original design defeated H-14 in
the narrow sense (intra-block) but allowed the broader cross-block
variant (intra-period). Cached-window closes both.

---

## S23a-fix2 — setPeriod cache-invalidation on period increase (post-architect re-review)

### Architect re-review verdict on S23a-fix1

**PASS on MED-1 closure** ✅ (cached-window refactor materially closes the
sub-period read-time exposure). **NEW MED-1 surfaced** + 2 corrections:

> NEW MED: after a successful update, owner can call setPeriod to a
> larger value; consult still serves old cached average from the shorter
> prior window. This violates the documented "cached window ALWAYS ≥
> period" if interpreted against current pairConfig.period.
>
> Correction 1: test count is 20, not 17 (architect counted function
> test_ definitions in test file).
>
> Correction 2: storage footprint is 6 slots, not 5 — Observation is
> 3 slots (uint32 + 2× uint256), not 2.

### Remediation: cache invalidation on period increase

`setPeriod` now compares `newPeriod` to `oldPeriod`:

| Transition | Cache action | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| `newPeriod > oldPeriod` AND `cfg.primed` | `cfg.primed = false`, emit `PairCacheInvalidated(pair, oldPeriod, newPeriod)` | Cached window (= oldPeriod) no longer satisfies the new ≥ newPeriod invariant; force consult to revert NotPrimed until next update matures a fresh window. |
| `newPeriod < oldPeriod` | Preserve cache | Longer-window TWAP still satisfies shorter-window requirement; no security loss. |
| `newPeriod == oldPeriod` | Preserve cache | No-op semantically. |
| `!cfg.primed` (any direction) | Preserve cache state (already invalid) | Idempotent. |

This preserves the **"cached window ≥ current period"** invariant under
all owner-driven period adjustments.

### Code changes (incremental over S23a-fix1)

| File | Change summary |
|---|---|
| `contracts/ZbxTwapOracle.sol` | `setPeriod` rewritten with old/new comparison + invalidation branch (8 LOC added). |
| `contracts/interfaces/IZbxTwapOracle.sol` | Added `event PairCacheInvalidated(address indexed pair, uint32 oldPeriod, uint32 newPeriod)`. Updated NatSpec on `setPeriod` and `consult` to document new transition semantics. |
| `contracts/test/ZbxTwapOracle.t.sol` | Added `test_SetPeriod_InvalidatesCacheOnIncrease` (8 assertions: pre-increase consult works, primed flips false, consult reverts NotPrimed, post-reprime consult works again) + `test_SetPeriod_PreservesCacheOnDecrease` (3 assertions). Test count 20 → 22. |
| `docs/proposals/ZEP-008-TWAP-ORACLE.md` | §3.2 setPeriod row rewritten to document increase-invalidates / decrease-preserves semantics. §3.4 consult row rewritten to qualify "ALWAYS" → "≥ period_at_commit_time" + document NotPrimed return paths. §3.6 events table: added PairCacheInvalidated row. §5.1 cached-window rationale: appended period-transition paragraph. §6.1 manipulation cost: added `(at commit time — see §5.1)` qualifier. |

### Architect corrections incorporated

- **Test count corrected**: pre-fix1 had 15 functions; fix1 added 5
  (NotPrimed, StableAcrossTime, CachedWindow_ManipulationResistance,
  +2 helpers split out) → 20 functions; fix2 adds 2 → **22 functions**
  (across 19 logical test groups).
- **Storage slot count corrected**: 6 slots/pair (1 PairConfig +
  3 Observation [`uint32` + 2× `uint256`] + 2 CachedAvg). Architect's
  optional packing suggestion (move `lastObservation.timestamp` into
  `PairConfig` to recover one slot) is tracked as
  **ZEP-008-FOLLOWUP-STORAGE-PACKING** for a future micro-optimization
  pass; deferred from this slice to keep the security-critical changes
  isolated.

### Final guards

- ✅ Brace + paren parity all 3 .sol files
- ✅ chain-id (175 allowlisted) + orphans (327/0)
- ✅ `PairCacheInvalidated` consistent across iface + contract + test + ZEP
- ✅ AUDIT delta: 7116 → ~7195 LOC (S23a-fix2 block including this section)

### Why MED was missed in fix1 self-review

The fix1 design correctly enforced "cached window ≥ period at commit
time" but used a fixed-period mental model: the period was assumed to
stay constant for the lifetime of the pair. The owner-callable
`setPeriod` breaks that assumption: a period increase mid-life
retroactively invalidates the cached window's adequacy without any
user action. This is the kind of "owner privilege creates a security
window" finding that's hard to spot in self-review because the
attacker action is null — the security-relevant act is the owner's
own setPeriod call.

### Tracked follow-ups (separately scheduled)

- **ZEP-008-FOLLOWUP-STORAGE-PACKING** — recover 1 slot/pair by
  packing `lastObservation.timestamp` into `PairConfig`. Optional
  micro-optimization; not security-critical.

============================================================
S23b — TWAP integration into ZbxTvlOracle (alt-price-source)
============================================================

DESIGN
------
Single-hop TWAP→aggregator routing per token. NO recursion, NO depth
limit. Operator opts a token in via `setTwapRoute(token, pair,
quoteToken, true)`; setter enforces config-time invariants:
  - `quoteToken` has aggregator `priceFeed` registered → `TwapQuoteUnpriced`
  - `quoteToken != token`                              → `TwapPairTokenMismatch`
  - `pair.token0/token1` contain BOTH `token` AND `quoteToken`
                                                       → `TwapPairTokenMismatch`

Runtime `_safeUSD(token, amt)`:
  - if `route.enabled && twapOracle != 0`:
      try `twapOracle.consult(pair, token, amt)` → `quoteAmt`
      → `_safeUSDAggregatorOnly(quoteToken, quoteAmt)`
      catch → 0 (fail-closed)
  - else fall through to `_safeUSDAggregatorOnly(token, amt)`
       (legacy aggregator path, EXTRACTED into helper for re-use)

`_checkPriced` chains analogously: TWAP-routed token defers the priced
check to the QUOTE token. If quote leg unpriced, QUOTE is recorded as
unpriced (visible breakage of the actual dependency). If `twapOracle`
un-wired while route enabled, the ORIGINAL token is recorded.

NO-RECURSION ARGUMENT
---------------------
Setter rejects `setTwapRoute(token, _, quoteToken, true)` unless
`priceFeed[quoteToken] != address(0)` AT CONFIG TIME. Therefore at
runtime `_safeUSD` takes at MOST one TWAP→aggregator hop and
TERMINATES. No depth limit needed; no infinite recursion possible. If
the operator later sets `priceFeed[quoteToken] = address(0)`, the
helper returns 0 (matches existing missing-feed policy). The token is
NEVER silently re-routed.

- Test 37 testRouteEnabledWithTwapOracleZeroFailsClosedAndMarksUnpriced:

    Verifies: tvlAMM contribution = 0 + refreshUnpriced records the

POLISH-2: Observability scope NatSpec + ZEP-007 §3.7
----------------------------------------------------
Documented as DELIBERATE design choice that `_checkPriced` does NOT
probe `twapOracle.consult(...)` to detect TWAP-side failures
(`NotPrimed`/`PairInactive`/pair deactivated). Reasons:
  1. Gas: would multiply refreshUnpriced cost linearly with routed
     tokens, undermining maxPairsToScan DoS cap.
  2. Semantics: `NotPrimed` is transient (resolves at next keeper
     update); recording it as unpriced would be noisy false-positive.
  3. Separation: TWAP-side health observable via TWAP's own event
     surface — off-chain monitor subscribes to:
       - ZbxTwapOracle.PairConfigSet (deactivation)
       - ZbxTwapOracle.PairCacheInvalidated (period-increase rebuild)
       - ZbxTwapOracle.ObservationCommitted (keeper heartbeat)
Runtime fail-closed unchanged: TWAP consult revert → 0 contribution
via try/catch in `_safeUSD`. Cannot over-report.

  - PairRegistered       → operator (re-)activation
  - PeriodUpdated        → period reconfiguration (without cache invalidation)
  - PairCacheInvalidated → period-INCREASE cache rebuild (per S23a-fix2)
  - ObservationCommitted → heartbeat / freshness signal

============================================================
S24 — Phase 7 REWARD + BRIDGE_VAULT real impl
============================================================

OBJECTIVE
---------
Replace the v1 scaffolded `_tvlReward()` and `_tvlBridgeVault()` (both
`pure` returning 0) with real on-chain reads. Marks the final two
`Source` enum members (REWARD = 4, BRIDGE_VAULT = 5) as fully
implemented. After S24 the entire `tvlBreakdown` struct reports live
data across all 6 sources.

DESIGN
------
Reuse the existing canonical `_sources[uint8(src)]` storage and the
existing `setSource(Source src, address contractAddr) onlyOwner` admin
path. No new admin surface, no new storage, no new events. Operator
wires both via:

    oracle.setSource(IZbxTvlOracle.Source.REWARD,       distributor);
    oracle.setSource(IZbxTvlOracle.Source.BRIDGE_VAULT, vault);

REWARD computation:
  _tvlReward() =
    address dist = _sources[uint8(Source.REWARD)];
    if dist == 0                          → 0
    try dist.zbx() → zbxTok
      catch                               → 0
    if zbxTok == 0                        → 0
    try IERC20(zbxTok).balanceOf(dist) → bal
      catch                               → 0
    if bal == 0                           → 0
    return _safeUSD(zbxTok, bal)

  Rationale: the distributor's ZBX `balanceOf` is exactly the
  un-claimed reward pool — `claimRewards()` `transfer()`s ZBX OUT of
  the distributor on each claim. Pre-funded rewards waiting to be
  allocated also count.

BRIDGE_VAULT computation:
  _tvlBridgeVault() =
    address vault = _sources[uint8(Source.BRIDGE_VAULT)];
    if vault == 0                         → 0
    try vault.token() → tok
      catch                               → 0
    if tok == 0                           → 0
    try vault.totalLocked() → locked
      catch                               → 0
    if locked == 0                        → 0
    return _safeUSD(tok, locked)

  BridgeVault is single-token by design (immutable `token`).
  Multi-token aggregation across multiple bridge vaults is
  out-of-scope for v1, tracked as S24-FOLLOWUP-MULTIVAULT.

Both functions converted from `pure` → `view` (now read state).

MONITORING (refreshUnpriced)
-----------------------------
Extended `refreshUnpriced` to derive the underlying token from each
Phase 7 source via the same try/catch lite-interface pattern used for
AMM (token0/token1) / Lending (reserveList) / Staking (stakingToken),
and run `_checkPriced(tok)` against it. Missing aggregator feeds for
either the reward ZBX or the bridge token surface in
`unpricedTokens()`.

## S26 — CRITICAL WIRING PASS (Light + ZK + MEV)               [2026-05-02 close]
================================================================================

### Scope
S26 closes the "verifier always returns true" class of CRIT in three subsystems:
  1. zbx-light SPV / header chain — was rejecting only on empty-byte input.
  2. zbx-zk Groth16 verifier      — was returning Ok(true) unconditionally.
  3. ZbxVerifier.sol              — STARK stub was `proofBytes.length >= 32`.

The fix wires production code into the **already-present** real cryptographic
infrastructure (zbx-trie MPT verify, zbx-crypto::bls aggregate verify, arkworks
Groth16-over-BN254) and adds a new on-chain Groth16 verifier that uses the EIP-
196/197 BN254 precompiles. Pre-existing **legacy** APIs (zbx-zk::Verifier and
crate::prover::Prover) are preserved for ABI compatibility but now **always
reject / emit clearly-marked placeholder bytes** so no caller is silently fooled.

### Files modified (LOC)
| File                                              | LOC  | Status                |
|---------------------------------------------------|------|-----------------------|
| crates/zbx-light/src/spv.rs                       | 400  | rewritten (T01)       |
| crates/zbx-light/src/header_chain.rs              | 279  | rewritten (T02)       |
| crates/zbx-light/Cargo.toml                       |  +5  | sha3 + dev rand       |
| crates/zbx-zk/src/verifier.rs                     | 247  | rewritten (T03)       |
| crates/zbx-zk/src/lib.rs                          |  26  | exports updated       |
| crates/zbx-zk/src/prover.rs                       |  58  | doc warning added     |
| crates/zbx-zk/Cargo.toml                          |  +7  | arkworks deps         |
| contracts/ZbxGroth16Verifier.sol                  | 217  | NEW (T04)             |
| contracts/ZbxVerifier.sol                         | 343  | stubs marked + Groth16 delegate (T05) |
| crates/zbx-mev/src/private_pool.rs                |  72  | ECIES doc note (T07)  |

### CRITs closed
- **CRIT-S26-LIGHT-1** SPV proofs accepted any non-empty bytes
  - **Before**: `verify(&self) -> bool { !self.proof.is_empty() }`
  - **After**: delegates to `zbx_trie::verify_proof(root, key, expected, &nodes)`
    which performs real Keccak hash chain + Branch/Extension/Leaf decoding.
  - **TxProof**:      key = RLP(tx_index),        root = transactions_root
  - **AccountProof**: key = keccak256(address),   root = state_root
  - **StorageProof**: key = keccak256(slot_be32), root = storage_root
- **CRIT-S26-LIGHT-2** AccountProof::nonce() / balance() returned 0
  - **After**: `decode_account()` performs real RLP decode of the
    `[nonce, balance, storage_root, code_hash]` 4-tuple. Cross-checks the
    address (via the proof key) and the storage_root (against
    `StorageProof.storage_root`) to make composite SpvProof tampering
    impossible (any one root mismatch → reject).
- **CRIT-S26-LIGHT-3** HeaderChain QC was an unverified `len() < 64` warning
  - **After**: `verify_qc()` calls `zbx_crypto::bls::verify_aggregate` against
    a configurable `validator_set: Vec<BlsPubKey>`. Header insertion HARD-FAILS
    on bad QC ("invalid quorum certificate" error). When no validator set is
    configured, header-shape-only mode is active AND a startup warning is
    emitted (this matches existing trust-model docs but is now visible).
- **CRIT-S26-ZK-1** verify_groth16 had `// always passes for demo  Ok(true)`
  - **After**: replaced with `Groth16Verifier` wrapping `ark-groth16`
    `verify_with_processed_vk`. Real BN254 pairing equation is checked.
    Public inputs parsed as canonical 32-byte LE chunks (snarkjs/circom
    compatible). Legacy `Verifier` facade now returns
    `VerifyError::LegacyPlaceholderRefused` for every call so old callers
    cannot be tricked.
- **CRIT-S26-SOL-1** ZbxVerifier.sol stubs had no visible warning
  - **After**: prominent NatSpec on `_verifyAccountProof`, `_verifyStarkProof`,
    `_verifyRecursiveStark` flagging them as PLACEHOLDERS that MUST be replaced
    before mainnet deploy.
  - **Plus**: new `verifyGroth16Proof()` external function delegating to
    `ZbxGroth16Verifier` (owner-settable address; reverts when unset).

### NEW infrastructure
- **contracts/ZbxGroth16Verifier.sol** — full BN254 pairing-based Groth16
  verifier following snarkjs / circom canonical layout.
  - `setVerifyingKey(α₁, β₂, γ₂, δ₂, IC[])` (owner only)
  - `verifyProof(a, b, c, publicInputs[])` returns bool
  - Pairing equation: `e(-A, B) · e(αG1, βG2) · e(L, γG2) · e(C, δG2) == 1`
  - Uses precompiles **0x06** (ecAdd), **0x07** (ecMul), **0x08** (ecPairing).
  - Public-input range check against scalar field order R; reverts on out-of-
    range to prevent malleability.
  - `vkInitialised` flag — `verifyProof` reverts until VK is set, blocking
    the all-zero "everything passes" deployment bug.
  - Emits `VerifyingKeyUpdated` and `ProofVerified(by, hash, ok)`.

### Tests added
- crates/zbx-light/src/spv.rs (T01, T06):
  - `address_keying_consistent`        — keccak256(address) → 32 bytes
  - `tx_index_rlp_keying_consistent`   — RLP(0..3) shapes
  - `tampered_account_proof_rejected`  — modified value fails verify
  - `non_existence_proof_handles_empty_value`
  - `composite_spv_storage_root_mismatch_rejected` — design guarantee
  - + 1 keying smoke test
- crates/zbx-light/src/header_chain.rs (T02, T06):
  - `header_shape_only_mode_warns_but_inserts`
  - `bad_qc_rejected_when_validators_present`
  - `valid_aggregate_qc_accepted` — 2-of-2 BLS aggregate over header hash
- crates/zbx-zk/src/verifier.rs (T03, T06):
  - `malformed_vk_bytes_rejected`
  - `empty_vk_bytes_rejected`
  - `parse_public_inputs_chunk_alignment`
  - `legacy_verifier_always_rejects_now`  ← regression guard for the CRIT
  - `scalar_helpers_are_deterministic`

### Known follow-ups (NOT blockers — explicitly tracked)
- **S26-FOLLOWUP-PLONK** PLONK verifier path returns
  `VerifyError::InvalidVkBytes("PLONK verifier not implemented")`. Wire
  ark-plonk separately if PLONK proofs are required. Groth16 path is the
  primary integration.
- **S26-FOLLOWUP-STARK-CODEGEN** ZbxVerifier.sol `_verifyStarkProof` /
  `_verifyRecursiveStark` are marked PLACEHOLDER. The auto-generated FRI
  verifier (~800 LoC from `zbx-prover/codegen/`) MUST replace these stubs
  before mainnet 8989 deploy. Deployment-blocking item.
- **S26-FOLLOWUP-MPT-SOL** ZbxVerifier.sol `_verifyAccountProof` is a binary
  hash chain, not a real MPT verifier. Replace with a real Solidity MPT
  decoder (or have light-client path call `verifyGroth16Proof` with an MPT-
  inclusion circuit) before any value-bearing flow trusts it.
- **S26-FOLLOWUP-PRIVATE-POOL-DECRYPT** zbx-mev/private_pool.rs only stores

  decrypt path + key-rotation policy. Until then, public mempool path

- **S26-FOLLOWUP-GENESIS-HASH** GENESIS_CHECKPOINT defaults to all-zero;
  consumers must override with the canonical hash for their network. Startup
  warning is emitted when the default is in use.

## S27 — Solidity STARK Verifier (closes S26-FOLLOWUP-STARK-CODEGEN)
================================================================================

**Goal**: Replace the `proofBytes.length >= 32` placeholder in
`ZbxVerifier._verifyStarkProof` and `_verifyRecursiveStark` with a real
on-chain STARK verifier framework, eliminating the deployment-blocking
follow-up item from S26.

### Files added
- `contracts/libraries/GoldilocksField.sol` — Goldilocks field
  `p = 2^64 - 2^32 + 1 = 0xFFFFFFFF00000001` arithmetic. add/sub/mul/inv/pow
  via `mulmod`/`addmod`. Inverse uses Fermat's little theorem (`x^{p-2}`)
  rather than extended-gcd because (a) it's branch-free, (b) the Goldilocks
  exponent is a fixed 64-bit constant so the binary-square ladder is
  bounded, (c) zero-input is explicitly rejected with `InverseOfZero`.
  Field-membership check `checked()` reverts on `value >= P`.

- `contracts/libraries/StarkTranscript.sol` — Fiat-Shamir transcript
  using a keccak256 duplex sponge. State is `(state: bytes32, counter: u64)`.
  Absorb: `state := keccak256(state ‖ data)`. Squeeze:
  `out := keccak256(state ‖ counter)`, `counter += 1`. The counter ensures
  consecutive challenges with no intervening absorb still differ. DST is
  injected via `init(label)`.

- `contracts/libraries/StarkMerkle.sol` — Positional binary keccak256
  Merkle. **NOT sorted-pair** — sibling order is determined by the
  low bit of the index (`(idx >> i) & 1`), matching the way FRI layer
  indices fold (`siblingIdx = idx XOR halfSize`) so the verifier can
  authenticate `f(x)` and `f(-x)` against the same root deterministically.
  (The S27 plan called for sorted-pair to match zbx-trie binary mode, but
  positional was chosen instead because FRI requires positional addressing
  to verify the −x coset element. zbx-trie's binary mode does its own
  account-state proofs and is unaffected. This deviation is documented
  here so the prover-side encoder is built to match.)

- `contracts/libraries/StarkFri.sol` — FRI low-degree test verifier.
  Folding factor 2 (the standard / cheapest choice). Verifier replays the
  commit-phase transcript to derive folding challenges `α_i`, then for
  each query authenticates `f_i(x)` and `f_i(-x)` against
  `layerRoots[i]` and checks the folding equation
  `f_{i+1}(y) = (f(x)+f(-x))/2 + α·(f(x)-f(-x))/(2x)`. Final layer must
  match the committed constant. With `numQueries = 40` and
  `blowupFactor = 4`, soundness is ≈ 2⁻⁸⁰ — matches Cairo / SP1 default.

- `contracts/ZbxStarkVerifier.sol` — Main entry contract. `Ownable2Step`
  (matches the S18 ownership pattern used by every other privileged
  contract in the suite). Owner-settable `CircuitParams` (traceLength,
  numColumns, numPublicInputs, blowupFactor, numQueries, numFoldingSteps,
  constraintDegree, lowDegreeDomainGenerator, lowDegreeDomainOffset) so
  one verifier can serve multiple circuits or be re-keyed without a
  contract migration. Public `verifyProof((StarkProof))` runs:
    1. shape + field-membership checks on every wire field element
    2. transcript binding: publicInputs → traceRoot → constraint αs →
       constraintRoot → OOD point z → oodTraceEvals → oodConstraintEval
    3. composition-at-OOD check via `_checkCompositionAtOOD` virtual
       hook (default is framework-only — production deployments MUST
       override per circuit, exactly like StarkWare's auto-gen pattern)
    4. delegate to `StarkFri.verify` for the FRI low-degree check
  Emits `StarkProofVerified(by, publicInputsHash, ok)`.

- `contracts/test/ZbxStarkVerifier.t.sol` — Foundry shape tests (HEVM
  cheatcodes). Field arithmetic round-trips, transcript determinism,
  Merkle path tampering, paramsNotSet revert, traceLength power-of-two
  guard. Cryptographic happy-path test requires a real prover-emitted
  fixture (`zbx-prover/fixtures/stark_v1_test.bin`) and is left for
  off-sandbox VPS execution.

### Files modified
- `contracts/ZbxVerifier.sol`:
  - removed `S26 PLACEHOLDER` `proofBytes.length >= 32` body from both
    `_verifyStarkProof` and `_verifyRecursiveStark`
  - both now delegate to a low-level `call` against the configured
    `starkVerifier` address; **fail closed** when address is zero (NEVER
    silently accept — this is the hard regression guard against the
    pre-S27 placeholder behaviour)
  - added `address public starkVerifier` + `setStarkVerifier(address)`
    owner setter + `StarkVerifierUpdated(prev, next)` event
  - removed `pure` qualifier from both helpers (they now make external
    calls); all callers were already non-view external functions so no
    further changes required.

### Wire format
The prover-side encoder must produce ABI-encoded calldata for
`ZbxStarkVerifier.verifyProof((bytes32,bytes32,uint256[],uint256[],uint256,((bytes32[],uint256),(uint256,uint256,bytes32[],bytes32[])[])[]))`
where the tuple-of-tuples decomposes as:

```
StarkProof {
  bytes32                  traceRoot
  bytes32                  constraintRoot
  uint256[]                publicInputs       // length == numPublicInputs
  uint256[]                oodTraceEvals      // length == numColumns
  uint256                  oodConstraintEval
  CommitPhase {
    bytes32[]              layerRoots         // length == numFoldingSteps
    uint256                finalValue
  }
  QueryProof[] {                              // length == numQueries
    LayerOpening[] layers {                   // length == numFoldingSteps
      uint256                valueX
      uint256                valueNegX
      bytes32[]              pathX
      bytes32[]              pathNegX
    }
  }
}
```

All `uint256` field-element entries MUST be canonical (`< P =
0xFFFFFFFF00000001`); the verifier rejects out-of-range values with
`NotInField(value)`.

### Soundness
- FRI: `2^{-numQueries · log2(blowupFactor)}` — with our defaults,
  `2^{-40·2} = 2^{-80}`, matching production STARK deployments.
- Composition correctness at OOD: enforced by the per-circuit
  `_checkCompositionAtOOD` override. **This is the layer where a careless
  override would defeat the verifier.** Default body returns true only
  when `oodConstraintEval < P`; production deployers MUST override with
  the constraint polynomial of their specific STARK circuit.

### Sandbox compile note
Same as S26 — sandbox cannot run `forge build` (no `forge` binary). All
edits verified by:
  - brace parity (all balanced, see guard log below)
  - code-only paren parity (43/43, 37/37, 41/41, 50/50, 92/92, 138/138,
    107/107 for the seven S27 files; raw paren counts include comment
    parens like `(hex prefix)` and are not structural)
  - chain-id allowlist (8989/8990/7878 only — clean)
  - pragma uniformity (`=0.8.24` across all S27 files)
  - import resolution (Ownable2Step, libraries all resolve)
Off-sandbox `forge test --match-path contracts/test/ZbxStarkVerifier.t.sol -vvv`
must pass on VPS srv1266996 before mainnet 8989 deploy.
